# Milestone 1 — the decision register

Status: **decisions 1–26 settled 2026-09-08** by the project owner, and the
queue in [`milestone-1-tasks.md`](milestone-1-tasks.md) is built on those and
would have to be rebuilt if any of them changed. **27 onwards were taken
later** -- 27–31 on 2026-09-11, 32–41 with M1-04, 42–52 with M1-06, 53–71
with M1-05, 72–84 with M1-07, three of which made M1-86, and 85–91 with
M1-08, 92-98 with M1-09, 99-105 with M1-10, 106-114 with M1-87, 115-122 with
M1-11, 123-141 with M1-12, 142-156 with M1-13, 157-172 with M1-14 and
173-185 with M1-15, 186 after it and 187-206 with M1-16 -- and section 9 holds them. *(The list stopped at M1-12
until 2026-09-25, two tasks after it stopped being the whole list -- the same
defect as the note below, found the same way.)* *(This said "27–31 were taken later" until 2026-09-19, three tasks
after it stopped being the whole list.)*

*(This was titled "the decisions taken before it started" and dated
2026-09-08 flat, until 2026-09-13 — a header that had stopped describing the
body once section 9 existed. Numbering runs on rather than restarting, so a
reference to "decision 30" stays unambiguous.)*

Nineteen of the twenty-six span files and are carried by **eight** architecture
decision records — that was task
[M1-02](tasks/m1-02-record-the-decisions.md), the second thing done in this
milestone. (This said "eight of them span files", which counted the records and
called them decisions.) This file is the register: it holds all of them,
including the ones too small or too local for an ADR, so that no decision lives
only in a conversation. **Section 8 maps every decision to where it is
recorded.**

**The rule they were taken under** is working agreement 1 in
[`../../CLAUDE.md`](../../CLAUDE.md): state the finding, propose the fix, and
wait. Every row below was put to the owner as a question with its alternatives
and their costs, and every row is the owner's answer.

**What belongs here, and what does not** — settled 2026-09-13, because the
register had fallen two days behind and it was not clear whether that was a
backlog or a boundary. It is a boundary:

- **The register** holds a ruling that changes the **plan, the queue, or an
  error budget** — anything the task queue is built on, which is what the
  status line above promises. Decisions 27–31 all qualify: ERFA rewrites tasks,
  M1-06-before-M1-05 reorders the queue, the Sun's span tightens a budget.
- **An ADR** holds a decision that spans files, whether or not it touches the
  plan. [`../adr/README.md`](../adr/README.md) is the list.
- **[`../HISTORY.md`](../HISTORY.md)** holds everything else, with the
  measurement that produced it. The nine rulings of 2026-09-11 to 09-13 — the
  guarded `length()`, the energy-based conic classifier, `a(1 + e)` for the
  apoapsis, the radius-and-speed hybrid, adopting double-double at 2.66x,
  moving the circular threshold, fuzzing on Windows, `-Wabi-tag` at the site,
  `stateFromElements` reporting — are there, and two of them earned ADRs 0017
  and 0018. **None of them belongs here**, because none changes a task.

That boundary costs something and the cost is named: "where is every ruling
written down" now has three answers rather than one. The alternative was a
register holding every choice of formulation, which duplicates `HISTORY.md` —
one fact in two places, which is the failure
[`../STATUS.md`](../STATUS.md) exists to prevent.

## 1. Verification

| # | Decision | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | How renderer work is machine-verified | **Probes plus golden images.** A headless `--probe` mode renders one deterministic frame; numbers are asserted against a reference computed independently on the CPU in the test, and images against a committed golden |
| 2 | Who looks at the frames | **The owner, before the next task starts.** Every probe writes a viewable PNG on every run, pass or fail, plus a diff image on a golden mismatch. A golden image is *an approved frame*: it enters the repository only after the owner has seen it, and updating one is a decision, never a way to make a test pass |
| 3 | Frame and golden geometry | Probes render **1280x720** and write that PNG. The golden is the same frame downsampled to **640x360**, about 150 KB. Two tolerances, both stated: **no pixel further than 4/255**, and **mean absolute error under 0.5/255**. Goldens only where the image is the artefact; LUT, geometry and radiometry probes stay numeric |
| 4 | External truth for the J2 integrator | **NASA GMAT fixtures.** Horizons cannot propagate an Earth satellite under a J2-only force model, so the milestone plan's original wording was not achievable as written. Horizons keeps Sun, Moon and Earth positions, and the time scales |
| 5 | Frame time | **Measured by a benchmark, not asserted in `check`.** A timing threshold on shared hardware fails for reasons unrelated to the change. Numbers are recorded in `PROJECT_STATE.md` at the end of phases D, C and G |
| 6 | Test framework | **Catch2 v3**, and both existing suites move to it in the first task, so there is never more than one harness in the tree |

## 2. Physics

| # | Decision | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 7 | Integrator | **Cowell first, then Encke.** Fixed-step Cowell with RK4 — so convergence order can be measured against theory — then a high-order tableau, then Encke reusing the two-body propagator as its reference conic. Both propagators are kept and diffed against each other |
| 8 | The seams | **Three, not one.** Force terms, the stepper, and where body positions come from. Adding the Moon later is adding a *term* and an *ephemeris*, not changing the integrator |
| 9 | How the seams are expressed | **Closed sets as `std::variant`.** No inheritance, no heap, no vtable; values stay trivially copyable, so a physics snapshot is a copy. An alternative the compiler has not seen handled is a compile error |
| 10 | Stepper interface | **Stateful, with explicit copyable state** — `reset(state, epoch)` then `advance()`. The only shape that admits a multistep method (Gauss-Jackson) or an adaptive controller later |
| 11 | Force-term traits | **Each term declares whether it depends on time, position or velocity.** A future symplectic or Nyström integrator can then refuse by name rather than integrate something wrong but plausible |
| 12 | Integrated state size | **Six now, steppers generic over a state concept.** Thrust with variable mass, and 6-DOF attitude, join later as channels without rewriting an integrator |
| 13 | Time acceleration | **Fixed step, more steps.** 1x and 10000x are bit-identical. If a machine cannot keep up, the simulation clock lags real time and says so; the model never changes |
| 14 | Time and frames in scope | **Full**: UTC, TAI, TT, TDB and UT1 as distinct types on a two-part Julian date; a leap-second table that reports rather than extrapolates; IAU 2006 precession and the Earth rotation angle; an analytic solar position. Nutation and ΔUT1 deferred **with their errors written down**. *Storage amended 2026-09-10 with M1-03: a Modified Julian Day beginning at midnight, and integer picoseconds within it — ADR 0009's update. Amended again 2026-09-11 (decisions 27–29, ADR 0016): ERFA computes the astronomy, nutation is modelled after all, and the precession is CIO-based with ERA — the pairing written here was measured 0.342° out* |
| 15 | Where the astronomy lives | **A new `src/astro/`**, in the same `orbsim_core` target. `src/orbit/` stays about trajectories; `astro/` takes where bodies are and how frames rotate |

## 3. Renderer

| # | Decision | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 16 | Radiometric chain | **Manual photographic exposure (aperture, shutter, ISO) and the AgX tonemap.** Auto-exposure deferred, and when it lands it must be pinned in probe mode |
| 17 | Where render-side CPU maths lives | **A new Vulkan-free library, `orbsim_view`**: camera, projection, `RenderQuality`, tile identity, screen-space error, and the atmosphere CPU reference. The rule that nothing under `tests/` includes a Vulkan or SDL header stays enforced by the link graph rather than by discipline |
| 18 | Quadtree LOD | **Skirts plus vertex morphing.** Skirts close cracks without neighbour bookkeeping; morphing removes the pop |
| 19 | Count-like units | **An integral `Count<Derived>` beside `Quantity<Derived>`** in `core/Scalar.hpp`. `Texels` and `Mebibytes` are counts; `Pixels` stays on the f64 base, because a screen-space error threshold of 2.5 px is a real quantity. This closes the question ADR 0007 left open. *(The **reason** stands and the **mechanism** does not, since 2026-09-17: [ADR 0019](../adr/0019-vectors-carry-their-unit.md) moved every type in `core/Units.hpp` onto mp-units, and `Quantity<Derived>` now carries only `Tolerance`, which takes no part in dimensional analysis. A `Pixels` written on the f64 base would be the one dimensional unit in that header outside the dimension system, so it is a `Scalar<>` like the rest. [M1-12](tasks/m1-12-render-quality.md) is corrected to match. Noted 2026-09-21.)* |

## 5. The error budgets

Stated before the code, per [`../VERIFICATION.md`](../VERIFICATION.md) rule 4.
Each appears again in its own task document, in the test that asserts it, and in
the commit message. Changing one is a decision with a written reason.

Where a budget is split, **code error** is what a test asserts about our own
arithmetic, and **model error** is what we knowingly do not model — recorded so
it is never mistaken for a bug.

| Phase | Claim | Budget | Checked against |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | UTC/TAI/TT round trip | exact to **1e-9 s** — UTC ↔ TAI wherever the table is valid, TAI ↔ TT over all of 1972–2035 (decision 38). Measured 2026-09-18: **bit-identical**, an error of zero, because every step is whole seconds and whole picoseconds | The leap-second table and published ΔAT steps |
| A | TDB − TT | within **20 µs** — 0.6 m of Earth's orbital motion. Was 100 µs until 2026-09-19 (decision 54) | USNO Circular 179 eq. 2.6, as Skyfield 1.55 evaluates it (decision 53), itself within 9.3 µs of the full series |
| A | TT ↔ TDB round trip | **≤ 1 ps**: one rounding to the nearest picosecond each way (decision 57). Was 1e-9 s | Derived, not measured against anything. And exact on at least 99% of a seeded sweep, asserted as a rate (decision 68): 1,000,000 of 1,000,000 measured exact |
| A | Precession, nutation + ERA, as implemented | **0.1 mas** (code), over 1900–2100. Was 0.1″ until 2026-09-19 (decision 76) | Skyfield 1.55, whose rotation is Greenwich apparent sidereal time applied to the equinox-based matrix -- an independent formulation of the same IAU models, not ERFA, which computes it (ADR 0016, decision 75). Measured 54 µas worst, 47 of it the TIO locator s′ |
| A | Precession, nutation + ERA, as modelled | **≤ 14.1″, about 440 m** on the ground where ΔT comes from the leap-second table: ΔUT1 = 0 (≤ 13.5″) and polar motion omitted (≤ 0.6″). **Past the table, plus the drift of a held ΔT**: at worst 17.3″ a year on record, 8.1″ a year since 2000 (decision 72) (model) | Recorded, not asserted |
| A | UT1 from TT (M1-86) | **exact**: both round trips the identity, and UT1 from the table's ΔT the UTC road's UT1 to the picosecond (decision 72) | The definitions, and the suite's own transcription of the published steps |
| A | Solar direction and distance | **0.02″**, **5e-8 AU**, over the fixture's span 2000–2050. Was 0.1″ and 1e-6 AU until 2026-09-20 (decision 85) | JPL Horizons fixtures, geometric. Measured worst 0.0085″ and 2.14e-8 AU |
| A | Radiometric chain | **0.5 %** of the analytic 130 W·m⁻²·sr⁻¹ for a Lambertian patch, albedo 0.3, normal to the Sun at 1 AU | Analytic value, read back from the HDR target before tonemapping. RGBA16F quantisation is 0.05 % |
| A | Camera-relative precision | a fixed world point at Earth radius moves **≤ 0.05 px** at 1920×1080 between consecutive frames | Computed on the CPU with the same maths |
| B | BC7 compression | **≥ 40 dB PSNR** against the source | The uncompressed source image |
| B | Pyramid generation | **byte-identical** for identical input | A second run |
| D | Transmittance, single scattering | **1 %** | A CPU brute-force reference written independently in the test |
| D | Multiple scattering | **5 %** | The same reference |
| C | Rendered radius vs the dataset | **1 m** | A bilinear sample of ETOPO 2022 |
| C | Geometric screen-space error | **≤** the configured threshold | The projected true surface against the tessellated one, on the CPU |
| E | J2 off, against the two-body propagator | **1e-9 relative** over one orbit | `propagate()`, which shares no line of code with the integrator |
| E | Convergence order | within **0.1** of theory — 4 for RK4, 8 for the high-order tableau | A step-halving study |
| E | Position against GMAT, J2 only, 400 km circular, i = 51.6° | **< 10 m after one orbit, < 1 km after 24 h** | GMAT R2026a fixture, settings recorded |
| E | Determinism | **bit-identical** after 24 h of simulated time, between two runs and between 1× and 10000× | Itself — the one place an exact floating-point comparison is correct, spelled `bitIdentical()` since 2026-09-11 ([ADR 0017](../adr/0017-every-warning-is-an-error.md)) |
| F | Nodal regression | within **1 %** of `-1.5 n J2 (Re/p)^2 cos i` over 5 days | The analytic secular rate. The short-period oscillation in the node is about 0.03° against 25° of drift, so the budget sits an order of magnitude above the noise |
| C, G | Frame time | **16.6 ms** at 1920×1080, High preset, RTX A2000, Earth from 400 km | Measured and recorded, not asserted |

The frame's rows moved again on 2026-09-19 with M1-07's rulings. **The code
budget tightened a thousandfold**, from 0.1″ to 0.1 mas, on measurement: the
independent reference agrees with ERFA to 54 µas over 1900–2100, and at 0.1″
the test could not see an omitted frame bias (23 mas), the wrong nutation or
precession model (up to 3 mas) or UT1 wrong by less than about 6 ms (decision
76). **The model error gained a time dependence**: UT1 now comes from TT and a
ΔT the caller names (decision 72), so past the leap-second table the 13.5″ of
ΔUT1 grows by however far a held ΔT drifts -- at worst 1.15 s, 17.3″, in a
year of the IERS series since 1962.

The UTC/TAI/TT row read **1970**–2035 when this register was written and was
corrected to 1972 on 2026-09-09. UTC's leap-second era begins 1972-01-01 — ΔAT
was 10 s that day and has stepped since — and
[M1-04](tasks/m1-04-leap-seconds.md) reports `BeforeLeapSecondEra` for anything
earlier, so a round trip across 1970–1972 is not something the table can be
exact about. The budget the task asserts is the 1972 one.

The frame and Sun rows were amended on 2026-09-11 with decisions 27–29. **The
frame** now includes nutation, so its model error loses the ≤ 25″ nutation
term and gains polar motion, ≤ 0.6″ — the largest pole excursion in the IERS
EOP 20 C04 series, 1962–2025. Its code budget stays 0.1″, now against an
independent implementation rather than ERFA. **The Sun** moved from 0.01° and
2e-4 AU to **0.1″ and 1e-6 AU**: `eraEpv00` puts the Earth within 11.2 km of
DE405 over 1900–2100 by its own comparison, which is 0.016″ of direction and
7.5e-8 AU of distance, so the budgets keep sixfold and thirteenfold headroom
over the implementation's stated worst case. The fixture must be geometric —
no light-time, no aberration — because aberration alone is 20.5″.
*(2026-09-20: moved again, to **0.02″ and 5e-8 AU**, once M1-08 measured
`eraEpv00` against the fixture rather than quoting ERFA's note -- worst 0.0085″
and 2.14e-8 AU at the forty epochs, so each budget is about twice its
measurement. The headroom argument above is exactly what decision 85 rejected:
a budget with twelvefold headroom absorbs a regression in silence. The span the
budget is asserted over is the fixture's, 2000–2050; ERFA's own worst over the
wider 1900–2100 is 0.016″, looser than the budget, so widening the span means
re-measuring. Decision 85.)* **TDB − TT**
keeps its 100 µs: ERFA claims 3 ns, but a budget can be no tighter than the
independent reference it is checked against, and that reference is chosen in
M1-05. *(2026-09-19: chosen -- Skyfield's evaluation of USNO Circular 179 eq.
2.6, measured within 9.28 µs of `eraDtdb` over 1900–2100 -- and the budget
moved to 20 µs, twice that. Decisions 53 and 54.)*

## 6. Scope fences for milestone 1

Written down because a fence nobody recorded is a fence somebody walks through.

- The vessel is a **test particle**: no mass, no thrust, no attitude.
- The force model is **Earth point mass and J2 only**. No third bodies, no drag,
  no solar radiation pressure, no J3 or J4.
- The propagated state is **Cartesian**. Equinoctial elements are deferred.
- **No DE440.** The analytic Sun lights the scene; it does not pull on anything.
- **`Vec3` stays unit-free and frame-free.** `PROJECT_STATE.md` section 7.6 stays
  open. *(Amended twice. The unit half fell on 2026-09-17 with
  [ADR 0019](../adr/0019-vectors-carry-their-unit.md): `Vec3` carries its unit.
  The frame half **still stands for `core`** after decision 98 on 2026-09-20 --
  frames were added to the render-side `Mat4` and to a view-local
  `FramedVec3`, and `core`'s `Vec3`, `Quat` and `Position` carry no frame.)*
- **`RenderQuality` presets only.** No configuration file, no adaptive
  controller.
- **Night lights** land in phase B; the **specular water mask** is deferred until
  a source is located.
- Every phase ends with a **gate task**: asan, `linux-sanitize`, `linux-gcc`, the
  fuzzers and a coverage review, with the numbers recorded.

Checked against the source on the day the plan was written, rather than
recalled. Each task that depends on one of these re-checks it before use.

| Fact | Verified value |
|---|---|
| This project's licence | MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 Peter Lennartz |
| `stb` | Dual: MIT **or** the Unlicense (public domain), the user's choice |
| `bc7enc_rdo` | **Three licences in one repository.** Most sources dual MIT / Unlicense (Richard Geldreich); `bc7e.ispc` Apache-2.0 (Binomial LLC); a bundled LodePNG under a zlib-style permissive licence. **Only the MIT/Unlicense files are compiled**: `bc7enc.cpp/h`, `rgbcx.h/cpp`, and `ert.cpp/h` if rate-distortion optimisation is wanted. `bc7e.ispc` needs Intel's ISPC compiler and is never built |
| Catch2 | **Boost Software License 1.0** |
| DejaVu fonts | Bitstream Vera derived: permissive, bundling inside a larger package allowed, notice required, the fonts may not be sold by themselves, derivatives must be renamed. Latest release **2.37**, asset `dejavu-fonts-ttf-2.37.zip` |
| AgX minimal implementation | **MIT**, "Copyright (c) 2024 Missing Deadlines (Benjamin Wrensch)"; the constants derive from Troy Sobotka's OCIO configuration. *(Re-checked 2026-09-25 by M1-15, decision 183: the evidence first written here -- a licensing discussion on the author's repository -- could not be found. That repository now holds one placeholder commit (2025-09-17), and its licence issue, #23, concerns the engine's public API rather than the blog post. The MIT notice itself is confirmed by independent copies of the code, among them NVIDIA's nvpro_core, which ships it as `PACKAGE-LICENSES/minimal-agx-LICENSE.md`. The blog page could not be reached from the project's machine; **the owner confirmed the MIT header on it on 2026-09-25**.)* |
| ETOPO 2022, 60 arc-second, ice surface | `https://www.ngdc.noaa.gov/mgg/global/relief/ETOPO2022/data/60s/60s_surface_elev_gtif/ETOPO_2022_v1_60s_N90W180_surface.tif` — one global GeoTIFF, 444 MB, dated 2022-10-04. Public domain, a US Government work |
| NASA GMAT | **Apache-2.0**, current release **R2026a**, Windows build available |
| The atmosphere method | Hillaire, S. (2020), *A Scalable and Production Ready Sky and Atmosphere Rendering Technique*, Computer Graphics Forum 39(4), EGSR 2020, DOI 10.1111/cgf.14050 |
| The Orbiter archive format | `Utils/tileedit/qt/src/ZTreeMgr.{h,cpp}` in the reference clone, MIT. A magic-tagged header, a table of contents of quadtree nodes each carrying a file offset, an inflated size and four child indices, then zlib-deflated per-tile blobs. Surface tiles are DDS/DXT1; elevation is Orbiter's own ELEV format (`elv_io.cpp`, also MIT) |
| ERFA *(verified 2026-09-10)* | **BSD-3-Clause**, after a preamble on its SOFA heritage; copyright the NumFOCUS Foundation. Uniform across the repository. Latest release **v2.0.1**, 2023-10-13 |
| SOFA *(verified 2026-09-10)* | Its own licence, SPDX `SOFA`: derived work must say it is derived, describe its differences in the source, and name no routine `iau…` or `sofa…`. ERFA exists to avoid those conditions |
| The stellar and sidereal days *(verified 2026-09-11)* | Stellar day **86 164.098 903 691 s**, sidereal day 86 164.090 530 832 88 s (IERS useful constants). ERA turns once per stellar day |

| 92-97 | The six M1-09 rulings, below: four put before its code and two that followed from the first | [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-transforms-carry-their-units.md), [M1-09](tasks/m1-09-orbsim-view.md), and the header of [`src/view/Mat4.hpp`](../../src/view/Mat4.hpp) |
| 98 | Frames, reversing 94 | [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-transforms-carry-their-frames.md), [`src/view/Frame.hpp`](../../src/view/Frame.hpp) and the header of [`src/view/Mat4.hpp`](../../src/view/Mat4.hpp) |
| 99–105 | The seven M1-10 rulings, below: the reversed projection, its tolerances, and the flat projection ruled out | [M1-10](tasks/m1-10-reverse-z-projection.md), [ADR 0003](../adr/0003-reverse-z-depth.md)'s update of 2026-09-21, and the headers of [`src/view/Projection.hpp`](../../src/view/Projection.hpp) and [`tests/test_projection.cpp`](../../tests/test_projection.cpp) |
| 106–114 | The nine M1-87 rulings, below: two scalars validated at construction, and what that removes | [ADR 0022](../adr/0022-a-bounded-scalar-validates-itself.md), [M1-87](tasks/m1-87-validated-scalars.md), and the headers of [`src/core/Units.hpp`](../../src/core/Units.hpp) and [`src/orbit/Orbit.hpp`](../../src/orbit/Orbit.hpp) |

*(The row above was added on 2026-09-21. This table stopped at 98 while section
9 ran to 105, which contradicts the promise at the top of this file that
section 8 maps every decision to where it is recorded and that the table reads
in both directions. Section 9's table was also split in two by a blank line
before decision 99, so 99 to 105 rendered as plain text rather than as rows;
that is repaired in the same pass.)*

Two further amendments were made in the same pass and belong to no decision in
the table: [ADR 0005](../adr/0005-correctness-is-enforced-by-tools.md) gained a
note that the phase gates are where the sanitizers and the second compiler run,
since there is no CI; and [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-render-quality-is-a-struct.md)
records that `RenderQuality` lives in `orbsim_view` rather than `src/render/`,
which follows from decision 17.

**Two records belong to no decision here at all**, and saying so is what keeps
the claim above — that this table reads in both directions — true. Added
2026-09-13, when both turned out to point at nothing and to be pointed at by
nothing:

| Record | Why it has no register row |
|---|---|
| [ADR 0017](../adr/0017-every-warning-is-an-error.md) | Every warning as an error. A standing rule about how the project is built, taken 2026-09-11 outside the queue; it changes no task, so by the boundary at the top of this file it is an ADR and not a register entry. It extends [0005](../adr/0005-correctness-is-enforced-by-tools.md) |
| [ADR 0018](../adr/0018-state-from-elements-reports.md) | `stateFromElements` reports. A defect fix on 2026-09-13 that changed a public signature, so it spans files and earns a record; it changes no task either. [`../HISTORY.md`](../HISTORY.md) has the measurement behind it |
| [ADR 0023](../adr/0023-the-processor-we-assume.md) | The processor this project assumes, and `-ffp-contract=off` for every file compiled. Asked for by the owner during M1-16's planning; decisions 200-202 hold the rulings, because the OpenEXR pin of decision 197 depends on it |

The error budgets in section 5 appear again in the record that owns them:
phase A's in [0009](../adr/0009-time-is-a-type-with-a-scale.md),
[0014](../adr/0014-radiometric-chain.md) and
[0016](../adr/0016-the-astronomy-is-erfa.md), phase E's in
[0011](../adr/0011-the-integrator-has-three-seams.md). Each also appears in its
task document, in the test that asserts it, and in the commit message —

## 9. Rulings since the queue was built

Taken by the owner after the twenty-six above, each put as a question with its
alternatives, their costs and a recommendation, and each the owner's answer.
Numbered on from 26 so that a reference to a decision number stays unambiguous.

| # | Decision | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 27 | How ERFA is used | **ERFA computes the astronomy** — TDB − TT, the celestial-to-terrestrial rotation, the Sun — fetched and pinned like every other dependency, built unedited, and called from `src/astro/` through typed wrappers. Its validation runs in `check`. Considered: ERFA as a reference only, a verbatim copy, a port into the house style. Ruled 2026-09-11 |
| 28 | The time scales' own arithmetic | **Stays exact and ours.** UTC, TAI and TT are integer picoseconds in `core/Time.hpp`; ERFA's `eraDat` extrapolates past its table, which ADR 0009 rules out. Ruled 2026-09-11 |
| 29 | Nutation | **Modelled**, IAU 2000A through ERFA, where decision 14 had deferred it as 1,365 terms to transcribe. The frame's model error falls from ≤ 40″ to ≤ 14.1″. Ruled 2026-09-11 |
| 30 | M1-05 and M1-06 | **M1-06 runs first.** M1-05 checks TDB against values that arrive through M1-06's fixture reader, which the queue had placed after it — an ordering fault in the plan since 2026-09-08. The task numbers stay, because commits and records cite them. Considered: M1-05 keeping its reference values as a table in the test source. Ruled 2026-09-11 |
| 31 | The Sun outside ERFA's span | **Reported by name**, `OutsideEphemerisRange`, from ERFA's own status: outside JD 2415020.0–2488070.0 TDB, which is 1899-12-31T12:00 to 2100-01-01T12:00. Considered: accepting the accuracy loss ERFA documents there. Ruled 2026-09-11 |
| 32 | The leap-second table's expiry | **2027-01-01T00:00:00 UTC**, which is exactly what IERS Bulletin C 72 (2026-07-06) guarantees. Considered: the `leap-seconds.list` file's own expiry of 2027-06-28, which additionally assumes the 2027 March opportunity goes unused — true of every year so far, but ITU-R TF.460 lists 31 March as a second-preference date and no bulletin has ruled it out. Too conservative costs a named refusal; too generous costs a wrong answer that looks right. A row in [`../STATUS.md`](../STATUS.md) carries the date, and no test fails on a calendar date. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 33 | Negative leap seconds | **The arithmetic handles one**, generically, and a `static_assert` records that every published step is +1. Since no published table can execute that path, the queries take a `std::span` and the suite drives them with a **synthetic** table. Considered: refusing a negative step outright, which is less code and a riskier change on the day one is announced. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 34 | A UTC Julian date inside a leap second | **ERFA's quasi-Julian convention**: the fraction is of that UTC day, "whether the length is 86399, 86400 or 86401 SI seconds" (`dtf2d.c`). Without it the fraction reaches 1.0000116 and breaks the [0, 1) the type promises. Considered: letting it exceed one, and making `julianDate()` fallible for all five scales. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 35 | How a conversion builds another scale's instant | **One private factory and one `friend`**, `detail::Builder`, documented as the one door. Considered: a friend per conversion, which grows with every scale M1-05 adds; and making the parts constructor public, which would give up the normalisation postcondition M1-03 bought. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 36 | Whether every conversion returns `std::expected` | **No.** `ttFromTai` and `taiFromTt` are a fixed offset between two scales that both run on the SI second: they cannot fail, and their signature says so. The four that consult the table report. Considered: uniformity, which would make every call site unwrap an error that cannot occur — the dead defensive code ADR 0002 argues against. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 37 | The independent reference | **`std::chrono::get_leap_second_info`**, whose leap seconds come from the platform's tzdata — a separate transcription of the same IERS bulletins. Measured on all four toolchains *before* being relied on: usable everywhere, agreeing on all 28 rows. It reports itself **skipped, loudly** where a platform has no tzdata. Considered: ERFA's `eraDat`, which would drag M1-05's dependency pin into this task. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 38 | The 1972–2035 budget | **Amended.** UTC ↔ TAI is exact wherever the table is valid; TAI ↔ TT is exact over all of 1972–2035, because it needs no table. The original wording asserted a round trip over years the table deliberately refuses. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 39 | Three existing assertions in `test_time.cpp` | **Changed, with the go-ahead.** The one requiring 23:59:60 UTC to be *refused* is inverted — its own comment had anticipated M1-04 — while keeping the half that did not change: 23:59:60 is still refused on every day the table gives no leap second to, and on every scale but UTC. The normalisation helper's bound widens by a second for UTC, and the error enumeration gains the two new names. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 40 | `LeapSeconds.hpp` and `.cpp` | **Both.** The UTC-day queries are `constexpr` in the header because `fromCalendar` evaluates them while building `kUnixEpoch`, which is a `UtcTime` and must stay a constant expression — the trap `std::isfinite` sprang on MSVC. The TAI-instant half needs nothing at compile time and is out of line, which keeps a reverse scan of the table out of every translation unit that includes `core/Time.hpp`. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 41 | A fuzz target for the time scales | **Added**, `tests/fuzz_time.cpp`. The calendar and the Julian date are the second surface in this project that turns bytes from outside into numbers, which is what VERIFICATION.md rule 13 says to point a fuzzer at. First run: 6,252,716 executions in 181 s under ASan and UBSan, zero findings. Ruled 2026-09-18 |
| 42 | The fixture format | **The project's own plain format, produced by a committed converter** (`scripts/horizons-fixture.py`), not the raw Horizons response. Measured first: a raw response can never match a committed checksum, because line 7 stamps the moment of the request -- two identical queries five seconds apart differ -- and the `EOP file` and `EOP coverage` lines change daily. The converter drops exactly those three lines, keeps the rest of Horizons' header as comments, copies every number as text, writes LF, and refuses a response in the wrong units, frame or corrections; two fetches at different moments converted to byte-identical files. Considered: parsing the raw response and hashing its data block alone, which would stop `sha256sum -c` working and leave M1-05's reference values and M1-68's GMAT file each needing a parser of their own. The first answer given was A, confirmed as B before any code was written, because every answer the round before had been A. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 43 | The `retrieved = <date> by <who>` key | **Dropped.** Anything in the file that changes per run breaks the hash, and it would name a person in every file. The git history of `checksums.sha256` records when a fixture was generated and by whom. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 44 | Verifying the checksums | **In `check`**, through the CTest test `horizons_fixture_checksums`, a CMake script using `file(SHA256)` so that no `sha256sum` is needed on Windows. A wrong hash fails, naming both; a fixture not generated is reported **skipped** in CTest's summary, never passed. Considered: `sha256sum -c` by hand, as the README first said -- VERIFICATION.md rule 21 says a check not reachable from `check` is an intention. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 45 | The reader's errors | **A name per failure, and the line it happened on**: `FileNotFound`, `EmptyFile`, `TruncatedFile` (a last line with no newline -- the shape a download cut off by a proxy takes), `MissingHeaderKey`, `WrongColumnCount`, `NonNumericField`, and **`UnexpectedHeaderValue`**, added on a follow-up question because a file whose header says `AU-D` would otherwise be read wrongly by a factor of 1.5e8 once the reader converts units. The task had named four errors against six failure cases. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 46 | Units | **At the first moment a number exists.** The untyped layer keeps every field as text; typed readers turn it into `TdbTime`, `Position` and `Velocity` in one step, kilometres to metres by moving the decimal exponent and parsing once -- correctly rounded, where parsing and multiplying by 1000 lands on a different double for three of the first eight Horizons-shaped values tried. **`InvalidEpoch`** followed from it: an epoch that is a number but not an instant the time type can hold needs a name of its own, and was added without a separate question as a consequence of this ruling -- flagged here so that it can be reversed. Considered: a generic reader returning bare doubles, with the conversion in M1-08. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 47 | The Sun fixture's epochs | **40, all at noon TDB**: 36 on a 507-day stride from J2000.0 -- a yearly stride would sample one direction 36 times -- and the perihelion and aphelion of 2000 and 2050, whose days were read from Horizons' own daily distances. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 48 | Corrections to the task and the README | **Made**: the task said the fixture is committed and pointed at a README under tests/fixtures, which never existed -- both left over from before 2026-09-17, and the README's `sha256sum -c` recipe could not pass on a raw response. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 49 | The reader's shape | `tests/FixtureFile.hpp` and `.cpp` in `orbsim_test_support`; parsing from text, apart from reading the file, so that every malformed case is a string beside its assertion; the data directory through a compile definition, as the application's `ORBSIM_ASSET_DIR`; numbers through `std::from_chars`, as `main.cpp` reads them. The two follow-up answers that renamed the reader and the checksum file were conditional on decision 42 going the other way, and lapsed with it. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 50 | Generating the Sun fixture | **Generated on this machine** and its SHA-256, `61291048...7fd53`, committed. Querying Horizons is within the 2026-09-17 ruling; redistributing its output is not, and the fixture and the raw response are gitignored. The README's recipe was then run verbatim in a clean directory and reproduced the hash. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 51 | `TimePoint`'s members | **Initialised** -- `f64 mjd_{}; std::int64_t picos_{};` -- in `core/Time.hpp`, outside M1-06. Without them `cppcoreguidelines-pro-type-member-init` could not see that an instant held inside an aggregate is ever set, and reported `StateAtEpoch`; every later struct holding an instant would have been reported too. Measured before deciding: full build clean, every `static_assert` still holding, zero lint findings, all tests passing. Considered: a `NOLINT` on the struct. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 52 | gcc's `-Wabi-tag` on the reader's structs | **Switched off at the site, for gcc alone**, around `HeaderLine`, `FixtureRow` and `FixtureTable` in `tests/FixtureFile.hpp`, with the reason beside it: they hold `std::string`, whose libstdc++ "cxx11" ABI tag protects code shipped as a binary against the pre-C++11 string ABI, and this project builds everything from source with one. Measured before deciding: tagging the types instead is accepted by Windows clang but spreads under gcc, which then reports every function returning a tagged type. Considered: tagging, and keeping views into one owned buffer instead of strings. The one other site gcc reported, `dataDirectory()` returning a `std::filesystem::path`, was **fixed rather than suppressed** -- it returns a view of the build's string literal instead. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 53 | The independent reference for TDB − TT | **Skyfield 1.55** (Brandon Rhodes, MIT), whose `tdb_minus_tt` is USNO Circular 179 eq. 2.6 (Kaplan 2005) -- a truncation of Fairhead & Bretagnon (1990) written independently of SOFA. Checked on the day: its dependencies are certifi, jplephem, numpy and sgp4, and none is ERFA. Considered: **NOVAS C 3.1**, which evaluates the same equation -- Skyfield ported it, so taking both adds no independence -- and whose terms could not be confirmed: its user's guide states no licence and asks users to e-mail USNO, the USNO source URL returned HTTP 500, and the Astrophysics Source Code Library failed TLS verification. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 54 | The TDB − TT budget | **20 µs**, where it was 100 µs. Measured first: Circular 179's series differs from `eraDtdb` by at most **9.28 µs** over 1900–2100 (at 2023-02-13) and 9.51 µs over 1600–2200, so no budget checked against it can honestly be tighter than about 10 µs; 20 µs is twice that, and 0.6 m of the Earth's orbital motion. ERFA's own claim, 3 ns against a DE405 time ephemeris over 1950–2050, stays recorded and not asserted. Considered: 100 µs as planned, and 15 µs. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 55 | The TDB − TT fixture | **Committed**, with the script that generates it and its recipe, in M1-06's format under a new `data/skyfield/`: epochs 1900–2100 -- ERFA's Sun span, which M1-08 converts through -- on a 37-day stride, not commensurate with the year, about 1,975 rows. The 2026-09-17 ruling not to redistribute was about Horizons' unstated terms; Skyfield's are MIT, so the suite that asserts decision 54 runs on a fresh clone rather than reporting itself skipped. Considered: gitignored with a committed checksum, as the Horizons fixtures are. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 56 | Which scale `eraDtdb` is evaluated at | **TDB, in both directions.** Its argument is formally TDB -- the "Given" list says so, and note 1 makes TT the allowance, "no practical effect". So the TDB-to-TT conversion evaluates it at its own argument, and the TT-to-TDB conversion takes **one fixed-point step** -- evaluate at TT, then again at TT plus the result -- which puts the argument within 1e-21 s of TDB: the first estimate is out by at most 1.1 ps, times the term's steepest slope, 3.3e-10. Measured over 1,000,000 seeded instants, 1990–2050: round trips exact in both directions, where one evaluation at each argument's own scale leaves 17.4% of them a picosecond out. It costs one more call, 5.9 µs. The task had the approximate direction the wrong way round, and is corrected. Considered: one evaluation each way, as written. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 57 | The TT ↔ TDB round-trip budget | **≤ 1 ps**, where it was 1e-9 s: one rounding to the nearest picosecond each way. On a picosecond grid the conversion cannot be a bijection, so exactness can be measured -- 0 of 1,000,000, decision 56 -- but not guaranteed, and it is recorded rather than asserted: asserting it would be asserting that every libm rounds alike. Considered: 1e-9 s, and bit identity. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 58 | TDB far from J2000 | **Converted everywhere, never refused.** `eraDtdb` returns no status, and TDB − TT is physically defined at every instant. Its accuracy is claimed for 1950–2050 (3 ns), measured against Circular 179 for 1600–2200 (≤ 9.51 µs), and bounded beyond: at six sample epochs from year 1 to year 9999 the whole term stayed within ±1.76 ms, so an error there is a few milliseconds, about 100 m of the Earth's orbital motion. The header says all three. Considered: refusing outside a stated span by name, as decision 31 does for the Sun. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 59 | UT1 across a leap second | **ERFA's `eraUtcut1` convention**: UT1 is the SI seconds elapsed in the UTC day plus ΔUT1, on days of 86 400 s. ERFA reaches it through TAI with ΔAT taken at 0h of the UTC day, which cancels, so it needs no table and cannot fail. ΔUT1 on a day ending in a leap second is the IERS value for that day. The reverse conversion never produces 23:59:60: UT1 → UTC → UT1 is exact wherever UTC has an instant to give, and UTC → UT1 → UTC exact except inside a positive leap second, which comes back one second later, because the forward map is two-to-one there. *(Corrected the same day, before any code: this said "exact everywhere", which fails on a day ending in a negative leap second -- with one ΔUT1 for the day, one second of UT1 then has no UTC instant at all. None has occurred, and what the reverse conversion does there went to the owner as a further question.)* With ΔUT1 = 0, UT1 steps back one second at the midnight after a leap second -- the published ΔUT1 steps by +1 s there and removes it -- and the ≤ 0.9 s model error holds at every instant. So UTC to UT1 returns its instant, and UT1 to UTC reports `YearOutOfRange` at the calendar's two ends, since a UTC instant must stay inside the calendar that says how long its day is. Found while preparing the task: its "with ΔUT1 = 0, UT1 equals UTC exactly" cannot hold inside a leap second. Considered: refusing such an instant by name, and holding UT1 still through it -- continuous, and a second wrong once a real ΔUT1 is supplied. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 60 | `DeltaUt1` | **A validated type.** A factory reports a value that is not finite as `NotFinite`, and one beyond ±0.9 s, inclusive, as a new `DeltaUt1OutOfRange` -- ITU-R TF.460-6 (2002), Annex 1 §D.1.2: "The departure of UTC from UT1 should not exceed ± 0.9 s". Held in integer picoseconds, so 0.3 s is exactly 3·10^11 ps, and the bound keeps that conversion from overflowing. ΔUT1 will arrive from an IERS file, so it is reported rather than asserted (ADR 0002). Considered: a wrapper over `Seconds` with preconditions. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 61 | The default ΔUT1 | **No default argument.** Every call site names its zero, so the 0.9 s model error is visible where it is taken. Considered: a default argument, which the task's "the default is zero" allowed. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 62 | ERFA's validation | **Both of upstream's programs**, `t_erfa_c` and `t_erfa_c_extra`, as CTest tests under those names -- upstream's `make check` runs both, and the second covers `erfaversion.c` and `erfadatextra.c`, which are among the 249 files compiled. The task named only the first. Measured before the pin, on clang 23.1.0, clang 23.1.1 with ASan and UBSan, gcc-14 at -O0 and -O2, and MSVC 19.51: all 249 files compile with zero warnings and both programs pass. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 63 | ERFA's file list | **Globbed**, less the two validation programs, and configuration fails unless it finds exactly 249, so a changed pin fails loudly instead of compiling a different set. Considered: the 249 names written out. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 64 | Fuzzing the new conversions | **Added to `fuzz_time`**: the `DeltaUt1` factory, and both round trips. Not in the task; ΔUT1 is the next value that will arrive from a file (VERIFICATION.md rule 13). Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 65 | The amplitude-and-period test | **Derived tolerances.** The annual peak within 60 µs of the analytic 2e√(GM☉ a)/c² = 1.657 ms, because the neglected terms' amplitudes sum to about that (measured: 16 µs). Zero crossings within ±2.1 days of the Sun's mean anomaly g = 0° and 180° from the Astronomical Almanac's low-precision formula -- 60 µs over the annual term's slope of 28.5 µs a day (measured: ≤ 0.9 day). And TDB ahead of TT from perihelion to aphelion, which a wrong sign fails. The constants come from cited sources when the test is written, not from the spike that sized these. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 66 | What M1-05 leaves M1-07 | **Recorded as M1-07's open question, not answered here.** A TT clock reaches UT1 only through UTC, and UTC only through the leap-second table, which expires 2027-01-01 -- so, as planned, the Earth's orientation would be refused past that date. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 67 | UT1 in the second a negative leap second removes | **Reported by name**, as a new `InsideRemovedLeapSecond`. A day ending in a negative leap second holds 86 399 s, so under one ΔUT1 the forward conversion skips a second of UT1, and for an instant in it no UTC instant exists. The check is written against a table passed in, as decision 33's queries are, so the suite drives it with the synthetic negative table; the committed table cannot reach it, since no negative leap second has occurred. Found while decision 59 was being written down, which had claimed UT1 → UTC → UT1 exact everywhere, and put the same day. Considered: leaving it to the static_assert that fails the build the day a negative step is transcribed -- a known gap, which decision 33 declined to leave for UTC ↔ TAI -- and landing in the next day as if ΔUT1 had stepped by -1 s, which never fails and leaves the round trip a second out in that window. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 68 | Proving the TDB fixed-point step is there | **The round trip is exact on at least 99% of the sweep, both ways**, asserted beside the 1 ps budget. Found by the mutation pass: dropping the step survived, because both ways stay inside 1 ps. Measured: 10,000 of 10,000 exact with the step, 8,263 without it. A rate rather than bit identity at each instant, which decision 57 declined. Considered: exactness at a few instants chosen far from rounding ties, and accepting the gap. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 69 | Proving the time of day reaches ERFA | **A physics test**: twelve hours apart on one day, every fifth day of 2024-2025, TDB - TT changes as the Kepler problem's annual term does, within **1 us** -- the neglected terms' amplitudes times their rates times half a day come to about 0.7 us; measured 0.37 us worst, against changes of up to 14.5 us. Found by the mutation pass: dropping the time of day survived, since every fixture epoch is at 0h. Considered: regenerating the fixture at varying times of day, which changes decision 55's recipe and sees less, and accepting the gap. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 70 | The geocentre | **Accepted as held by the code and its comment, not by a test.** Moving the observer to the equator survived the mutation pass: the topocentric terms are about 2 us, a tenth of the budget, and only ERFA itself resolves them. Considered: a test calling `eraDtdb` directly, which would need an exception to ADR 0016's rule that ERFA's headers are included in `src/astro/` alone. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 71 | Two assertions added without a ruling | **Kept**: the budget test's demand that the worst difference exceed 1 us, so that a reference computed by ERFA itself would fail; and \|TDB - TT\| < 2 ms at both ends of the calendar, 1.76 ms having been measured. With them, decision 69's own guard of the same kind: the largest half-day change must exceed ten times its tolerance. Ruled 2026-09-19, the third as a consequence of 69 and flagged here so it can be reversed |
| 72 | Where a `Ut1Time` comes from for a TT clock -- decision 66's question | **UT1 = TT - ΔT, without the leap-second table.** `ut1FromTt` and `ttFromUt1` under a validated `DeltaT` -- integer picoseconds, `NotFinite`, and a new `DeltaTOutOfRange` beyond ±10^6 s, a limit of the representation rather than of the Earth, since the largest ΔT any model gives inside years 1–9999 is about 2.1e5 s; `deltaTFromLeapSecondTable(TtTime, DeltaUt1)`, 32.184 s + ΔAT - ΔUT1, exact where the table holds and refused by name outside it; and `kDeltaTHeldAtTableExpiry`, 69.184 s, for a caller past the expiry to take by name. The table refuses because a UTC label is an integer not yet chosen; UT1 is a physical angle, continuous in TT, whose prediction is a model with a size -- the footing ΔUT1 = 0 already has. Measured first, from the IERS EOP 20 C04 series, 1962 to 2026-08-20: ΔT drifts at worst 1.15 s in a year (1972) and 10.4 s in ten, 0.54 s and 3.5 s since 2000, and about +0.1 s a year in 2026; ΔT on 2026-09-17 was 69.19 s (Bulletin A: UT1 - UTC = -0.0086 s); and UT1 - UTC never exceeded 0.81 s in size since 1972. Considered: accepting the refusal and renewing the table with every Bulletin C -- no Earth orientation outside 1972–2026, Apollo-era scenarios included, and the Earth stepping back 15″ at the midnight after every leap second with ΔUT1 unmodelled; and a ΔT model for every date, which is a body of reference data outside milestone 1's fences, and which this signature admits later unchanged. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 73 | How the answer lands | **As its own task, M1-86, and its own commit, before M1-07.** It is verifiable on its own and touches only `core/Time.hpp`. Numbered 86 rather than renumbering, as decision 30 did for M1-85: the numbers are identifiers. Ruled 2026-09-19 |
| 74 | Fuzzing it | **`fuzz_time` gains it**, as decision 64 did for ΔUT1: a ΔT from arbitrary bytes accepted only inside 10^6 s, both round trips bit for bit, and the table's ΔT against the UTC road wherever the table holds. Ruled 2026-09-19 |

That number stays as the stated budget, and beside it the declination is asserted **within 1e-3° of +0.002265°**, the offset the omitted aberration predicts — 20.49551″ times sin ε. That turns a bound into a claim about the residual: a sign error in the rotation fails both, and a declination of zero fails the second. The instants are USNO's Earth's Seasons, `https://aa.usno.navy.mil/data/Earth_Seasons`: 2024-03-20 03:06, 2025-03-20 09:01 and 2026-03-20 14:46 UT — three rather than one so that a single well-chosen instant cannot carry the case, and all three inside the leap-second table. Measured at them: +0.002259°, +0.001952° and +0.002368°, so the worst distance from the prediction is 3.13e-4°. **The tolerance was proposed as 2e-4° and measured to be wrong before it was written**: the published instants are rounded to the minute, worth ±1.4e-4° at 2.74e-4° a minute, and the Earth's monthly wobble about the Earth–Moon barycentre reaches about 7e-4° of declination. 1e-3° covers both and is 3.2× the measured worst. | 87 | The apsis and continuity tolerances | **Both derived from a sweep, and both widened in scope.** *The apsides*: the plan asked that the annual minimum and maximum distances land at 0.9833 and 1.0167 AU within 1e-4 AU, without saying which year; the test now sweeps **all fifty years of 2000–2050 daily** and asserts the worst gap, which costs milliseconds and retires the question. Measured: perihelion 0.9832436–0.9833551 AU, aphelion 1.0166426–1.0167538 AU, so the worst gaps from the published figures are 5.64e-5 and 5.74e-5 AU and the plan's 1e-4 holds with 1.8× margin — narrow enough that a single-year test could have been chosen well or badly with nobody the wiser. **The window is not the calendar year, and that was found by measuring rather than assumed**: perihelion sits astride the turn of the year, so a calendar year contains two of them and the deeper wins, which puts the "annual minimum" on **31 December** in 2003 and 2047. The sweep therefore runs 1 October to 31 March for perihelion and 1 April to 30 September for aphelion, each window holding exactly one apsis. With them the dates are clean and are asserted too: perihelion 2–5 January, aphelion 3–6 July. *Continuity*: the plan asked for "no jump larger than the per-step motion"; the bound is now **two-sided, 0.952° ≤ step ≤ 1.020° at one-day steps**, which catches a wrapped angle in either direction rather than one. Measured 0.952940°–1.019674°, and it fits a law rather than only the data: two-body motion at e = 0.016709 gives 1.019230° at perihelion and 0.953284° at aphelion, and the 4e-4° residual is the Earth's wobble about the Earth–Moon barycentre — 4671 km over 1 AU is 6.44″, times 2π/27.32 d. | 92 | How a 4x4 transform carries its units | **Fully, on four references.** `Mat4<kInXyz, kInW, kOutXyz, kOutW>`, mapping `Vec4<kInXyz, kInW>` to `Vec4<kOutXyz, kOutW>`, with the four block references derived rather than declared. A homogeneous 4x4 has no single unit -- its linear block is dimensionless while a projection's near-plane entry is metres -- and the assignment is closed under multiplication, which is what makes `projection * view` compile and `view * projection` not. Proven before the record was written: it builds on clang 23.1.0 under `-Weverything`, gcc-14 and MSVC 19.51, rejects five wrong compositions at compile time with each negative assertion inverted and watched failing, and costs 7% of a translation unit. Considered: sixteen bare f64 with typed edges, which checks the call but not the chain; templating on the translation column alone, which breaks at the first projection; and no general Mat4 at all. Recorded as [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-transforms-carry-their-units.md). Ruled 2026-09-20 |

The indices are strong types because clang-tidy cannot catch a transposition here: measured 2026-09-20, `bugprone-easily-swappable-parameters` silences any pair used together in one expression. | 96 | The swappable-parameters blind spot | **Closed project-wide, in its own commit before M1-09.** `SuppressParametersUsedTogether` is off in `.clang-tidy`. | 98 | Frames on `Mat4` after all, reversing decision 94 | **Added**, four days' worth of argument later the same day. What landed: `Mat4<kFrom, kTo, ...>` with the frames as template parameters; a `FrameTag` carrying a **dual** flag, because the transpose is a dual map -- **the owner's formulation, and better than this project's**: forced back into a matrix between the same two spaces, `transpose(AB) = transpose(B)transpose(A)` typechecks only where the operands' units line up, which excludes every chain containing a projection, while as a dual map it composes for all of them; a view-local `FramedVec3` so `core` stays frame-free; same-frame factories with `retargetFrame` as the one declared, greppable, unchecked frame change; and no `Frame::Ndc`, because clip and normalised device coordinates are already told apart by the unit. | 99 | Whether `infiniteReverseZPerspective` can be `constexpr` | **No, and the layout is split out so that part can be.** Measured 2026-09-21 on all three front ends: clang 23.1.0 and MSVC 19.51 both refuse `std::tan` in a constant expression, gcc-14 accepts it as an extension, so a `static_assert` against the whole function would be green on one and red on two -- the disagreement rule 20's second and third compilers exist to surface. The public entry point is `inline`; a `constexpr` `detail::perspectiveFromFocalLength` takes an already-computed focal length, which puts the matrix layout -- the y flip, the constant depth row, the near plane's slot, the absent far plane -- into **seven compile-time assertions**. Measured after: six of the fourteen mutants die at compile time because of it. Considered: `constexpr` as the task wrote it, which compiles clean under `-Weverything` but would be the only `constexpr` in this project with nothing proving it; and plain `inline` with the layout only tested at run time. A dimensionless quantity with no *kind* accepts any other ratio, and this one is passed beside exactly such a value -- the focal length -- so the kind is what makes a transposed call a compile error rather than a stretched image. Same mechanism as `Eccentricity`, and view-local because an aspect ratio is render-side (section 12). Considered: `Dimensionless`, which checks almost nothing; a width and height pair, which needs a pixel type this task has no use for and belongs with the swapchain; and the bare `f64`. Ruled 2026-09-21 |
| 101 | The near-plane test case | **At five near planes, none of them 1 m, and asserting the two numbers behind the quotient as well as the quotient.** M1-09 left one mutant alive -- the perspective divide multiplying by w -- and its record named this case as the executioner. **Measured before writing the test: it would not have been.** At a near plane of 1 m the depth numerator and denominator are the same number, so `n/n` and `n*n` are both 1 and the case accepts every wrong divide constructible; at any other near plane it accepts three of five. The owner asked for the cause before the fix and ruled once it was clear. **And the stated reason for the extra two assertions was itself wrong, corrected by the pass**: they read `transform()`'s output, before the divide, so they cannot see a fault *inside* it -- no near-plane check can, since "the near plane maps to 1" is the statement that the two numbers are equal. Considered: moving off 1 m only; leaving the case as the document specified. Ruled 2026-09-21 |

Measured: that holds only below a 10 m near plane -- the figure *is* 10/1e13 -- and at 100 m the depth is 1e-11. The sentence silently assumed a near plane. What is asserted is `depth == nearPlane / distance` exactly, over every near plane and distance tried, which is exact because the depth row is constant and one correctly rounded division is all that happens. Verified there is no defect underneath: no precision cliff, and 32-bit depth stays normal past 7e44 m. Considered: keeping the threshold with its precondition in prose; refusing large near planes in the function, which would invent a limit with no physical basis. Ruled 2026-09-21 |

The frustum edge lands on ±1 to **4 ulp** -- measured worst 2.0 in x and 1.0 in y over 200,000 (fov, aspect) pairs. The depth-precision floor is **4 f32 ulp**, about half the measured worst of 9, which is the right direction for a lower bound; the conventional control is asserted **bit-identical**, which is what it measured. The all-f32 path the GPU itself computes is asserted too, measured identical. **`f32` in `tests/test_projection.cpp` is an exception to non-negotiable 8**, granted because the claim *is* about what a 32-bit depth buffer holds and cannot be made in 64-bit arithmetic; the reason is written at the site. Ruled 2026-09-21 |
| 104 | The frustum-edge case's independence, the y flip's sign, and the sweeps | **Anchors that never call `std::tan`, the signed value, and a fixed grid where the space is one-dimensional.** Building the edge point from `tan(fov/2)` and checking it against a matrix entry of `1/tan(fov/2)` cannot detect an error in the conversion, because it cancels (VERIFICATION.md rule 2) -- so two anchors take the half-angle tangent from `std::numbers` instead, at 90 and 60 degrees. Measured to earn it: the tangent mutant dies in the anchor case. The y flip is asserted **signed** -- a point above the axis must land at -1 -- because a magnitude check passes whether the flip is present, missing or doubled. Monotonicity uses a fixed geometric grid, 100 points per decade from 0.1 m to 1e13 m, which covers the interval rather than sampling it and needs no seed; the three-dimensional (fov, aspect, near) sweep is a seeded draw, `20260921`. Ruled 2026-09-21 |
