Nothing else in the milestone asks for one: the orbit track draws in the 3D scene. **Measured against the real headers**: a flat projection is a different `Mat4` type from the perspective one, with a per-metre linear block and plain-number output, and it is **affine** -- so `transformPoint` accepts it, loosening the guard decision 93 put up. A second projection would also want `Projection` renamed to `PerspectiveProjection`, editing M1-09's shipped header. Considered: building the view-space variant now, which is coherent but has no caller in this milestone and is the speculative generality section 17 warns about; building the pixel variant now, which cannot be specified yet. Ruled 2026-09-21 |
| 106 | Where a validated scalar's error lives | **A new `enum class UnitError` in `core/Units.hpp`**, beside the types it belongs to, with `describe()` and an exhaustive `switch`. `core` cannot depend on `orbit`, so `OrbitError` is out of reach, and this is the precedent decision 90 set for `EphemerisError`: an error enum lives in its module's header rather than in a layer-wide one. Considered: moving `Eccentricity` into `orbit/` to reach `OrbitError`, which takes a unit out of the header ADR 0019 made the home of all nine; and reusing `TimeError`, which would be a lie in a header of the kind that got `ParabolicElements` removed. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 107 | What a validated `Eccentricity` accepts | **Finite and non-negative, with no upper bound.** A non-finite value is `NotFinite`, a negative one `NegativeEccentricity`. No cap: a hyperbolic eccentricity is unbounded in principle and `Orbit.hpp` records convergence measured from 0 to 100, so any cap would be a threshold carrying a hidden scale -- the defect class this project already shipped once. Considered: a stated cap, and refusing only negatives while letting a NaN through, which is the case the probe found. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 108 | Whether `GravParam` is validated too | **Yes, and `NonPositiveGravity` goes with it.** Both scalars become validated types, so the five checks that reported a non-positive `mu` -- in `elementsFromState`, `propagate` and `propagateElements` -- are removed along with the enumerator, because an error a caller cannot receive is the dead defensive code ADR 0002 argues against. **The cost was measured and put up before the ruling**: it supersedes ADR 0019's clause that `mu / (r*r)` produces an acceleration type, and M1-62's planned named failure for a non-positive `mu`. Decision 110 then recovered the first of those. Considered: validating `Eccentricity` alone and leaving `mu` reported, which keeps both; and deferring `GravParam` to M1-62, where the force model is the first code that needs `mu` in unit arithmetic. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 109 | How the change lands | **As its own task, [M1-87](tasks/m1-87-validated-scalars.md), before M1-11, with one ADR.** It changes a public interface of `orbit/` and a type in `core/Units.hpp` that every later task builds on, so it is not a fix to fold into the next thing. Numbered 87 rather than renumbering, as decisions 30 and 73 did for M1-85 and M1-86: the numbers are identifiers. Considered: commits now without a task number, which would leave a public signature change recorded only in a commit message; and writing the document and running it after M1-11. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 110 | The shape of a validated scalar | **A class that keeps its quantity.** A private constructor, a `from()` factory returning `std::expected`, `value()` for the bare `f64` as today, and -- for `GravParam` -- **`quantity()` returning the `Scalar<m^3/s^2>`**, so `mu.quantity() / (r * r)` is still an m/s^2 quantity and M1-62 has what ADR 0019 promised it. This works because `Scalar<R>` *derives from* `mp_units::quantity<R, f64>`: the unit algebra is the base's, so it survives being held rather than inherited. **Found while implementing decision 108, and put up rather than absorbed**, because it recovers a cost that decision had accepted. `Eccentricity` takes the plain shape, having no arithmetic role now or in any task document. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 111 | The error names | **One name per failure**: `NotFinite`, `NegativeEccentricity`, `NonPositiveGravity` -- the last keeping the name it had in `OrbitError`, which commits, tests and records already use. Decision 45's rule, and each says what it means physically rather than which predicate failed. Considered: generic `NotFinite` and `OutOfRange` shared by both factories, which tells a caller less than every other error enum in this project does; and an enum per type, where decision 90 chose one per module. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 112 | Four assertions that become unwritable | **Removed, and replaced by tests of the factories.** Making a non-positive `mu` unrepresentable means the code four existing assertions call can no longer be called: three in `tests/test_orbit.cpp`'s `TEST_CASE("failures are reported, not approximated")` and one in `tests/test_orbit_scales.cpp`'s `TEST_CASE("non-finite inputs are refused by name")`. **Neither case is deleted**, both keep every other assertion, and the first keeps the name `VERIFICATION.md` rule 6 cites it by. What they checked has not stopped being checked: it moved from a runtime report to a type that cannot hold the value, which is rule 24 -- prefer the bug you cannot write. Put up explicitly under working agreement 1, which forbids changing a test without a go-ahead. Considered: keeping a runtime path so the assertions still compile, which is the dead code ADR 0002 rejects. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 113 | How 56 construction sites read | **The factory, plus a `consteval` literal helper.** `from()` returns `std::expected` for a runtime value; `eccentricity(f64)` and `gravParam(f64)` beside it are **a compile error** on a bad literal rather than a runtime unwrap. 3 sites are in `src/` and 53 in `tests/`, where an element table should stay readable and a mistyped literal should fail the build. Considered: the plain `from(x).value()` at every site, which is exactly `kDeltaUt1Unmodelled`'s spelling and makes 53 test sites longer; and user-defined literals `_ecc` and `_mu`, which are shortest and read less clearly in an element table. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 114 | `orbitInfo`'s return type, reversing a ruling of the same day | **It keeps its plain `OrbitInfo`.** Earlier the same day it was ruled to return `std::expected`, alongside `stateFromElements`, to close the split where three entry points reported a non-positive `mu` and two asserted it. Decision 108 then made the value unrepresentable, and `mu > 0` was `orbitInfo`'s **only** guard -- every other quantity in it is computed to avoid overflow deliberately and returns infinity on purpose -- so a `std::expected` would carry nothing. The reversal is recorded rather than quietly dropped, as decision 98 was for 94. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 115 | Where the naive narrowing actually fails, and where M1-11's test puts its teeth | **Assert the law, and keep the 400 km case as a recorded negative.** The task document asked that narrowing before subtracting fail the 0.05 px budget "by a wide margin" with a camera at 400 km altitude looking at a point at Earth radius. **Measured before any code was written, in two independent implementations that agree to four digits -- a NumPy model and the project's own `Mat4`, `Projection` and `Camera`: it lands at 5.8e-4 px, 86 times *inside* the budget.** Half a metre of error at a range of 400 km is 1.25 microradians, which is 0.0016 px at 1920x1080. What camera-relative rendering actually buys is an error **independent of the distance to the origin** -- flat at 5.3e-5 px from Earth radius to 4.5e12 m -- where the naive error grows in proportion to it, reaching 4.7 px at 1 AU and 228 px at Neptune's distance, and grows as the range shrinks, reaching 0.43 px at 1 km and 5.6 px at 100 m. So the suite asserts the **law** across nine geometries, asserts the flatness directly, and puts the naive path's failure at 1 AU (95 times over) and at short range (8.6 and 111 times over). **The 400 km case stays, asserted to pass**, so that the document's original claim cannot quietly come back; it is also asserted to be at least five times worse than doing it properly, so the two paths are still visibly different there rather than the test comparing a thing with itself. Considered: moving the geometry to one where the naive path fails, which abandons the 400 km case phase A's acceptance criterion is stated in; and keeping the document as written, whose "must fail" assertion would have had to be deleted -- the decoration `VERIFICATION.md` rule 23 is about. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 116 | Whether the camera validates itself | **Yes: a private constructor and a `from()` factory returning `std::expected`, as ADR 0022 gives `Eccentricity` and `GravParam`.** The task document specified a plain aggregate; it was written on 2026-09-08, two weeks before a bounded scalar was made to validate itself. Three of the camera's four members have exactly that kind of bound, and the orientation is the one that matters: a quaternion that is not unit length produces a matrix that is **finite, plausible and not a rotation** -- the failure shape ADR 0022 exists to remove, and the same shape the eccentricity probe found. **Measured before the shape was chosen**: a class with a private constructor is still trivially copyable, which is what the task asked for, so the stronger form costs nothing structurally. A new `CameraError` with four values lives in `view/Camera.hpp`, which is decision 106's precedent -- an error enum lives in its module's header. Considered, and recommended by this project before the owner ruled otherwise: a plain aggregate with the unit-quaternion check as an assertion inside `viewMatrix`, on the grounds that `core/Math.hpp` states deliberately that unit length is a caller's precondition rather than an invariant `Quat` maintains, and that M1-21's controls rebuild the camera every frame and must now handle a reported error. The owner took the stronger option. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 117 | Whether M1-11 also builds the projection from the camera | **Yes, and it is temporary: M1-13 deletes it.** `projectionOf(const Camera&, Aspect)` returns the existing `std::expected<Projection, ProjectionError>` unchanged. Without it the camera's field of view and near plane would be carried but unread for two tasks, and M1-11's suite would have to wire the chain by hand. **Only `InvalidAspect` is reachable**, because `Camera::from` has already refused anything that could produce the other two -- and it refused it with *the same two predicates*, `view/Projection.hpp`'s own `isUsableFieldOfView` and `isFinitePositive`, rather than a second pair that could drift from them (`VERIFICATION.md` rule 2). The obligation to remove it is written into [M1-13](tasks/m1-13-pipelines.md)'s "Done when" list as well as into the header, so that it sits on a checklist rather than in a comment nobody opens. Considered: leaving it out entirely, which is section 17's "write the specific thing" and defers to M1-13. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 118 | M1-11's tolerances, the coupling that sets two of them, and the value that moved twice | **All three derived from measurement, two of them are the same number twice, and the first answer was wrong on a figure this project supplied.** The task asked for a view matrix orthonormal to 1e-15 and a round trip to 1e-14. **Measured: a quaternion of length 1 + e produces a matrix whose orthonormality residual is about eight to ten times e**, across six decades from 2.2e-16 to 1e-9 -- so whatever `Camera::from` admits for the orientation *is*, times that factor, the best the view matrix can be claimed to be. The coupling was assumed away in this project's first proposal and found only by measuring it. **The tolerance was then ruled twice.** First at 4 ulp, twice the 1.5 every producer here leaves over 500,000 draws, on this project's statement that the cost was "renormalise within a few hundred compositions". **That statement was wrong by two orders of magnitude and the test suite found it by failing**: composing unit quaternions without renormalising reaches 2.5 ulp after one product, 3.0 after two, **4.5 after five** and 6.0 after ten, because a freshly normalised quaternion is not itself exactly unit and the two errors add. Put back up with the corrected number, the owner ruled **16 ulp**, which gives the ten-product caller the same 2.7 times margin that 4 gave the direct producers. The coupled budgets follow: orthonormality, the determinant and the round trip are **270 ulp**, twice the 135, 135 and 132 measured over 200,000 orientations sitting on the 16 ulp boundary. The task's 1e-15 was unreachable at either value, and its 1e-14 is 45 ulp, which the admitted orientation error now exceeds outright. 16 ulp remains four orders tighter than `kRotationTolerance`, whose written argument -- "what fails this was never a rotation" -- it inherits. The reversal is recorded rather than quietly dropped, as decision 98 was for 94 and 114 for its own morning. Ruled 2026-09-22, twice |
| 119 | The 32-bit exception in `tests/test_camera.cpp` | **Granted, for two helpers, on decision 103's grounds.** `narrowedBeforeSubtracting` is the naive path the budget is measured against, and `pixelsIn32Bits` is the whole chain computed in 32 bits as a graphics card computes it. Both claims *are* about what a 32-bit float can hold and cannot be made in 64-bit arithmetic, which is exactly the reason decision 103 gave `tests/test_projection.cpp` its one exception to non-negotiable 8. The reason is written at both sites. **Measured: the 32-bit chain lands at 1.55 of the conditioning law's coefficient where narrowing the position alone lands at 0.683**, so a single bound of twice the law covers both paths and the suite carries one constant rather than two. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 120 | M1-20's claim about what quantises visibly | **Amended in place, with a dated note pointing at M1-11's measurement.** `m1-20-planetary-grid.md` said the `grid-jitter` probe's 0.25 m camera steps were "a distance that quantises visibly under naive `f32` narrowing". At 400 km altitude that is 5.8e-4 px, which is not visible, and at the limb it is 7.9e-5 px -- indistinguishable from doing it properly. The probe and its 0.05 px budget are unchanged, because they are the end-to-end confirmation of M1-11's number through the real GPU path; what changed is the sentence that said the naive path would visibly fail there. Considered: leaving it for M1-20 to discover, which leaves a known-false sentence in a task document -- the thing [`../STATUS.md`](../STATUS.md) exists to prevent. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 121 | The four small additions M1-11 needed outside its own files | **All four in, each at the smallest scope that works.** (1) `isUnitQuaternion(const Quat&, Tolerance)` and `kUnitQuaternionTolerance` join `core/Math.hpp` beside `isRotation` and `kRotationTolerance`, which is where a quaternion predicate belongs and where a test can reach it -- the argument `isRotation` and `isAffine` already make for being public. (2) `bitsOf(f32)` joins `core/Scalar.hpp` as an overload of the `f64` one, so the project's single spelling for bit identity works on the one type it keeps in 32 bits; without it `bitsOf(someFloat)` promotes and draws `-Wdouble-promotion`, which is an error here. (3) `bitIdentical(const Vec3f&, const Vec3f&)` is a free function in `view/Camera.hpp`, carrying the one new `NOLINT` in this task -- `bugprone-easily-swappable-parameters` on a provably symmetric pair, which is decision 96's sanctioned form and the wording `Mat4.hpp`'s own `bitIdentical` uses. (4) `toRenderSpace` asserts that the camera-relative offset is inside 32-bit range, through a public `detail::isNarrowable`: converting a `double` past `f32`'s maximum is **undefined behaviour** by [conv.double], not merely inexact, and the `linux-sanitize` preset makes `float-cast-overflow` a fatal test failure. It takes an offset beyond 3.4e38 m to reach, which is why it is asserted rather than reported (ADR 0002). Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 122 | The unguarded perspective divide, and the worse hazard beside it | **The zero is asserted; the sign gets a name of its own.** `STATUS.md` had carried this finding since 2026-09-21: `perspectiveDivide` had no guard on its denominator, so a point on the camera plane "returns infinity and a not-a-number silently" -- the third option ADR 0002 separates from reporting and asserting. **Measured before fixing it, and the finding turned out to be the smaller half.** A zero w is loud: infinity, or a not-a-number where the numerator is zero too. A **negative** w is the quiet one, and it is the one that matters: under this project's clip convention w is the distance in front of the camera, so a negative w is a point behind it, and dividing by a negative number flips both signs -- the point lands back **on screen, mirrored**. A point 10 m behind the camera at view-space (-0.5, -0.4) produces x and y **bit-identical** to one 10 m in front at (+0.5, +0.4). Not close: identical. Only the depth differs, coming out negative and so outside [0, 1], which a caller has to know to look for. That is the "finite, plausible, wrong" shape ADR 0022 exists to remove, found in the render maths. **Two answers, because they are two different faults.** The zero is a precondition of `perspectiveDivide` and is asserted -- `absOf(w) > 0.0`, which is the spelling `-Wfloat-equal` allows and which rejects both signed zeros and a NaN -- because a Vec4 with w = 0 is not an affine point at all but a point at infinity, and because a graphics card never reaches that state, clipping first, so a processor-side caller that has not clipped is the thing that is wrong. The sign is **not** folded in, because homogeneous coordinates are scale-invariant and a negative w is legitimate for a Vec4 in general; it means something only in clip space. So it becomes `isInFrontOfCamera(ClipPoint)`, on the clip-space type and nowhere else, with the measurement in its comment and a regression case in `tests/test_projection.cpp` that asserts the bit-identical mirror rather than describing it. Nothing calls it in `src/` yet -- nothing clips on the processor -- and it is here so the first thing that does has a name to reach for rather than a sign test somebody has to think of. Considered: reporting through `std::expected`, which is the heavier answer and which the divide, as the most-used operation in any render path, is the wrong place for; and refusing a negative w inside `perspectiveDivide`, which would be mathematically wrong for a general projective point. Ruled 2026-09-22 |
| 123 | Where `Texels` and `Mebibytes` are declared | **`core/Scalar.hpp`, beside `Count` and `Tolerance`.** `Tolerance` is the precedent exactly: the one type built on the *other* base lives with that base. Considered: `core/Units.hpp`, which states its own invariant -- every type in it is an mp-units quantity and "adding a unit is adding a name and a reference" -- which a count is not; and `src/view/`, which keeps renderer words out of `core` but separates `Count` from both its users and would need a second home the first time a count has nothing to do with drawing. One is already waiting: `render/VulkanContext.hpp`'s `BufferRequest` says a strong `Bytes` type "would be the other answer" and is deliberately not taken there, naming this decision as the reason. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 124 | What a count's arithmetic does instead of wrapping round | **A call, on the wrapping branch, to a function that is deliberately not `constexpr`.** An expression that reaches such a call is not a constant expression, so `Texels{1} - Texels{2}` fails the build and names the function -- in **every** tree, because that is a rule of the language rather than a property of `NDEBUG`. **Measured before it was chosen, and the measurement is why the obvious answer was rejected.** `ORBSIM_EXPECTS` alone refuses the underflow only in the Debug tree: under `NDEBUG` `assert` expands to nothing and the underflow becomes a valid constant expression worth 4,294,967,295. A guard that quietly stops guarding in the shipped configuration looks exactly like one that works, which is `VERIFICATION.md` rule 23. Confirmed on **three implementations in two configurations each** -- clang 23.1, gcc-14 14.3.0 and MSVC 14.51, at `-O2 -DNDEBUG` and at `-O0` -- with a **positive control** proving the check can also answer yes, since "not a constant expression" and "the proof is broken" are otherwise the same answer. **Cost measured at nil**: under `NDEBUG` the body is empty, so it inlines away, the branch dies with it, and a guarded subtraction emits `mov`, `sub`, `ret` -- the three instructions an unguarded one emits. Considered: `ORBSIM_EXPECTS` alone, rejected above; an always-live check that aborts, which holds everywhere but costs 8 instructions against 3 and lets ordinary arithmetic kill a shipped process; and an explicit `std::is_constant_evaluated()` guard beside the assertion, which measures identically to the chosen form and is one line longer. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 125 | Which of a count's operations are guarded | **All three.** The task document named only wraparound at zero, but `Texels{3'000'000'000} + Texels{2'000'000'000}` and `Texels{kCountMaximum} * 2U` wrap just as quietly, and the guard costs nothing. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 126 | Whether `Pixels` validates itself | **Not yet, and it becomes a validated class once a caller says which bound it wants.** The task document said "on its own mp-units *kind*, like `Eccentricity`", which had two readings after 2026-09-22: `Eccentricity` stopped being a kind that day and became a class holding its own bound (decision 107). The mechanism follows the document's own words -- a kind, which is mp-units' term for a dimensionless quantity no other ratio converts into. The bound does not follow yet, because there is no settled bound to hold: a subdivision threshold wants "finite and above zero", a screen-space *distance* wants "not negative", and a pixel *coordinate* -- which decision 105 leaves to M1-80 -- wants any sign at all. Choosing now would be choosing for a caller that does not exist; M1-50 or M1-59 is where it becomes ADR 0022's shape. The reasoning is in the header beside the type. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 127 | How the quality value reaches the frame | **A `FrameContext` field the caller fills**, rather than an argument to `beginFrame`. The consequence is named rather than left implicit, in the field's comment: nothing forces a future frame path to fill it, so a forgotten assignment is a wrong image rather than a compile error, where the argument form would have had the compiler demand it. M1-13 brings the draw calls and is where it becomes a parameter if it should. Considered: `beginFrame(RenderQuality)` storing it, which is the safer shape; and a separate draw entry point, which has nothing to draw yet. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 128 | Whether the application gets a `--quality` argument | **No; it names a preset in code, with a comment saying why.** A command-line switch is an actual setting, which the task puts out of scope, and all four presets are the same value today, so it would be a control with no effect. M1-16's probe mode is the first task that needs a preset by name. Which preset the application names is **not** an answer to ADR 0007's open question about the default at first run, and the comment says so. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 129 | Where M1-12's tests live | **A new suite, `tests/test_render_quality.cpp`**, linking `orbsim_view`, rather than extending `tests/test_view_math.cpp`. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 130 | What M1-12 records | **Dated notes on [ADR 0001](../adr/0001-units-in-the-type-system.md) and [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-render-quality-is-a-struct.md), and these rows. No new record.** Both ADRs carry a 2026-09-08 update whose *reason* stands and whose *mechanism* stopped being true on 2026-09-17; the notes correct the mechanism rather than the reasoning, which is how every amendment in this project is written. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 131 | `Count`'s comparison surface, and the type of a scale | **`==` is provided and there is no `bitIdentical`; a scale is exactly `std::uint32_t`.** On a double, exact equality is what `CODING_GUIDELINES` section 11 forbids and `-Wfloat-equal` reports, and `bitIdentical` says the other thing by name; on a whole number the two are one claim, so a second spelling would be two names for one thing. The scale is constrained to the exact type rather than merely declared as it, because a parameter would accept an `f64` through an implicit conversion and `Texels{2} * 1.5` would compile and mean `* 1`. A call site therefore writes `* 2U`. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 132 | `core/Scalar.hpp`'s claim to depend on nothing but the standard library | **Include `core/Contract.hpp` and correct the sentence.** The guard needs `ORBSIM_EXPECTS`. Considered: calling `assert` directly, which keeps the sentence true and loses the house spelling. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 133 | A named ratio of two counts | **Not yet.** Decision 19 says a ratio of counts is a named function returning `f64`; nothing wants one before M1-59 at the earliest, and writing it now is the speculative generality `CODING_GUIDELINES` section 17 warns about. A comment beside `Count` says where it goes. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 134 | A `_px` literal suffix | **In**, reversing the recommendation put up with it. The argument against was the same as decision 133's -- no call site until M1-50 -- and the argument for is that the other four suffixes exist and a screen-space threshold is written often enough in the render-side maths to want the fifth. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 135 | The stale `Pixels` sentence in M1-59's document | **Left for M1-59 to fix, deliberately, and the decision is recorded here so that leaving it is a choice rather than an oversight.** `m1-59-terrain-quality.md` says `screenSpaceError` is "a real quantity, on the f64 base", which was decision 19 as written on 2026-09-08 and stopped being the mechanism on 2026-09-17 when [ADR 0019](../adr/0019-vectors-carry-their-unit.md) moved every type in `core/Units.hpp` onto mp-units. The *reason* in that sentence is still right and is the reason `Pixels` is not a `Count`. Considered: amending it in place with a dated note, which is what decision 120 did for M1-20's wrong sentence the day before -- ruled the other way here because M1-20's sentence was a false *measurement* that would have sent a later task chasing a fault that was not there, while this one is a mechanism that the task will meet in the header the moment it opens it. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 136 | Whether the run-time half of the count guard gets a test | **Yes: a probe of its own that must abort.** M1-12's mutation pass declared a survivor -- nothing in the project provokes a wraparound at run time and watches it fail, so the ORBSIM_EXPECTS beside each guard was unexercised and could be deleted without any test noticing. Catch2 cannot survive an abort, so the probe is a small program of its own, run by a CMake script that fails if it returns normally, and **reported skipped rather than passed in a tree where assertions are not live** -- the pattern `horizons_fixture_checksums` already uses for a fixture that has not been generated. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 137 | What separates a pixel from every other ratio | **A dimension of its own, not a kind** -- reversing the mechanism decision 126 chose that morning, on a measurement taken that afternoon. **A kind does not do what it was chosen to do.** It restricts *implicit* conversion and, by design, permits explicit construction: `explicitly_convertible(dimensionless, kPixelKind)` is **true**, measured on clang 23.1, gcc-14 and MSVC 14.51 alike, so `Pixels{someRatio}` was legitimate mp-units. It *appeared* to be refused under clang and MSVC, and **that appearance was an accident of this project's own code**: `Scalar<>` deletes a conversion operator for every target type, which the three front ends resolve differently for class targets, and `linux-gcc` was what said so. Traced to a fourteen-line reproduction with no library in it. **The fix is the mechanism mp-units itself uses for the angle** -- formally dimensionless too, and given `dim_angle` regardless -- which this project already depends on, since it is why an angle cannot be built from a ratio. `units::kPixelDimension`, `kScreenLength` and `kPixel` replace `PixelKind`, and every claim then holds **by construction on all three front ends**. Considered and rejected: asserting only the implicit-conversion claim, which is true but says less than the type should; narrowing the deleted operator, which is a real fix for a real latent defect but makes `Pixels{ratio}` legal *everywhere*, so it does not settle this; and making `Pixels` a validated class, which works but forces a bound nothing has asked for yet, gives up the unit algebra that type-checks M1-50's screen-space error, and deletes the only thing currently detecting the operator defect. **What this does not fix is that operator**, which is recorded as an open finding rather than folded in: it changes all nine types and belongs in a task of its own. It is now far less exposed, because after this change no *named* type in `core/Units.hpp` is dimensionless. Ruled 2026-09-23 |
| 138 | `Scalar<>`'s deleted conversion operator, which M1-12 found and left | **Narrowed to arithmetic targets**, closing the finding M1-12 recorded rather than fixed. The operator exists to stop a dimensionless quantity decaying to a bare number, and it was deleted for **every** target type -- which also puts a deleted candidate into overload resolution for **class** targets, where the three front ends disagree: gcc-14 accepted what clang 23.1 and MSVC 14.51 rejected, reduced to fourteen lines with no library in them. That divergence had already produced one wrong answer, M1-12's first `Pixels` assertion, where the agreement of three toolchains was the *incorrect* result. `std::is_arithmetic_v<V>` is exactly the set mp-units' own operator serves and exactly the set this one refuses, so the narrowing gives up nothing: measured before and after on three implementations -- `f64{ratio}` still refused, the trait still agreeing with the compiler in every row, `SpecificEnergy{m2/s2}` still converting -- and the front ends now agree, which they did not. Five assertions pin what the operator is for, so losing it or narrowing it further is a build failure. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 139 | Whether `mutate.py` can judge a test whose success is a non-zero exit | **Yes: a mutant may name a CTest entry.** The count-wraparound probes abort on purpose, so `run_mutant` -- which reads any non-zero exit as a kill -- would have called every mutant caught. A `"ctest"` field runs `ctest -R` instead, and the CMake script behind the entry already knows which way round the probe's exit means, including reporting SKIPPED where assertions are compiled out. A `"targets"` field builds what the entry needs. The three run-time guard mutants stop being declared survivors and become ordinary kills, which is what they always were in substance. A skip still is not a kill: `ctest` exits zero on it, so those mutants survive a release-tree pass, which is honest rather than convenient. Ruled 2026-09-24 |

| 140 | A `Bytes` type, and what `Count` has to become to carry one | **One `Bytes`, 64-bit, on a `Count` parameterised by its representation, with the two operations `Count` forbids given names.** Ruled before M1-13 because M1-13 is where a byte count first becomes real: it implements the push-constant range arithmetic, and `VkPushConstantRange` is two adjacent `uint32_t` byte counts, `offset` and `size`, which transpose silently -- non-negotiable 1's exact case. **Sixty-four bits, not thirty-two**: `VkDeviceSize` is a `uint64_t` and KTX2 carries 64-bit offsets and lengths which M1-26 parses **from an untrusted file**, where a silent truncation is the memory-safety defect rather than an inconvenience. Vulkan's 32-bit fields become a boundary that narrows in a named function, which is the discipline `toRenderSpace` already sets. **`Count<Derived, Rep = std::uint32_t>`**, so `Texels` and `Mebibytes` are untouched at their declarations, the limit moves from one namespace constant to `Count::kMaximum` per representation, and a scale may be any unsigned integer no wider than the representation. **`alignedUpTo` and `howManyFit` are members**, not free functions: two adjacent parameters of one type are what non-negotiable 1 forbids, and `offset.alignedUpTo(step)` cannot be written backwards. Considered and rejected: widening `Count` for everything, which would put a narrowing cast at every Vulkan texture dimension to serve one type's need; and a separate `Bytes` class beside `Count`, which is two count mechanisms in one codebase. **Two things the work found.** `alignedUpTo` first used a modulo, and `clang-analyzer-core.DivideZero` was right that under NDEBUG an alignment of zero reaches it -- undefined behaviour, where the wraparound guard only ever prevented a *defined* wrong answer. It uses masks now and does not divide at all. And the mutation pass caught that no run-time case can require an alignment to be a power of two, because the suite can only pass ones that are; four compile-time refusals close it. Ruled 2026-09-24 |

| 141 | What `Bytes::howManyFit` does with a block size of zero | **It reports**, through `std::expected<std::uint64_t, CountError>`, where the day before it returned zero. The returned zero was the third option [ADR 0002](../adr/0002-error-handling-strategy.md) separates from reporting and asserting, and it was there because the alternatives looked worse: an assertion vanishes under `NDEBUG` and would leave a division by zero, which `clang-analyzer-core.DivideZero` correctly called undefined behaviour -- strictly worse than a wrong number. Reporting is the answer that is neither. **Two things come free.** Unwrapping a failed expected is not a constant expression, so `Bytes{100}.howManyFit(Bytes{0}).value()` fails the build exactly as `eccentricity(-0.5)` does; and the assertion that it refuses *by name* can be written, which a returned zero could not express. `CountError` lives in `core/Scalar.hpp` rather than beside `UnitError`, on decision 90's precedent that an error belongs to the module that produces it -- and `core/Units.hpp` is above this header, so reaching for a `UnitError` would invert the include order. *(It has one since 2026-09-25, decision 186.)* **It has no `describe()` yet**, which is the one place this departs from ADR 0002's shape: a `switch` over a single enumerator is what `readability-trivial-switch` reports, and decision 91 ruled that exact case for `describe(EphemerisError)` with a suppression at the site -- which is the owner's to grant and was not asked for. Nothing is lost while the enum has one value, and `STATUS.md` carries it so it is not left to memory. `alignedUpTo` is deliberately **not** changed: its bad path is defined, since it masks rather than divides. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 142 | Where a pipeline's inputs are described, and whether that needs an ADR | **In `orbsim_view`, in a vocabulary of its own, and src/render translates it field for field.** M1-13's task document asks for the vertex input description and the push-constant arithmetic to be tested in `orbsim_view`, which may never see a Vulkan header (ADR 0012), so `view/VertexLayout.hpp` and `view/PushConstants.hpp` carry their own `AttributeFormat`, `ShaderLocation`, `ShaderStages` and byte counts, and `render/Pipeline.cpp` copies them into `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription` and `VkPushConstantRange`. **No new ADR**: ADR 0012 already decides where the boundary lies, and this applies it. Considered: a record of its own. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 143 | Which pipelines M1-13 creates, and so which vertex types it defines | **Both pairs of shaders, at start-up, and M1-13 defines the two vertex types they read**: `LineVertex` (a `Vec3f` and an `Rgba`, 28 bytes) and `BodyVertex` (a `Vec3f`). The shaders fix both layouts already, so defining them is reading rather than guessing; M1-19 uses `LineVertex` rather than defining its own. Considered: the line pipeline only, which leaves `body.*` unloaded and the smoke test covering one shape. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 144 | How a vertex layout is built and checked | **Computed from its formats in order, and checked against the compiler.** The n-th attribute gets location n and the sum of the widths before it as its offset, so neither can be typed wrong; `static_assert`s then compare the stride and each offset with `sizeof` and `offsetof` of the C++ struct -- the compiler's layout, formed independently of this code (`VERIFICATION.md` rule 2). `AttributeFormat`'s value is its number of float components, so a width is a multiplication rather than a table. `attributes()` returns by value: a reference would want `[[clang::lifetimebound]]`, whose portable macro lives in src/render. The numbers the tests expect were read from the compiled shaders with `spirv-cross --reflect`. Decided in the course of the work, within decision 142 |
| 145 | The limit a push-constant range is checked against, and the body's range | **128 bytes, the guaranteed minimum of `maxPushConstantsSize` in core Vulkan** ("128 ({core})" in the specification's Required Limits table; 256 from 1.4, and this project targets 1.3), rather than the device's own figure -- both of this machine's GPUs report 256, and every block here is 96 or less. **One range for the body, vertex and fragment together, bytes 0-96**, because the two shaders declare one block and the specification forbids two ranges naming the same stage (VUID-00292). The five rules are the specification's own valid-usage statements for `VkPushConstantRange`, read from its source; the order a range is refused in -- empty, then out of bounds, then misaligned -- is written beside the enum. The bound is never formed as a sum, so a range whose end would wrap 64 bits is refused rather than accepted as small. Considered: asking the device, and two ranges split at byte 64. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 146 | Where the push-constant sizes 80 and 96 come from | **Two C++ structs mirroring the GLSL blocks**, `LinePushConstants` and `BodyPushConstants`, with `static_assert`s pinning their size and member offsets to the reflected values; the ranges are built from `sizeof` of them, and M1-19 and M1-20 fill them. Considered: two named constants citing the shaders, which nothing checks. Left to the assistant by the owner, 2026-09-24 |
| 147 | What `GraphicsPipeline::create` returns | **A `GraphicsPipeline` owning the layout and the pipeline together**, where the task document said `std::expected<UniquePipeline, RenderError>`. The push-constant ranges belong to the pipeline layout, which every draw needs to send its push constants through, so returning the pipeline alone would destroy the layout on return. The description still carries the ranges, as the task said. Considered: two functions, with the description carrying a layout handle instead of the ranges. Raised after the first round of questions, because it was missed in it. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 148 | How "a missing SPIR-V file is reported by path" is tested, when `loadShaderModule` needs a device | **A `--shader-dir <path>` argument and a CTest entry, `shader_missing_is_reported`**, labelled `gpu`, which runs the application against a directory that does not exist and requires exit code 1 and the missing file's name on stderr -- both, through `cmake/VerifyMissingShader.cmake`, because `WILL_FAIL` alone passes on any failure and a regular expression alone ignores the exit code. The argument is read as UTF-8, which is what SDL hands `main` on every platform. Considered: a hidden self-test flag. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 149 | `-Wmissing-designated-field-initializers` in `render/Pipeline.cpp`, and where the depth constants live | **Off for that file**, with the reason at the site, as `VulkanContext.cpp` has it: the file fills Vulkan's C structs, whose unused fields are zero by the specification's own convention. **`kDepthClear` and the new `kDepthCompareOp` move into `VulkanContext.hpp` beside `kDepthFormat`**: the three are one convention (ADR 0003), and the comparison is not a field of the pipeline description, so the only way to get it wrong is to edit that line. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 150 | Whether `beginFrame` takes the quality value (decision 127's open half) | **Yes: `beginFrame(RenderQuality)`, which copies it into the frame.** A frame can no longer begin without somebody saying at what quality, and the compiler is what asks, where the caller's assignment could be forgotten and draw at the default. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 151 | Deleting `projectionOf` | **Deleted, with its test case, as decision 117 ruled.** The camera's field of view and near plane are carried unread again until M1-19, which is the first task that projects something; that gap is known and accepted. M1-11's mutant of the deleted function is removed from `scripts/mutants/m1-11.json`, with a dated note saying why, because a mutant of code that no longer exists cannot run -- `mutant-anchors` reported it. Considered: keeping it until M1-19, which would amend decision 117. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 152 | Who owns the scene's pipelines | **`ScenePipelines` in `src/render/`**, built by a factory after the context and held by the application's frame loop, rather than new members on `VulkanContext`. Its constructor takes the two pipelines by name, because both are `GraphicsPipeline` and reversed they would draw lines with the body shaders. The body pipeline does not cull until a mesh exists to say which way its triangles wind. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 153 | `CountError`'s missing `describe()` | **Left open**, as STATUS records it: M1-13 does not use `howManyFit`, the one operation that reports a `CountError`. Left to the assistant by the owner, 2026-09-24. *Closed by decision 186.* |
| 154 | Two vertical flips, found while M1-13 was written | **The viewport stops flipping; the projection matrix keeps the only flip.** `VulkanContext::beginRendering` set a negative viewport height "so that +Y is up in clip space", while `view/Projection.hpp` negates the y entry of the projection for the same purpose and states that the flip lives there and nowhere else. Together they cancel into an upside-down image, which nothing showed because nothing was drawn; M1-19's lines probe would have been the first frame to show it. The matrix is the half that is documented and tested (`tests/test_projection.cpp`), so the viewport is the half that goes. Considered: removing the matrix flip instead, which would move the convention out of the one header that states it. Ruled 2026-09-24, and done in its own commit |
| 155 | How the application stops a failed assertion opening a dialog | **`_set_abort_behavior` in `main()`, with `#include <stdlib.h>` and a `NOLINTNEXTLINE(modernize-deprecated-headers)` on that one line.** M1-13's mutation pass hung on a mutant whose assertion fired inside the application: the Windows debug runtime opened a modal dialog, and `orbsim_smoke` waited for a click. The call is the one every suite already makes. It is declared in the Windows runtime's `<stdlib.h>` itself, which `misc-include-cleaner` requires and `modernize-deprecated-headers` forbids, so one of the two had to be silenced; the include names the header the function truly lives in, and the suppression covers one line. `_CrtSetReportMode` and `_set_error_mode` were tried on 2026-09-24 and do not stop the dialog. Considered: `NOLINTNEXTLINE(misc-include-cleaner)` on the call instead, and no call at all. Ruled by the owner 2026-09-24 |
| 156 | Test sources that were never linted | **`tests/AbortBehaviour.cpp` and the three `count_wraparound_*.cpp` probes join the lint list.** The list named each suite's own file only, so these four -- and `tests/CountWraparound.hpp`, which only the probes include -- had never been linted; decision 155's conflict was found in `main.cpp` for exactly that reason. Their findings were fixed in the code: `printf` became `std::println`, every ignored C result is discarded by name, `main` no longer names an `argv` it does not use, and both abort-setting files carry decision 155's one-line suppression. **`std::println` can throw where `printf` could not**, so the probe catches inside its `noexcept` function and returns an ordinary failure, and `VerifyCountWraparound.cmake` now accepts an abort only if stderr carries the assertion's own "precondition violated" -- which names why the process died rather than only that it did. Shown to refuse an abnormal exit without that message before it was trusted. Ruled by the owner 2026-09-24 |
| 157 | Who owns M1-14's resolve pass | **A `ResolvePass` class in `src/render/`, created by the application after `ScenePipelines` and handed to `endFrame`**; the HDR target itself stays in `VulkanContext`, because it lives and dies with the swapchain. Matches decision 152, which kept pipelines out of the context, and needs no change to M1-13's headers. Considered: everything in `VulkanContext`, which makes `Pipeline.hpp` and `VulkanContext.hpp` include each other and needs `RenderError` and the depth constants moved to a new header. Amends the task document's "in `VulkanContext.cpp`". Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 158 | How the resolve pass reads the HDR target | **`texelFetch` at `gl_FragCoord`, from a `SAMPLED_IMAGE` descriptor, one descriptor set per frame in flight rewritten every frame.** No sampler, no filtering, no texture coordinates -- so nothing can flip or shift the image, the defect class decision 154 found once. It brings the project's first descriptor set forward from M1-31. Rewriting the slot's set each frame picks up a resized target without being told; measured at 0.25 us of CPU per frame, median of three runs. Considered: a combined image sampler with coordinates; one set rewritten on resize. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 159 | One HDR target, or one per frame in flight | **One**, as the depth image is. The barriers order the frames on the one queue, so a second image would buy only overlap those barriers already give away, for a second 11.5 MB at 1600x900. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 160 | Synchronization validation | **On in code wherever the validation layers are**, so `orbsim_smoke` checks every barrier on every `check`, rather than on the one manual `vkconfig` run the task asked for. Measured before it was trusted: a barrier planted to wait for nothing fails the run with exit 3 and "WRITE_AFTER_WRITE hazard detected", and the same fault **with synchronization validation off passes silently, exit 0** -- the base layers cannot see it, as the task said. Cost: nothing measurable on a 2 s validated run -- -53 ms against a run-to-run spread of 88 ms, three runs each, `docs/measurements/m1-14-frame-cost.md`; *(corrected 2026-09-24: a single-run spike had suggested about +0.1 s, and that figure went to the owner before the repeated measurement)* -- and nothing at all without `--validate`. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 161 | The swapchain format, on hardware this machine is not | **An ordered list of four UNORM formats in the sRGB colour space -- B8G8R8A8, R8G8B8A8, A2B10G10R10, A2R10G10B10 -- and a refusal by name of anything else**, checked against Vulkan-Utility-Libraries' format table. Found while reading vk-bootstrap v1.3.302: when no desired format is offered it takes the driver's first (`find_best_surface_format`), which may be `_SRGB` and would encode a second time with no error. The owner asked on 2026-09-24 that the renderer be flexible about what hardware supports; the list is that flexibility, the refusal is what keeps it from being wrong. Both of this machine's GPUs list B8G8R8A8_UNORM first, so the refusal cannot be reached here (decision 166). Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 162 | The HDR format, and what the scene pipelines read | **`kHdrFormat = R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT`, a named constant beside the depth constants, with no fallback format, and `colorFormat()` renamed `swapchainFormat()`.** Its two uses are mandatory in the Vulkan specification and listed in every Khronos desktop baseline profile 2022-2026 and Android's 2021 baseline (checked against the SDK's profile files), so a fallback would be code no conformant device reaches; the device is still asked and a missing feature reported by name. The rename makes every old use fail to compile, because Vulkan compares a pipeline's formats with the attachments only when it draws. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 163 | The scene's clear colour in the HDR target | **The old display colour decoded to linear light**, `view/SceneClear.hpp`, so the frame shows the same 8-bit codes (1, 2, 3) it did -- measured; stored undecoded it would show (13, 18, 29). f32 literals held to the decode by a test, so `src/` gains no narrowing cast. **It is a tuning constant under ADR 0014, and M1-15 must settle it**; M1-15's document now says so. Considered: black, and the numbers unchanged. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 164 | The CPU sRGB code | **`view/Srgb.hpp`: double precision, two types -- `LinearValue` and `EncodedValue` -- so encoding twice does not compile, inputs clamped to [0, 1] as the GPU clamps them, a NaN asserted.** `shaders/tonemap.frag` clamps too, because GLSL leaves `pow` of a negative number undefined. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 165 | The sRGB tolerances | **8 ulp against 50-digit references, twice the measured 4; a round trip of 1e-15, twice the measured 4.4e-16 -- and the standard's own exception asserted rather than assumed away.** The plan's 1e-6 would have accepted almost anything. The encode and the decode were recommended at "1e-15 everywhere" on a sweep too coarse to see the knee; the finer measurement taken before the test was written found a window 7.28e-9 wide just above 0.0031308 where the round trip is out by up to 2.33e-9, because the standard's rounded constants make the encode step down by 2.85e-8 there and send the value to the other decode branch. The test asserts the budget outside the window, the window's position and width, and its error bounded by the standard's decode jump. `encode(1)` is 0.9999999999999999 in doubles and is asserted to within one ulp. Ruled 2026-09-24, the window added the same day as a measurement within that ruling |
| 166 | Gaps M1-14 leaves, and who closes them | **Declared survivors in `scripts/mutants/m1-14.json`.** Accepted by the owner in advance: the shader's encode, which nothing reads back before M1-16 and M1-18 compares to 1/255; and the two refusals no device here can reach. **Found by the mutation pass and raised with the task's report, not yet ruled on**: scene pipelines built for the swapchain's format, a resolve pass never drawn, and a full-screen triangle a quarter the size -- each a valid program that draws the wrong thing, with the same cause. M1-16's probe frames and M1-19's first draw are what kill them. Ruled 2026-09-24 for the first two; the third accepted on 2026-09-25, decision 171 |
| 167 | Three details of the resolve pass | **The display image is cleared to black before the triangle covers it** -- measured at 0.29 us, and a faulty triangle then leaves the same black every frame rather than stale pixels; **the HDR target gains `TRANSFER_SRC` with M1-16**, which needs it and not before; and **the resolve pass is created after the scene pipelines**, so `shader_missing_is_reported` still sees `line.vert.spv` named first. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 168 | Other compilers for M1-14 | **`linux-gcc` and `windows-msvc` now, not only at M1-23's gate**, because M1-14 adds code to `src/view/` and gcc has caught three problems there before. Ruled 2026-09-24 |
| 169 | Recording what a task costs so another machine can compare | **`scripts/measure-frame-cost.py` and a spec per task, `scripts/measurements/m1-14.json`**, asked for by the owner on 2026-09-24: "I want to be able to run the same test-suite on a different machine and be able to compare the results." The spec names two commits and the temporary timing edits for each; the script builds each in a git worktree under `build/measure/` -- never in the working tree -- runs every variant round-robin, and writes the machine, the commits, every run and the medians to `docs/measurements/m1-14/`. `docs/measurements/m1-14-frame-cost.md` describes each test. **Not a test and not in `check`**; M1-22's benchmark mode remains the permanent instrument. Decided in the course of the work, within the owner's request |
| 170 | What a failed renderer start-up does in an automated run | **No dialog when `--seconds` is given; stderr and exit code 1 only.** M1-14's mutation pass found that `runRenderer` opened `SDL_ShowSimpleMessageBox` on a failed start-up, which waits for a click, so `orbsim_smoke` hung until CTest's timeout instead of failing -- older than M1-14, and never reached before. Every automated run passes `--seconds`; a person starting the application does not, and still gets the dialog. Considered: never showing it, which loses the message for someone who started the application without a terminal; and a separate `--no-dialog` flag, which every automated caller would have to remember. With it, the two start-up mutants are caught and M1-14's pass is 12 caught, 6 declared survivors, none hung. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 171 | The three survivors M1-14's pass found | **Accepted, as declared in `scripts/mutants/m1-14.json`**: scene pipelines built for the swapchain's format, a resolve pass never drawn, and a full-screen triangle a quarter the size. Same cause as the gaps accepted in advance -- nothing reads a pixel back before M1-16 -- and M1-16's probe frames and M1-19's first draw kill them. Closes decision 166's open third. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 172 | M1-13's header, which gcc-14 and MSVC reject | **Fixed now, in its own commit, rather than at M1-23's gate.** Missing brace pairs in `view/VertexLayout.hpp`, `render/Pipeline.cpp` and `tests/test_pipeline_inputs.cpp`, and `noexcept` on the fold's lambda; under MSVC the application did not build at all. Measured before it was proposed and again after: MSVC 225 of 225 with no warning, gcc-14 223 of 223. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 173 | How AgX's output becomes display values (M1-15) | **AgX, then undo its 2.2 curve, then the IEC 61966-2-1 encode** -- what Filament and Wrensch's own code do, and what ADR 0014 already says. The curve's output is encoded for a display that decodes with a 2.2 power ("AgX Base" in Sobotka's configuration, confirmed by Wrensch in his repository's discussion #12), so it is raised to 2.2 on the GPU and the CPU, and `view/Srgb.hpp`'s encode follows unchanged. Measured before the ruling: deep shadows come out up to **9 of 255 display steps darker** than sending the curve's output straight to a 2.2 display (at 5 stops below mid-grey, 7 instead of 16), and identical from mid-grey up. Considered: the curve's output straight to the display, as Sobotka's configuration does, which would have amended ADR 0014's wording; Blender's variant, which undoes a 2.4 curve. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 174 | How faithful to the minimal AgX (M1-15) | **Wrensch's constants, 6th-order curve and step order exactly, no "look", and three guards where GLSL leaves the reference undefined**: negative input clamped to zero before the inset (as Sobotka's configuration does), each channel raised to at least 2^minEv before log2, the output clamped to [0, 1] before the 2.2 power. The polynomial is evaluated by Horner's rule rather than as a sum of powers -- same coefficients, fewer roundings; a form, not a value. Measured against Sobotka's own curve (`AgX_Default_Contrast.spi1d`) before the ruling: the 6th-order fit is strictly increasing on [0, 1] and at worst 5.8e-3 away; the 7th-order fit Filament uses is **not** increasing everywhere. Sobotka's table itself cannot be shipped: his repository carries no licence. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 175 | The radiometric-to-photometric conversion (M1-15) | **Sunlight's own luminous efficacy, 98.9225 lm/W, with Rec. 709 luminance weights -- replacing ADR 0014's 179 lm/W.** Radiance's source defines 179 as the efficacy of "equal energy white 380-780nm": watts counted over the visible band only, while this renderer's radiance comes from the *total* solar irradiance, so 179 would have made every sunlit luminance 1.81 times too bright, a hidden 0.86-stop error. The value is the SI definition applied to the Sun's spectrum, as pbrt-v4 and Bruneton's atmosphere derive photometric quantities: 683 lm/W times CIE 1924 V(lambda) over the TSIS-1 Hybrid Solar Reference Spectrum, 134 633.5 lx at 1 AU, over the 1361 W/m^2 of `astro/Sun.hpp` -- `scripts/solar-efficacy.py`, worst-case uncertainty 0.30 %. **The choice of TSIS-1 over ASTM E-490 was the assistant's, within this ruling**, and is put to the owner with the task's report: E-490 gives 97.59 lm/W on its own total of 1366.1 W/m^2, 1.4 % lower; TSIS-1 is the newer, absolutely calibrated reference and sits on the same total irradiance as the constant it multiplies. M1-18's 129.97 W/(m^2 sr) is unchanged. Considered: 179 with visible-band radiance (Radiance's own meaning), which would have made M1-18's value about 71.0; and leaving both as written. Ruled 2026-09-25; amends ADR 0014 |
| 176 | The application's default exposure (M1-15) | **f/16, 1/125 s, ISO 100 -- the "sunny 16" rule**, the task's own worked example, as a named constant in `src/app/main.cpp` with that source. A sunlit surface of albedo 0.3 lands 0.89 stops above a metered mid-grey. The probes of M1-16 pin their own. ADR 0014 had left the default open. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 177 | How camera settings become the exposure factor (M1-15) | **ISO 12232's saturation-based sensitivity**, 1 / (1.2 * 2^EV100) with 1.2 = 78 / (100 * 0.65), as Lagarde & de Rousiers (2014) derive it and Filament implements it. The only convention of those surveyed that uses all three settings by a published standard. Considered: the light-meter calibration of ISO 2720 (K = 12.5, mid-grey to 0.18), about 0.8 stops brighter and with K a manufacturer's choice between 12.5 and 14; pbrt-v4's pass-through, which ignores the aperture. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 178 | The exposure types (M1-15) | **`Aperture`, `ShutterTime` and `Iso` validated at construction** -- finite and greater than zero, no other bound, refused by name through `ExposureError` (ADR 0022); **a typed `ExposureValue100`** and **typed factors** (`PerLuminance`, `PerRadiance`); and **`Radiance`, `Luminance` and `LuminousEfficacy` in `core/Units.hpp`**, so the compiler checks that a radiance times an efficacy is a luminance. The task document's bare `f64` signatures would have broken non-negotiable 1. Radiance is built on mp-units' *angular* steradian: over the dimensionless SI one, an irradiance converted explicitly into a radiance, measured compiling. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 179 | How the exposure reaches the GPU (M1-15) | **One 32-bit float in a fragment-stage push constant, narrowed in one named function, `view::toShaderExposure`** -- so `grep static_cast<f32> src/` now finds two narrowing functions where it found one, and `.claude/rules/cpp-style.md` says so. The exposure is fixed when the resolve pass is created: nothing in milestone 1 changes it at run time, and a probe wanting another creates the pass with another (the assistant's shape within this ruling). Considered: a uniform buffer, which still narrows and costs more code. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 180 | M1-15's planned tests, amended by measurement | **"0 maps to 0" kept, and every grey up to 2.17e-4 asserted exactly black too**; **"strictly increasing over 12 orders of magnitude" replaced** -- it cannot hold, since AgX's log range is 16.5 stops: grey is asserted strictly increasing over the 4.88 decades from 2.17e-4 to 16.3, never decreasing across twelve, and flat beyond; saturated colours are left out of the claim, because the other channels of a pure primary dip by up to 1e-5 as it brightens, which is recorded rather than asserted; **"exactly +1 / +2 stops" asserted to the task's own 1e-12**, because exact equality fails by one unit in the last place in 10-15 % of cases; and **a 50-digit reference script and a case that tells a matrix from its transpose** added. The stop-relation sweep is a fixed grid rather than a seeded random draw, which needs no lint suppression (the assistant's choice). Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 181 | The scene's clear colour (M1-15; closes decision 163) | **Zero radiance.** Anything dimmer than about 8 cd/m^2 renders as display code 0 at the default exposure, and a dark-sky background is some forty thousand times dimmer, so a sourced background would render identically. `view/SceneClear.hpp` holds zero, with a `static_assert` so that re-tuning it fails the build. **The `test_srgb` case that held the old colour was removed**, with the owner's go-ahead, and so was M1-14's mutant aimed at it, whose code no longer exists. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 182 | What checks the shader before M1-18 (M1-15) | **Its operations: nothing, declared in advance** -- the shader mutants that change an operation are declared survivors in `scripts/mutants/m1-15.json`, killed by M1-18's port check. **Its constants: `scripts/check-tonemap-constants.py`**, which reads GLSL's matrices column by column and compares every AgX constant with `view/Tonemap.hpp`'s exactly. It runs in `check` as the CTest test `tonemap_constants`, and its `--self-test` as `tonemap_constants_self_test`, which alters copies of the two files five ways and fails unless each is reported. CTest tests rather than a build target so that the mutation harness can name them as judges (the assistant's shape within this ruling). Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 183 | The AgX licence record (M1-15) | **Corrected to the evidence that could be verified**, in section 7 and in `THIRD_PARTY.md`, and **the MIT header on the blog page itself confirmed by the owner**, 2026-09-25 -- the project's machine could not reach it. The full MIT notice is in `shaders/tonemap.frag` and `src/view/Tonemap.hpp`, as MIT requires. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 184 | Other compilers and the frame cost for M1-15 | **`linux-gcc` and `windows-msvc` now, and `scripts/measure-frame-cost.py` for the added shader work**, as decisions 168 and 169 did for M1-14. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 185 | What M1-15 raised after its report | **All four answered by the owner on 2026-09-25.** The AgX licence confirmed on the blog page (decision 183). **The floor before `log2` accepted as a declared survivor**: equivalent on the CPU, where IEEE `log2(0)` is minus infinity and the clamp that follows restores it; the guard is for GLSL. **`ORBSIM_VIEW_SOURCES` added to the lint list**: `src/view/Camera.cpp` had never been through clang-tidy since M1-11 added it, and its first run found two findings, both one missing include of `view/Frame.hpp`, fixed in the code. **And the assistant's choices within the rulings accepted** -- TSIS-1 over ASTM E-490 (175), the exposure fixed when the resolve pass is created (179), the constants check as CTest tests (182), a grid for the stop relations (180), the clear colour pinned by a `static_assert` and M1-14's mutant removed (181), and two suites rather than one. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 186 | `CountError`'s `describe()` (decisions 141 and 153) | **Written now, as a one-case `switch` with no `default:` and `NOLINTNEXTLINE(readability-trivial-switch)` on that line** -- decision 91's shape for `describe(EphemerisError)`, so that adding a second enumerator is a -Wswitch error at the function rather than a silent "unknown". It was waiting for a second enumerator to make the switch non-trivial; the owner chose to write it now and granted the suppression by asking for it, 2026-09-25. A `static_assert` beside it holds that it says something. Ruled 2026-09-25 |
| 187 | How a probe gets a frame of exactly 1280x720 (M1-16) | **Its own 1280x720 images -- an HDR target, a depth image and display images -- built by the same functions and recorded by the same scene and resolve code as the window's frame**, submitted once and waited for on a fence, then copied to host-readable memory. The window's swapchain is created as usual and never presented. Considered: sizing the window to 1280x720 and reading back its swapchain image, which breaks under display scaling (many laptops run at 125-150 %) and is what the task's "regardless of window size" rules out. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 188 | `TRANSFER_SRC` on the window's HDR target (M1-16; decision 167) | **Yes: one image description for both paths**, so the probe's HDR target is the application's, as decision 167 expected. Considered: the flag on the probe's image only. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 189 | What the `clear` probe draws (M1-16) | **Four bands and an undrawn strip**: grey in rows 0-149, red 150-299, green 300-449, blue 450-599, nothing in 600-719, so the clear radiance (zero) shows. In column x each band's radiance is L_lo * 2^(16.5 (x + 0.5) / 1280), even in stops across exactly the range AgX displays at the pinned exposure -- 0.0682 to 6,324 W/(m^2 sr) -- derived from AgX's constants and the camera, not chosen by eye; a colour band carries its radiance in one channel only. Drawn with `fullscreen.vert` through `GraphicsPipeline::create`, depth-tested with the project's comparison. Chosen because a vertically asymmetric, coloured frame is what lets a person see a flipped image or swapped channels, two of the three things the task asks to be judged. Considered: one grey ramp, which looks the same flipped vertically. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 190 | The state `clear` pins (M1-16) | **Epoch J2000.0 TT (`kJ2000`); camera at the origin, identity orientation (looking down -z), 45-degree vertical field of view, 1 m near plane; quality preset `high`; exposure f/16, 1/125 s, ISO 100** -- the application's own preset and exposure. The preset's name is stored beside its value in the probe registry; `RenderQuality` is unchanged. `clear` draws with neither the epoch nor the camera, and records both. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 191 | What a probe writes (M1-16) | **Five files, on every run.** `<name>.hdr.f32`: an ASCII line `orbsim-hdr-f32 v1 1280 720`, then little-endian binary32 R G B A, rows top to bottom -- what M1-18's tests read. `<name>.png`: 8-bit RGB, the GPU's own 8-bit resolve, what M1-17's goldens compare. `<name>.16.png`: 16-bit RGB, **the same resolve drawn by the GPU a second time into a 16-bit UNORM image** (asked for by the owner; the device is asked for the format, which Khronos's desktop baseline profiles 2022-2026 require and the core specification does not). `<name>.exr`: the linear HDR target as half floats, an exact copy of the GPU's values, channels R G B, lossless ZIP, with the Rec. 709 / D65 chromaticities attribute and a comment giving the unit, W/(m^2 sr) (asked for by the owner). `<name>.txt`: `key = value` provenance -- probe name and description, epoch, camera pose, preset, the three camera settings and the exposure, GPU with vendor and device IDs, driver name, version text and raw number, Vulkan API version, build configuration, compiler, and **the run's date and time in UTC** (the build date is refused by `-Wdate-time` and makes builds unrepeatable). No git commit hash: it would need a build step on every build to stay true. Considered for the 16-bit file: rounding a 16-bit image to 8 bits on the CPU, and a 16-bit PNG of linear radiance, which a PNG cannot label. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 192 | The probe command line (M1-16) | **`--probe <name>`, `--probe-list` and `--probe-out <dir>`**, the last defaulting to `<build>/probes/`. `--probe-list` prints names and descriptions without a window or a device. `--probe` with `--seconds`, or an unknown name, is a usage error (exit 2). The window is created **hidden** in probe mode -- measured on 2026-09-26 that a swapchain builds on a hidden SDL window on this machine. `--validate` behaves as it does today and the CTest tests pass it. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 193 | The artefacts of a failing run (M1-16) | **Written before any verdict**: render, read back, write every file, tear down, count validation errors, exit -- and the test script checks the files whatever the exit code. The one failure a probe can have today, a validation error, is shown once by hand with a planted fault and recorded; M1-17's golden mismatch (exit 4) becomes the permanent test. **If rendering itself fails there is no frame, and the sidecar is written alone, carrying the error.** Considered: a hidden switch that forces a failure, which puts test code in the product. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 194 | The mutants handed to M1-16, and a numeric check of `clear` (M1-16) | **Both options.** (A) M1-16's own tests -- exit code, files present, two runs byte-identical -- cannot kill the survivors declared by M1-13, M1-14 and M1-15, each of which draws the same wrong frame every time; they are re-run at the end of the task and each still-surviving declaration is re-pointed at the task that kills it. (B) **A Catch2 test reads `clear.hdr.f32` and asserts the bands**, computing the expected radiance itself from the definitions -- the camera settings, 98.9225 lm/W, AgX's range -- and nothing in `src/view`. **Budget: every value read back is one of the two binary16 values either side of the exact one**, a relative 2^-10 (0.098 %): the Vulkan specification leaves the binary16 rounding mode undefined -- a value lands on one neighbour or the other *(corrected the same day: this said "at most half a step"; measured, this machine's GPU rounds toward zero, every value of a 1,280-pixel row landing on the lower neighbour, up to 9.4e-4 below -- inside the budget, which never assumed nearest)* -- and the GPU's `exp2` is within 3 + 2|x| ulp of binary32 by the Vulkan precision rules, about 3.4e-6 relative here. A half-pixel shift moves a value 0.45 %, so the columns are checked too. Also asserted: colour bands exactly zero in their other channels, the grey band's channels equal, the undrawn strip exactly zero, alpha one everywhere. **`fullscreen.vert` writes depth 0.5 instead of 0**, so the gradient passes the reverse-Z `GREATER` test and would fail `LESS`; the resolve pass does not test depth. This kills M1-13's depth-comparison survivor and M1-14's quarter-size triangle now. The pattern is M1-18's fixture, arriving one task early. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 195 | The `stb` pin (M1-16) | **Commit `2c980bb59875b0d32144a71867fbdebb2f77cd20` (2026-08-02)**: stb has no releases or tags (measured: none). **Its decoder, `stb_image` v2.30, is what M1-16 uses** -- in the tests, to check both PNGs pixel by pixel, and in M1-17 to load goldens; `stb_image_write`, which writes 8-bit PNG only, is not used (decision 196). Recorded as dual MIT / Unlicense. Measured: included as a system header, `stb_image_write`'s implementation raised no warning under this project's set and no clang-tidy finding (736 warnings as an ordinary header). Ruled 2026-09-26; amends the task document, which named `stb_image_write` |
| 196 | The PNG encoder (M1-16) | **lodepng, for both depths**, at commit `ed6fe5825c6a4fbb7f58ab35a4231c7543cd452a` (2026-05-28; no releases or tags), zlib licence, compiled as its own static library as ERFA is: its source raises 1,841 warnings under this project's set and none under defaults, while its header is clean. Measured before the ruling: 8- and 16-bit images encoded by lodepng and decoded by `stb_image` agreed in every sample, and two encodes of one image were byte-identical. Considered: `stb_image_write` for 8 bits beside lodepng for 16 (two encoders), and a 16-bit writer of our own on stb's compressor (format code of our own). Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 197 | The EXR library (M1-16) | **OpenEXR 3.5.1** (Academy Software Foundation, BSD-3), the reference implementation: its C API (`OpenEXRCore`) only, threading off, Imath pinned at v3.2.3 (BSD-3), with its vendored libdeflate (MIT), OpenJPH (BSD-2) and zstd (BSD-3, or GPLv2 -- BSD taken). Measured: it does not build unmodified with this project's clang, because `internal_zip.c` treats any compiler defining `_MSC_VER` as having SSE4.1; with the instruction set of decision 200 it does, 118 files in about 16 s in Debug. Considered: tinyexr v1.0.13 (a single header its author now calls deprecated), tinyexr v3.2.0 (a C rewrite of July 2026 with Apache-2.0-derived parts), and a writer of our own for uncompressed EXR. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 198 | The binary16-to-binary32 conversion (M1-16) | **Hand-written and exact, in `orbsim_view`, `constexpr` with `static_assert`s; tested on all 65,536 inputs** against an independently written formula (`std::ldexp`) and, where the compiler has `_Float16`, against the hardware conversion as a third opinion. NaN payloads keep their bits. Measured: `std::float16_t` is not provided by the Windows standard library; clang's `_Float16` is. Considered: converting on the GPU, which contradicts the task. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 199 | Where M1-16's code lives (M1-16) | **`orbsim_view`**: the conversion, the dump format, the sidecar text, the PNG and EXR encoding -- all headless and tested without a device. **`src/render`**: the probe registry (`Probes.hpp`), the offscreen targets and the readback. **src/app/ProbeMode.cpp** (new): the orchestration and the file writing. **shaders/probe_gradient.frag** (new). The two test runs per tree leave about 60 MB of dumps in the build directories. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 200 | The processor this project assumes | **x86-64 with MMX, SSE, SSE2, SSE3, SSSE3, SSE4.1, SSE4.2, FMA3, AVX and AVX2, set as compiler flags for every file compiled, dependencies included** -- `-mmmx -msse -msse2 -msse3 -mssse3 -msse4.1 -msse4.2 -mfma -mavx -mavx2` for clang and gcc, `/arch:AVX2` for MSVC. The owner's list also named **SSE4A, which is left out**: it is AMD's alone, and this machine's Intel Core i9-12900H does not have it (measured), so a build allowed to emit it could stop with an illegal instruction here. **"MMX-plus" has no compiler flag**: it is AMD's name for MMX additions Intel ships as part of SSE, which the flags cover. **No run-time check**: on an older processor the program simply stops, because a check cannot be guaranteed to run before the first such instruction. Recorded in [ADR 0023](../adr/0023-the-processor-we-assume.md). Asked for by the owner 2026-09-26 |
| 201 | Multiply-add fusion once FMA3 is enabled | **`-ffp-contract=off` for every file compiled, dependencies included**, beside the instruction-set flags. Measured: with `-mfma`, clang fuses `a * b + c` into one instruction by default (0 fused without the flag, 1 with it); this project's own targets already forbid it, but Catch2 -- whose `WithinAbs` and `WithinRel`, used 168 times, are computed in its own compiled code -- SDL, VMA and the new dependencies did not. Considered: leaving third-party code to its defaults. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 202 | F16C joins the assumed instruction set | **Added**, asked for by the owner on 2026-09-26 once it came up: F16C is the hardware conversion between 16- and 32-bit floats, and the owner's rule is that the project assumes what this machine's Intel Core i9-12900H and AMD's Zen 2 share. Measured: `/proc/cpuinfo` under WSL lists `f16c`, and LLVM's processor models define `__F16C__` for both `-march=alderlake` and `-march=znver2` (and `__SSE4A__` for Zen 2 alone, which confirms decision 200's exclusion). `-mf16c` for clang and gcc; MSVC's `/arch:AVX2` already covers it. Found alongside: without F16C, clang on Windows cannot link a `_Float16` conversion at all (`__extendhfsf2` undefined) unless its builtins library is linked; the hardware conversion turns a signalling NaN quiet, while that library keeps its bits. Recorded in [ADR 0023](../adr/0023-the-processor-we-assume.md). Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 203 | BMI2 joins the assumed instruction set | **Added**, the owner's choice on 2026-09-26 when OpenEXR's build stopped with `_bzhi_u64 requires target feature bmi2`: the zstd library OpenEXR bundles defines `STATIC_BMI2` wherever `_MSC_VER` and `__AVX2__` are both defined, which this project's clang now does -- and under MSVC it would emit BMI2 instructions without being asked. Measured: LLVM's models define `__BMI2__` for both `-march=znver2` and `-march=alderlake`, and this machine's `/proc/cpuinfo` lists `bmi2`. `-mbmi2` for clang and gcc; MSVC's `/arch:AVX2` already lets it through. Considered, and recommended by the assistant: defining `STATIC_BMI2=0` for OpenEXR's library alone, which keeps the list as ruled and costs nothing, since zstd compresses only zstd-compressed EXRs and this project writes ZIP. Recorded in [ADR 0023](../adr/0023-the-processor-we-assume.md). Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 204 | Where OpenEXR's C interface meets the lint (M1-16) | **Three answers, each measured before it was put to the owner.** (1) **The channel's pixel pointer is set by rebuilding the channel description in a designated initialiser**, not by assigning it: it lives in an anonymous union, and an assignment is the union access `cppcoreguidelines-pro-type-union-access` reports; an aggregate initialiser chooses the member without accessing one, and names every field, so `-Wmissing-designated-field-initializers` catches a field OpenEXR adds later. Considered: a `NOLINT` at each site. (2) **OpenEXR writes the EXR itself, handed the path as UTF-8**, instead of a write callback building it in memory: the callback's signature is OpenEXR's -- two adjacent pointers, two adjacent 64-bit sizes -- and `bugprone-easily-swappable-parameters` reports it even written as a lambda (measured with clang-tidy 23; *corrected the same day: this said a note in `render/VulkanContext.cpp` claiming otherwise was out of date -- that note is about the boolean-parameter rule, not about this check, and it stands*). OpenEXR widens a UTF-8 name with `MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8)` on Windows (`internal_win32_file_impl.h`), so a Unicode path is safe. The PNGs stay in memory, because lodepng's file writer takes the code page. Considered: keeping the callback with a `NOLINT`. (3) **`NOLINTNEXTLINE(readability-trailing-comma)` at the two initialisers' last lines**, one in `src/view/ImageFiles.cpp` and one in `tests/test_image_files.cpp`, granted by the owner: the check contradicts itself at the designated initialiser of an anonymous union's member -- "should not have" with the comma, "should have" without it, and a braced form adds `-Wbraced-scalar-init`. Considered: the plain assignment with a union-access `NOLINT` instead, and configuring the check project-wide. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 205 | A signalling NaN under MSVC's compile-time evaluator (M1-16) | **The conversion is split: `halfToFloatBits` returns the binary32 bit pattern, `halfToFloat` is its `bit_cast`, the dump is written from the bit pattern, and the `static_assert` that a signalling NaN keeps its payload is made on the bit pattern.** Found by the `windows-msvc` preset: MSVC's constant evaluation turns a signalling NaN quiet whenever it passes through a `float` -- measured, `0x7FA02000` came back `0x7FE02000` at compile time and unchanged at run time, where clang keeps it in both. The conversion was right; the claim was being made on a representation one compiler's evaluator does not preserve. `test_half` gained a case holding the two functions to each other on all 65,536 patterns at run time, on every compiler. Considered: keeping the assertion on `halfToFloat` and skipping it under MSVC, which leaves MSVC checking nothing about NaNs while compiling. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
