| 206 | What M1-16 raised after its report | **Both answered by the owner on 2026-09-26.** **The host-visibility survivor accepted as a declared one**: removing the barrier that makes the readback copies visible to the host goes unnoticed on this machine, whose readback memory is host-coherent, and the validation layers do not check host access; no test found can see it here. Considered: looking for a machine or setting where the barrier matters. **The frame cost measured**, with `scripts/measurements/m1-16.json`: no cost the measurement can see -- -6.0 us over seven rounds, +8.0 us over three, each variant spreading over 10 to 11 us in about 380 ([the record](../measurements/m1-16-frame-cost.md)). Thirty-second runs, not M1-15's eight, because the application ran at about 120 fps that day and eight seconds held too few frames for the instrumentation to print. Ruled 2026-09-26 |
| 207 | What verification should cost, and how to cut it | **Seven proposals adopted**, from the measurement of 2026-09-27 (`docs/measurements/verification-cost.md`) and `docs/plan/verification-cost-proposals.md`: P1b, P2a, P3, P4, P5a, P6 and P7b, recorded as [ADR 0024](../adr/0024-verification-costs-what-changed.md). **Each is a queue task, M1-88 to M1-94, run before M1-17**, one commit each, and the scripts with real logic get a mutation pass. Considered: one infrastructure change set outside the queue, which is faster but departs from how every other change here is recorded and verified. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 208 | The size of the lint pool (M1-88) | **`floor(physical memory / 3 GiB)`, at least 1**: 10 on the 32 GB development machine, the setting measured safe beside CLion. Each clang-tidy needs about 1.04 GB. Considered: RAM / 2 GB, which gives 16 here, close to what ran out; and a fixed 10. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 209 | What decides that a file is re-linted (M1-89) | **The file's own dependency list**, written as a Ninja depfile by the lint step, plus the configurations, the verify step and clang-tidy itself. Considered: the whole target's objects (P1a), which is simpler and public CMake but coarser. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 210 | `tests/AbortBehaviour.cpp` (M1-90) | **Compiled once, as an OBJECT library linked into every suite**, with a CTest test requiring the listener in every suite. Considered: a reduced compile database for lint only (P2b), which leaves 22 redundant compiles. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 211 | Parallel tests (M1-91) | **Half the processor threads, computed at configure time**, and every test needing the GPU under one shared lock. Considered: a fixed 8. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 212 | When an older mutant file runs again (M1-92) | **The strict rule**: whenever any file its judging programs depend on has changed since it last passed, listed by a script from git and Ninja's dependency records; the full pass at every gate. Considered: the loose rule (mutated file or judging tests changed), rejected because a verdict also depends on the code in between, so it could hide a weakened test for up to a phase. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 213 | The fuzz corpus and budgets (M1-93) | **A corpus committed under `tests/corpus/<target>/`, and budgets from the measured curves**: `fuzz_orbit` 240 s, `fuzz_time` 900 s, re-measured when a target changes. **A task that changes code a fuzzer reaches runs it for its budget before its commit**, not only at the gates. Considered: the corpus in the build tree only; and fuzzing at the gates only. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 214 | Which test files are split (M1-94) | **`test_time.cpp` into three** (core time; the leap-second table, M1-04; UT1 and DeltaT, M1-05 and M1-86), **`test_orbit_scales.cpp` into two** (scales and properties; the element-accuracy half). `test_fixture_file.cpp` stays whole: its cost is the headers it includes, so splitting it would add work. Every case builds its own random generator, checked 2026-09-27, so no sweep changes. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 215 | Where these rules are anchored | **In the repository only**: ADR 0024, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/VERIFICATION.md`, `.claude/rules/lint-config.md`, the queue's standing rules and the gate documents, because assistant memory does not travel between machines. Considered: also a note in the assistant's own memory. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 216 | The measurement's own conditions | **Rulings taken during the 2026-09-27 measurement**, recorded so the figures can be read correctly. (1) Every lint step capped at 10 jobs after the first Debug lint ran out of memory; the release lint was re-timed at that cap. (2) The WSL trees and `linux-fuzz` dropped after a second memory stop: "windows based tests are enough to get the picture". (3) The mutation pass from M1-10 onwards run with `CMAKE_BUILD_PARALLEL_LEVEL=10` after a third. (4) No mutation pass under MSVC. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 217 | How M1-89 knows what a file includes | **Each lint step depends on its own file's compiled object**, not on a depfile written by a wrapper script. Ninja already records every header the compiler reports for each object -- 377 for `src/view/Camera.cpp`, `cassert` from MSVC's library among them, measured 2026-09-27 -- and rebuilds the object exactly when one changes, so the precision is the same with no new script. Amends decision 209's mechanism; its intent is unchanged. Considered: the wrapper and depfile as first proposed, independent of Ninja's records but needing to split Windows command strings. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 218 | The run-time shader path in `mutants-due.py` (M1-92) | **Kept, and its mutant declared a survivor.** M1-92's mutation pass found it equivalent here: `ninja -t inputs orbsim.exe` already lists all 7 shader sources, because orbsim depends on orbsim_shaders for its build order, measured 2026-09-27. The explicit path stays as a safeguard for the day that dependency changes. Considered: removing the path and relying on the build order alone, which would stop a shader edit making the application's judges due, silently, if that ever changed. Ruled 2026-09-27. **Amended by decision 393** (M1-109): build-order edges are no longer followed, so the run-time path is the only one, and its mutant, re-anchored, is expected caught |
| 219 | When the mutation reruns of M1-92 to M1-94 run | **Once, before M1-94's commit.** M1-92's closing commit added a test to `CMakeLists.txt`, which under the strict rule makes all 15 mutant files due again, and M1-94 will change `CMakeLists.txt` once more. M1-93 changes nothing a mutant's judges depend on. So one full rerun before M1-94's commit covers all three, and nothing is pushed before it has run clean. M1-93 is committed while the files are due, which the rule as written would not allow; this ruling allows it. Considered: following the rule to the letter, a full rerun before M1-93 and another before M1-94, about 100 minutes instead of 50. Ruled 2026-09-27 |
| 220 | M1-16's last box: the owner's look at `clear.png` | **Signed off, 2026-09-28**, in the owner's words: "I see 5 stripes. the lowest is completely black. the upper four are becoming brighter as you move from left to right, from completely black over fully saturated colors to a whitish tint. The colors are uniform within the stripes. Their colors are from top to bottom: white, red, green, and blue." The gradients are smooth, with no visible steps. That matches decision 189 row by row: grey on top, so the right way up; red, green and blue below it; the undrawn strip black; AgX taking saturated highlights towards white. M1-17 is next |
| 221 | How `mutate.py` restores between mutants (M1-95) | **After restoring a mutated file, it rebuilds the previous mutant's targets**, so the restored file is recompiled and those programs relinked before the next mutant is judged. Ninja notices the restored file, but rebuilds only what it is asked to build, and the harness asked only for the next mutant's targets; M1-90's first pass counted two kills for the wrong reason that way. Considered: rebuilding the whole tree after every mutant, certain but turning a 2-9 minute pass into hours. Ruled 2026-09-28. **Superseded 2026-10-13 by decision 516**: a pass puts the whole tree back from a snapshot after each mutant, so nothing built from one is left for the next |
| 222 | What limits compiles (M1-96) | **One shared Ninja pool, `orbsim_memory`, for this project's own compiles and the lint steps**, of `floor(physical memory / 3 GiB)` jobs, so that together they never exceed about 10 GB here. Third-party libraries built in their own directories keep Ninja's default. A full rebuild ran `check` out of memory on 2026-09-27, about 20 compiles beside up to 10 lint jobs. Replaces decision 208's lint-only pool, which is renamed. Considered: a separate compile pool beside lint's, which allows about 20 heavy processes at once; and pooling third-party compiles too, which slows fresh builds for no memory gain. Ruled 2026-09-28 |
| 223 | How 221 and 222 are carried out | **As queue tasks M1-95 and M1-96**, harness first, so that later passes can be trusted; one full mutation rerun after both, then the push. Considered: two commits without task documents. Ruled 2026-09-28 |
| 224 | What `mutate.py` rebuilds after a restore (M1-97) | **The previous mutant's targets, less those the next mutant builds itself**: its own build rebuilds them from the restored file anyway. Measured on 2026-09-28, 191 of the 225 rebuilds decision 221 added across the 17 files were of that kind. The rule lives in one function, `rebuild_plan`, proven by `mutate.py --self-test` (the CTest test `mutate_self_test`) and by m1-97.json; `m1-95.json` stays the end-to-end regression test. Considered: keeping 221 as it was, simpler but about three times slower. Ruled 2026-09-28. **Superseded 2026-10-13 by decision 516**: the function and m1-97.json are deleted |
| 225 | A build tree set up from text no longer on disk (M1-98) | **Refused in two places.** (1) The configure fails if a file it read from this repository changed before it ended (`cmake/ConfigureInputs.cmake`), so a CLion reload mid-edit fails rather than leaving a stale tree. (2) `check` waits for `configure-current`, which compares the fingerprints the configure recorded with the files on disk, catching an edit during CMake's last step, writing the build plan. On a mismatch, **it stops and names the command** that sets the tree up again. The hazard was reproduced first in a scratch project: Ninja compares dates, and a plan written after the edit is dated after it. Considered: only the check in `check`, which finds the stale tree later; and having `check` also mark the tree so the next build sets itself up again, one more part to test. A gap of milliseconds, between CMake reading a file and fingerprinting it, remains and is written down. Ruled 2026-09-28 |
| 226 | CLion's automatic reload, and how 224 and 225 are carried out | **CLion's automatic reload is switched off**, on each machine: Settings, Build, Execution, Deployment, Build Tools, "Reload project after changes in the build scripts". The setting lives in `.idea/workspace.xml`, which is not in the repository, so it is written down in `docs/PROJECT_STATE.md` section 2. Decisions 224 and 225 are carried out as **queue tasks M1-97 and M1-98**, with **one shared full mutation rerun after both, at full speed**. Considered: CLion build folders of its own, which doubles disk use and build time for what CLion builds; a rerun capped at 4 jobs; and one rerun per task. Ruled 2026-09-28 |
| 227 | A check that can never decide on its own (M1-98) | **Removed.** M1-98's mutation pass found the "record lists no files" check in `scripts/check-configure-current.py` surviving its mutant: an empty record never lists `CMakeLists.txt`, which is refused separately, so the check changed no outcome. The self-test hid it, because its empty-record case held two faults at once -- the trap M1-88 recorded. An empty record is still refused, and the mutant went with the check. Considered: keeping it for its clearer message and declaring the mutant an equivalent survivor. Ruled 2026-09-28 |
| 228 | How M1-17 shrinks a frame to 640x360 | **In the display's linear light**: each 8-bit value k stands for the encoded value k/255, is decoded by `view/Srgb.hpp`'s `srgbDecode`, the four values of a 2x2 block are averaged, and the average is encoded by `srgbEncode` and rounded to the nearest 8-bit value. **Where all four values are 0 to 10** -- the straight segment of the sRGB curve, where decoding and encoding are exact inverses scaled by 12.92 -- **the result is computed in whole numbers as (a + b + c + d + 2) / 4**, so an exact tie rounds up. Measured on `clear` before the ruling: 4,275 of the 691,200 averaged values are exact ties, all at or below 10.5, and floating point decided 750 of them by rounding noise (0.49999999999999994 against 0.5); every other value lay at least 1e-6 of a step from a tie, against arithmetic error near 1e-13. Every one of the 256 values survives a decode and an encode unchanged, at least 0.4999 of a step from a rounding boundary. What remains: a later frame could put a value within about 1e-13 of a half step on the curved segment, where another compiler's `std::pow` could round it the other way -- 1/255 on that value, inside the 4/255 cap, and never within one build. **The display's linear light, not the scene's**: averaging the HDR target and tonemapping it on the CPU would take the GPU's resolve pass out of what the golden checks, which is what M1-14's and M1-15's survivors need it to check. Considered: averaging the stored values (the assistant's first recommendation), rounding ties to even, and treating anything within 1e-9 of a tie as one. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 229 | What M1-17's two tolerances measure | **The R, G and B values of the two 640x360 images as stored, 691,200 of them; alpha is not in a PNG.** The cap passes a largest difference of 4/255 and fails 5/255. The mean fails when twice the sum of the absolute differences is at least the number of values -- a mean of 0.5/255 or more, "under" being strict -- **all in whole numbers**, so no rounding enters a verdict. Considered: floating-point means. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 230 | M1-17's exit codes | **4 only for a measured mismatch**, which always has its diff image. A golden that is missing, unreadable, not an 8-bit RGB PNG or of another size is exit 1, with the reason and, for a size, both sizes named. **When several apply, 1 wins, then 3 (validation errors), then 4**: a validation error makes the frame itself suspect. `main` read a probe's exit code before the validation count until now, and is changed to that order. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 231 | M1-17's diff image | **`<name>.diff.png`, 1920x360**: the downsampled frame, the golden, and the absolute difference of each channel times 32, capped at 255, side by side -- a 1/255 difference shows as 32/255 and the 4/255 cap as 128/255. **A `<name>.diff.png` left by an earlier run is deleted at the start of every run**, so one can never be mistaken for this run's. Considered: x16, x64, and marking values over the cap in a colour of their own, which loses which channel differed. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 232 | What `--accept-golden` does | **It needs `--probe` and `--golden`; it turns the validation layers on, and `--no-validate` beside it is a usage error.** If a golden is already there it is compared first and the two numbers printed, so the owner sees what is being replaced. **The golden is written only once the renderer is torn down and the validation count read**, and only if no validation error was reported and every file was written; it is written to a temporary file and renamed over the golden, so a failed write never leaves half of one. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 233 | Holding `--accept-golden` out of scripts | **The help text says so, and a CTest test refuses any registered test whose command line names both `--accept-golden` and `--golden`** -- the only combination that can write a golden. Considered: the help text alone, and refusing to accept unless run from a terminal, which could block the IDE's run window and so break "one command". Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 234 | `--help` | **Added**: every option, one line each, with `--accept-golden`'s rule beside it, and exit 0. Until now the only help text was the usage message printed on a usage error. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 235 | Reading a golden | **stb_image, as decision 195 planned, compiled in one library that `orbsim_view` and the tests both link** -- its implementation in two would not link. The tests still read lodepng's output with a library that did not write it. **Only an 8-bit RGB PNG is read**: the header's bit depth and colour type are checked from the PNG specification's layout before stb_image decodes, and anything else is refused by name rather than converted. **No fuzz target**: a golden is a file the owner commits, not untrusted input; recorded in `THIRD_PARTY.md`. Considered: lodepng's decoder, which would have one library writing and reading the golden. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 236 | M1-17's permanent mismatch tests | **A test program, linking `orbsim_view` and no Vulkan, writes altered copies of `tests/golden/10de-25ba/clear.png` (directly in `tests/golden/` until M1-110 moved it) into the build tree**: a 16x16 block moved by 8/255, which only the cap catches, and every value moved by 1/255, which only the mean catches. A CTest test renders `clear` against each and requires exit 4, the diff image, both numbers on stderr with the right one over its limit, and the five usual files -- decision 193's permanent test of a failing run's artefacts. Two more: a missing golden and one of the wrong size, each exit 1 with the reason named. Considered: committed broken PNGs, and a Python writer, a third PNG encoder. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 237 | The golden in the sidecar | **The sidecar records the golden's path, the two measured numbers and the verdict** -- matches, mismatch, not compared, or accept requested. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 238 | One golden for every machine | **One.** Measured 2026-09-29 by rendering `clear` on this machine's Intel UHD Graphics as well as its RTX A2000 (the NVIDIA driver hidden from the Vulkan loader with `VK_LOADER_DRIVERS_DISABLE`): after the downsample, a largest difference of 1/255 and a mean of 0.020/255, against 4/255 and 0.5/255; the two build trees on one GPU agree to the bit. Considered: a golden per GPU, which the numbers do not justify. Ruled 2026-09-29. **Amended by decision 287, 2026-10-03**: one golden per graphics card, since lines may land on different pixels on different cards (decision 278) |
| 239 | The other compilers for M1-17 | **Run now**, `linux-gcc`, `linux-sanitize` and `windows-msvc`, rather than at M1-23's gate, as for M1-15 and M1-16. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 240 | The PNG encoder's own choice of colour type (found by M1-17) | **Always RGB at the stated depth**: lodepng's automatic choice of colour type is switched off in `view/ImageFiles.cpp`. Found on 2026-09-29 when a 320x180 crop of `clear` came out of `encodePng8` as a palette PNG: lodepng picks the smallest type the pixels allow unless told not to, so a probe PNG was 8- or 16-bit RGB, as decision 191 says, only while its picture had enough colours, and a golden written that way would be refused by decision 235's reader. Seen failing first: two colours became a 1-bit palette, a grey image a palette, and 16-bit values that fit in 8 bits a 1-bit image; three regression tests in `tests/test_image_files.cpp` now require RGB. Measured: today's `clear.png` and `clear.16.png` are byte-identical before and after. Considered: letting the reader accept other types, which contradicts decision 235. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-29 |
| 241 | M1-17's two looks, and the first golden | **Signed off by the owner, 2026-09-29.** `clear.png` "looks right"; the owner ran `--accept-golden` and wrote `tests/golden/10de-25ba/clear.png` (directly in `tests/golden/` until M1-110 moved it) -- checked afterwards to hold, value for value, what an independent Python implementation of decision 228 makes of the same frame. The diff image of a deliberately broken run (the GPU's sRGB encode with exponent 2.2, M1-14's declared survivor) was read: "I see vertical stripes in the third pic" -- the amplified difference, where the wrong curve departs from the right one. That run was caught by both tolerances: largest 9/255, mean 2.59/255 |
| 242 | What M1-17 raised after its report | **All three answered by the owner on 2026-09-29.** (1) **The local commit before the mutation pass**, as for M1-97 and M1-98: the harness runs only on committed code, and nothing is pushed before the pass has run clean. (2) **Both survivors of `scripts/mutants/m1-17.json` accepted as declared ones**: the write `--accept-golden` makes, which decision 233 forbids a test to run, and decision 230's order of a validation error before a mismatch, which needs a validation error planted in the product (decision 193). (3) **The three older failures the other compilers showed become separate tasks after M1-17**: gcc's unused constants in `tests/OrbitSweepSupport.hpp` and `tests/TimeTestSupport.hpp`; the script checks that assume the application in the core-only Linux trees; and `configure_current` and `memory_pool` in the MSVC tree. Considered: fixing them inside M1-17's commit |
| 243 | When decision 242's three tasks run | **Next, before M1-18, and in a session of their own**: the owner wants M1-18 begun on a clean slate. That session writes the three task documents, puts every question each raises to the owner before any code, as agreed, and carries them out -- gcc's unused constants in `tests/OrbitSweepSupport.hpp` and `tests/TimeTestSupport.hpp`; the script checks that assume the application in the core-only Linux trees; and `configure_current` and `memory_pool` in the MSVC tree. Ruled 2026-09-29 |
| 244 | How decision 243's three tasks are carried out | **As queue tasks [M1-99](tasks/m1-99-gcc-unused-constants.md), [M1-100](tasks/m1-100-core-only-script-checks.md) and [M1-101](tasks/m1-101-backslash-paths.md)**, in phase A before M1-18, in that order: one commit that adds them to the queue, then one commit each. **A mutant file for M1-100 and for M1-101**, which change script logic; **none for M1-99**, which moves where constants live and whose only judge is gcc's build, which the harness does not run. **The other compilers run once, after all three** -- `windows-msvc`, `linux-sanitize`, `linux-gcc` -- since M1-99 must land before `linux-gcc` can build at all. Every failure was reproduced before its answer was proposed. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 245 | gcc's unused constants in two test headers (M1-99) | **A constant only one suite uses moves into that suite; a shared one becomes `inline constexpr`.** Measured first: gcc-14 with `-Wunused-const-variable=2 -Werror` reports a plain `constexpr` header constant a file does not use, and not an `inline` one; clang 23 with `-Weverything` is clean on both. Six of the seven reported constants move (`kNaN`, `kFuzzerHyperbola` and `kFuzzerMu` to `test_orbit_scales.cpp`, the three published Julian dates to `test_time.cpp`), where an unused one is still reported by both compilers; `kInf` in the orbit header and `kNaN` and `kInf` in the time header are shared and become inline, as `tests/OrbitTestSupport.hpp` already writes its constants. Considered: all seven inline, after which nothing would ever report them unused. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 246 | The script checks in the core-only Linux trees (M1-100) | **The four `mutants_due` tests that read the tree are registered only where the application is built**, as `accept_golden` is (M1-17); `mutants_due_self_test` stays everywhere. **`parallel_tests` is told `--gpu-tests expected` or `--gpu-tests none` from `ORBSIM_BUILD_APP`**, with no default: where nothing can run a GPU test, one labelled `gpu` is the fault, and `-j` is checked in every tree, since the Linux trees' `check` runs `ctest -j 10` too. **Found while reproducing**: `mutants_due_unmet_expectation_fails`, marked to fail, passed in those trees because the script crashed. Considered: `mutants-due.py` skipping mutant files whose judges the tree does not build -- a filter that drops findings looks exactly like a clean result; and `parallel_tests` only where the application is built, losing the Linux trees' `-j` check. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 247 | `configure_current` and `memory_pool` in the MSVC tree (M1-101) | **Both scripts read paths with either separator.** CMake's Ninja generator writes the MSVC tree's paths with backslashes and the clang trees' with forward slashes (measured: `build/debug/build.ninja` has 363 edges beginning `build CMakeFiles/` and none with a backslash; `build/windows-msvc/build.ninja` the reverse), and both scripts searched for `CMakeFiles/`. Read with either, the MSVC tree follows both rules -- `check` waits for `configure-current`, and all 349 of the project's compiles are in `orbsim_memory` -- so the checks were wrong and the tree was not. Each fix is one line, placed so that no anchor of `m1-96.json` or `m1-98.json` moves, and each self-test gains the backslash form of a correct and a faulty tree, seen failing first. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 248 | What the other compilers' run after M1-101 found, and the order of what follows | **Three findings, all answered by the owner on 2026-09-30.** (1) `linux-gcc`'s header self-check reported one more unused constant, `kSweepCases`, missed because M1-99 was checked by building five suites rather than the whole tree: `inline constexpr` under decision 245, in a follow-up commit. (2) The MSVC tree's `mutants_due` tests failed because two queries of Ninja's header record at once destroyed it: queue task [M1-102](tasks/m1-102-ninja-deps-race.md), decision 249. (3) The owner asked whether a build-definition change must make every mutant file due -- nearly every task paid about two hours for it: queue task [M1-103](tasks/m1-103-judge-fingerprints.md), decision 250. **Order**: the follow-up, M1-102, M1-103, then **one** full mutation pass, the three other trees, and one push of everything -- one two-hour pass instead of three, at the price of a later push. The MSVC tree is repaired by a full rebuild. Considered: pushing M1-99 to M1-101 first, which the failing trees ruled out |
| 249 | Ninja's header record, read by tests running at once (M1-102) | **The four `mutants_due` tests that read a tree hold one CTest lock, `ninja_deps`**, and `parallel_tests` requires it of every test running `mutants-due.py` on a tree; **`mutants-due.py` refuses an empty header record**; and **`mutants_due_unmet_expectation_fails` passes on its own message** rather than on any failure, which a refusal or a crash also produced. Reproduced first in a scratch project: past its compaction threshold Ninja rewrites the record whenever it is read, and three queries at once went wrong in 7 of 10 trials, 3 of them reporting success with an empty answer and the record destroyed; one at a time, 0 problems in 30. Considered: compacting the record once in a set-up step, which the threshold can outrun; reading Ninja's file format directly, a second reader of a format the project does not own. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 250 | A build-definition change, judged by its effect (M1-103) | **Narrows decision 212's strict rule for the build definition only.** A clean pass records a fingerprint per judge -- a program's full Ninja command list and the contents of every untracked, unbuilt file it is built from, **toolchain files included**; a CTest entry's definition without its line number -- in `scripts/mutation-passes.json`, with the repository's and tree's paths as placeholders. A change to `CMakeLists.txt`, `cmake/` or `CMakePresets.json` then makes a file due only through a changed fingerprint; `scripts/mutate.py` and `data/` still make every file due; anything unknown makes a file due. File hashes are cached in the build tree by size and date. `m1-92.json`'s mutant "the build definition is no longer always an input" is removed with a note, its concern carried by `m1-103.json`. Measured first: 0 of 26 test programs' command lists changed between 849b899 and 41001ba, 26 of 26 under a control edit. Accepted: one more full pass, which also records the first fingerprints; a full rerun per toolchain update, which the strict rule never noticed. Considered: splitting `CMakeLists.txt` into files under `cmake/`, which the strict rule covers just the same; a hand-written map from build files to judges, which fails silently when one entry is missing. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 251 | How the unmet-expectation test decides (M1-102, found by the full pass) | **`mutants-due.py --expect-unmet <file>`**: it runs the check and passes only if the check failed *and* said the file was not due. Found when `m1-92.json`'s mutant that makes the check return 0 survived the full pass of 2026-09-30: decision 249's CTest message rule ignores the exit status. Measured in a scratch CTest project: the message rule alone, "expected to fail" with it, and "expected to fail" with a failure pattern each get one of three cases wrong -- the check that fails and says so, the one that says so and exits 0, a refusal. With the mutant applied by hand the new mode fails, "it returned 0, and said so". Considered: a general CMake helper script that expects a failure and a message, new build machinery for one test; going back to "expected to fail", under which a refusal passes. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 252 | A restore that did not happen (M1-104) | **`scripts/mutate.py`'s restore checks git's exit status and `git status` afterwards, and stops the pass if the files are not back.** Found when the full pass after M1-103 was stopped for want of memory: a killed rebuild inside `m1-12.json` made the harness stop, its unchecked `git checkout` left a mutant in `src/core/Scalar.hpp`, and the loop ran `m1-13.json` and `m1-14.json` on top of it. The three files were restored by hand and no record after the damage kept. Measured in a scratch repository: a file held open makes `git checkout` exit 255, "unable to unlink old". **And, owner's rulings the same day**: the loop running a full pass stops when a source file is left modified, a full pass builds with two jobs, and the pass restarts only after this task -- every file due again, since this changes the harness. Considered: relying on `mutate.py` refusing to record a pass on uncommitted code, which keeps a bad record out but lets a whole pass run on a mutant. Confirmed by the owner 2026-09-30 |
| 253 | M1-103's self-test on Linux | **Its example paths are made right on every system**: the two made-up repository locations are real paths under the temporary folder, the capitalised spelling is used only on Windows, and one case holds the case rule on each system -- the same path on Windows, another one elsewhere. Found by the other compilers' run on 2026-10-01: `mutants_due_self_test` failed in both Linux trees, 298 of 299 each, because the locations were Windows paths. Checked first that the code was right on Linux with Linux paths: two places agreed, a changed flag, file and test differed, and a path in capitals stayed a different path. Considered: running those cases on Windows only, which drops two checks Linux can run. Confirmed by the owner 2026-10-01 |
| 254 | Moving the mutated parts of the build into their own files (M1-105) | **Three blocks of `CMakeLists.txt` move, text unchanged, into `cmake/TestSuites.cmake`, `cmake/MemoryPool.cmake` and `cmake/ScriptTests.cmake`**, each included where it stood and registered with the configure guard; the compile half of the pool becomes a function `CMakeLists.txt` calls last. `m1-98`'s mutants stay, since the guard must sit in `CMakeLists.txt`. The seven moved mutants are re-pointed to their new files, search and replacement text untouched. Proof: each tree's generated build files and CTest's test definitions compared before and after in both Windows trees and the core-only Linux tree, and every judge's fingerprint. The owner's idea of 2026-09-30, after M1-103 left seven files due on any `CMakeLists.txt` edit because they mutate that file. Considered: a hand-written map from build files to judges, which fails silently when one entry is missing; keeping the seven due, about 30 minutes an edit. Confirmed by the owner 2026-10-01 |
| 255 | Which tree a mutant file is judged in (M1-106) | **The tree its pass was recorded in, whatever tree is named**, marked in the output; the named tree where there is no record or the changes are made up; and due, with the reason, where the recorded tree is not on this machine. Found on 2026-10-01 after M1-105: asked about `relwithdebinfo` the script listed 13 files and about `debug` 19, where 7 could be reached -- each tree's view of the other tree's files fell back to the strict rule. Afterwards both trees list the same files. Considered: a line in CLAUDE.md saying to ask both trees and combine the answers, which leaves the trap in place. Confirmed by the owner 2026-10-02 |
| 256 | How M1-18's variants are rendered (M1-18) | **As separate probes, each with everything fixed**: `lambert`, `lambert-half-au`, `lambert-two-au`, `lambert-tilted-60`, `lambert-exposure` (f/8 instead of f/16, two stops brighter: 212/255 instead of 157/255, computed before the ruling) and `tonemap-port` (decision 261). Measured before the ruling: one probe run takes 3 to 6 s here, so about 20 to 40 s more per `check`. Considered: one probe with several patches, which the task's scope excludes; command-line overrides, which break the rule that a probe fixes everything that could vary. Ruled 2026-10-03 |
| 257 | How the patch is drawn (M1-18) | **Through the camera, as real geometry** -- the owner's choice. | 258 | Albedo (M1-18) | **A validated `view::Albedo`**: finite and from 0 to 1, refused by name otherwise, through a factory (ADR 0022). Considered: a plain constant, which the non-negotiables rule out for a value with a physical bound. Ruled 2026-10-03 |
| 259 | How much of a frame the radiance checks read (M1-18) | **Every pixel**, as `test_probe_clear` does, instead of the centre and four other points. Each value must be within the 0.5 % budget **and** on one of the two binary16 values either side of the exact radiance (decision 194's criterion; for 129.966 W/(m^2 sr) those are 129.875 and 130.0). Ruled 2026-10-03 |
| 260 | How wide the port check is (M1-18) | **Every pixel of every probe's 8-bit PNG, `clear` included, to 1/255; and every pixel of the 16-bit PNG to 2 of 65,535 steps.** Measured on `clear` before the ruling, all 921,600 pixels: the 8-bit image within 0.56/255 of the CPU chain on the RTX A2000 and 0.51/255 on the Intel UHD; the 16-bit within 0.0022/255 (0.57 steps) on both. Needs a 16-bit PNG reader in `orbsim_view`, with its own tests. Considered: the task's one centre pixel. Ruled 2026-10-03 |

Considered: a band added to `clear`, which changes its approved golden; accepting the survivor. Ruled 2026-10-03 |

On the Intel UHD the 16-bit PNG moves by at most 7 of 65,535 steps (0.027/255), the 8-bit not at all. Considered: running the probes on the Intel GPU as well, which costs time and ties `check` to this machine. Ruled 2026-10-03 |

| 264 | What a probe's sidecar records (M1-18) | **The scene's parameters too**: for the lambert probes the albedo, the Sun's distance, the irradiance it gives and the angle of incidence, beside the camera, epoch and exposure the sidecar already holds. Ruled 2026-10-03 |
| 265 | VERIFICATION.md Part 4 after M1-18 (M1-18) | **Rule 3's row is not claimed for the image**: an analytic value is not "data this project did not produce", so external truth for the renderer stays open until a real photograph is used. **Rule 4's row records the renderer's first stated budget**, 0.5 % against the analytic radiance. | 266 | When the other compilers run, and two smaller points (M1-18) | **Now, in the task**, as M1-15 to M1-17 did: gcc-14, UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer and MSVC. **No frame-cost measurement**, because the interactive frame does not change; **fixture names and file layout follow the task text**. Ruled 2026-10-03 |

Measured 2026-10-03 on the AMD card, with the recommended `lines` scene: the default rule and `VK_KHR_line_rasterization`'s Bresenham mode give **bit-identical frames**, 889 lit pixels each; the switch was shown to reach the driver by asking for a mode the card lacks (rectangular), which the validation layers refused. If they differ, Bresenham becomes a required device feature, with an ADR. **They differ: measured on the first machine on 2026-10-03, so Bresenham is required.** The bundle's patch, built in a throwaway tree with clang 23.1.2, every run exit 0 under `--validate`; compared with standard-library Python, whose control -- a frame against itself shifted one pixel -- saw differences every time. **The RTX A2000 (`10de-25ba`) reports `strictLines = true` and by default draws rectangles: 1003 lit pixels against the AMD's 889, 337 pixels differing, up to 171/255.** With Bresenham it lights the AMD's 889 pixels exactly, 350 of them differing by 1/255 in value -- decision 238's rounding between cards, nothing moved. **The Intel UHD (`8086-4626`)**, the NVIDIA driver hidden from the loader with `VK_LOADER_DRIVERS_DISABLE=nv-vk64.json`, reports `strictLines = false` like the AMD card and matches its frames to the bit under both rules. | 393 | How a program's inputs are read (M1-109) | **Ninja's graph is followed through explicit and implicit edges, not build-order-only ones**, which say "build that first", not "this reads it" -- `orbsim.exe`'s `|| orbsim_shaders` made every shader an input of every judge built from the application. **A header the compiler reported that a build step generates is followed back through its own inputs**, so a file compiled into a program still counts by the way it really reaches it, with a self-test case for exactly that. Found by the final RelWithDebInfo `check` of 2026-10-06: all seven tests that read the tree failed at `ninja -t deps`, exit 1, throughout the test phase, and passed once the record had been rewritten by a read made with no build running. Ninja 1.12.0's source says why (`deps_log.cc`, `ninja.cc`): `-t deps` and `-t query` load both logs and, outside a dry run, open them for writing, which rewrites a log holding over 1,000 records and more than three times the live ones -- deleting and replacing the file, which Windows refuses while another process holds it open, as the Ninja running `check` does. **Reproduced in a scratch project**: past the threshold a plain read rewrote the record (57,496 bytes to 28,696); with the file held open it failed, "opening deps log: Permission denied", and left a `.ninja_deps.recompact` behind; with `-n` the same read returned all 400 records both times. M1-102's lock covered two readers, not a reader and a build; M1-109's `-t query` calls had multiplied the reads. The `ninja_deps` lock stays. Ruled 2026-10-06 |

Uniform draws found none of the 330,702 -- they lie within 2^-26 of the top of a binade -- so the test also draws from that sliver. **`split` keeps its own scaled branch and states its domain as an asserted precondition**, |a| below (2 - 2^-26) 2^1023, where its high half would round to 2^1024. Today's form overflows `sinh` and `cosh` past |H| of about 710 and returns pi/2 past about 1,420 at e = 2, where the answer is 2.0944, just short of the asymptote (measured 2026-10-11). **`eccentricToMeanAnomaly` reports `NotFinite`** where M is not a double at all. A change no test can see would be a mutant that survives. Considered: the shared helper there too, declared as an accepted survivor. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 478 | `stateFromElements` and a semi-latus rectum that is not positive (M1-118), found while planning finding 3.12 | **Reported as `NotFinite`, as `propagateElements` reports the same condition**, and its header says so; the assertion goes. An element set with p <= 0 is something a scenario file can hold (rule 3), and the assertion aborted a Debug build -- the fuzz build among them, which finding 3.12's arbitrary element sets would have reached at once -- while a release build ended in the same `NotFinite` by way of a NaN. The report-and-assert split M1-87 closed for mu. Considered: a documented precondition the fuzzer respects; queueing it. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 479 | Where the probes' abort setup lives (M1-118, finding 1.7) | **In `tests/AbortProbe.hpp`, shared by the count-wraparound probes and `angleBetween`'s**, with the one suppression it needs moved there from `tests/CountWraparound.hpp` rather than written twice: `<cstdlib>` does not satisfy misc-include-cleaner for `_set_abort_behavior` (measured 2026-10-11), and `<stdlib.h>` needs the suppression decisions 155 and 156 approved. Considered: a second suppression in the new probe. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 480 | The time fuzzer's check of a whole day (M1-118, finding 3.12) | **`requireNormalised` compares the day with `std::trunc` of itself, bit for bit**, instead of a round trip through a 64-bit integer, which is undefined beyond about 9.2e18 days. M1-118's new arithmetic check reached an instant 1.8e77 days away on its first run and UBSan stopped the fuzzer there; the library's own `isNormalised` had passed the same instant. The input that found it joins the committed corpus. Considered: keeping the new check's durations below the integer range. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 481 | The fuzzers' budgets after M1-118 changed both targets | **Kept, 240 s and 900 s, with the day's curves written beside them in VERIFICATION.md rule 13**: from the committed corpus, `fuzz_orbit` found its last new coverage about 2 s into its 241 s run and `fuzz_time` about 10 s into its 901 s, within 1% of each budget. Considered: a 30-minute run from empty for each, as for a new target; for `fuzz_orbit` alone. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 482 | M1-118's mutant that survived | **Replaced, not declared**: raising `twoProduct`'s scaling threshold from 2^996 to 2^1023 is an equivalent mutant -- below 2^1023 no split half rounds to 2^1024 and no partial product overflows, and measured over 3.9 million products near the top of the range both thresholds gave no wrong error term (2026-10-11) -- so no test can catch it. Its place is taken by `twoProduct` never scaling, which the tests must catch. The code keeps 2^996, which matches `split`'s own ceiling. Considered: declaring it a survivor; moving the threshold to 2^1023. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 483 | How the tests a change affects are found (M1-129) | **By fingerprint**: a test runs only when a hash of its program, its command line, its environment and every file it reads differs from its last passing run -- what Bazel does. Measured first on M1-118's 16 commits in a worktree, removed afterwards: **2,284 test runs instead of 8,771 (26%)**, 1,008 of them the 63 GPU tests counted as always running. Considered: the changed files traced through the build graph, as `mutants-due.py` does, which a restored file's new time misleads; which functions each test executes, which cannot see compile-time checks and goes stale. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 484 | Where tests still all run (M1-129) | **A full, unskipped run before every push**, with a self-test that the skipping skips only what it should (VERIFICATION rule 23). Considered: at phase gates only; once a day. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 485 | How the code is structured (M1-130) | **As C++20 modules, level by level from the bottom, with libraries along the same boundaries as the fallback.** Spiked first with this toolchain: a named module builds; mp-units v2.5.0 builds as modules; CMake refuses `import std` for clang with Microsoft's library, but **Microsoft's own `std.ixx` builds with clang 23.1.2 in-tree** and works, its leap-second table included; recompiling a file that uses mp-units 1.4 s by import against 5.0 s by include, linting it 7.4 s against 12.3 s. **libc++ and libstdc++ are not needed**: LLVM's Windows release ships no libc++, and libstdc++ on Windows means MinGW, the migration working agreement 6 rules out. Considered: libraries first and modules later. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 486 | One function per file | **Dropped.** Measured 2026-10-11: an empty file that includes `core/Units.hpp` costs 3.9 s to compile and 5.1 s to lint before any code of its own, so `Orbit.cpp` as about 40 files would cost about 360 s where it costs 13 s, and every broad rebuild would slow down accordingly. Considered: it, for every function. Ruled 2026-10-11 |
| 487 | M1-129's proposal as one task or two | **Two**: tests by fingerprint as [M1-129](tasks/m1-129-tests-by-fingerprint.md), the code as modules as [M1-130](tasks/m1-130-code-as-modules.md), since the first pays off whatever the second decides. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 488 | Reproducible builds (M1-129) | **In every tree**: the compiler's `-mno-incremental-linker-compatible` and the linker's `/Brepro`. Measured 2026-10-11: without them an unchanged file compiles and links to different bytes; with them all 48 programs were identical after a clean rebuild. Considered: only the two `check` trees. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 489 | What a test reads besides its command line (M1-129) | **Each test records the files it opens**, as the probes already do for shaders, **with a conservative rule as the safety net** for a test not yet recording: a change under `data/`, `tests/golden/` or the build files reruns every test that could read it. Considered: each test declaring its files in CMake, which can fall out of date silently; the rule alone. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 490 | The GPU tests (M1-129) | **Fingerprinted too**: their programs, the shaders, the driver version and the card. They were 1,008 of the 2,284 runs the simulation kept. Considered: always running them. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 491 | Where fingerprints are kept (M1-129) | **In the build tree**, never committed: they describe one machine's build. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 492 | The target structure (M1-130) | **Five levels, settled with M1-127's rulings on the `Quantity` base and the `Scalar.hpp` split**: (1) `contract`, `scalar`, `units`; (2) `math` and `time`; (3) `orbit` and `astro` beside `view`; (4) `render`; (5) the application; the test helpers along the same lines. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 493 | What decides the modules' fallback (M1-130) | **The owner, weighing every measurement of the bottom level together**, rather than any single open point deciding by itself. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 494 | include-what-you-use after modules (M1-128) | **Decided after M1-130's decision point**, with a measurement of what it still finds where `import` replaces `#include`; decision 458 stands until then. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 495 | The order of the coming tasks | **Lower levels first, and nothing built twice**: M1-129; M1-127, moved up, since its rulings shape the bottom level; M1-130; M1-119; M1-121, its link-graph check (4.9) written for the new structure; M1-120, M1-112, M1-113; M1-128; then M1-122, M1-123, M1-124 and M1-125, last as before. **M1-121's findings 4.5 and 4.8 and M1-124's item (a) move into M1-129**, which changes the same machinery. M1-126 unchanged, on the second machine; if modules go ahead, its pass includes building `std.ixx` there. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 496 | Where a test is skipped (M1-129) | **In a driver script that `check` calls**, which decides from the fingerprints which tests to hand to `ctest`. A plain `ctest`, and `mutate.py`'s `ctest -R`, still run every test they are given. Considered: a wrapper around each test inside CTest, through which a skip could reach a mutation pass and be read there as a surviving mutant. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 497 | What a skipped test looks like (M1-129) | **One summary line in `check`'s output** -- how many tests ran and how many were skipped as unchanged -- **and the full list of skipped tests in a file in the build tree**. Considered: CTest's own "Skipped" line for each test, which would bury the tests that ran among 500 that did not. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 498 | How a test records what it read (M1-129) | **A record file per test, `test-reads/<test name>.txt` in the build tree, one path per line**, written through the test helpers and read after the run. A test without a record is covered by decision 489's conservative rule. Considered: the record's path handed to the test in an environment variable. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 499 | Knowing a test's record is complete (M1-129) | **A check inside `check` that every file a test under `tests/` opens goes through the recording helpers**, and **a one-time audit with Microsoft's Process Monitor**, which shows every file a program opens, comparing what the tests really open with what they record. **The owner installs Process Monitor by hand**; it is not installed for them. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 500 | Tests that read the whole repository (M1-129) | **`doc-links` and the `mutants_due` tests fingerprinted on every tracked file and the tree's Ninja files**, with what that costs measured first. Considered: always running them. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 501 | The environment in a fingerprint (M1-129) | **`PATH`, every `VK_*` and `ORBSIM_*` variable, and the `ENVIRONMENT` CTest sets for the test**; the libraries a program loads count as part of the program. Considered: the whole environment, which changes between shells for reasons no test sees and would rerun everything. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 502 | How the graphics card is identified (M1-129) | **The clear probe's record file**, which already names the card and driver, at about 4.4 s per `check`. Considered: `vulkaninfo`, a second program to keep in step with the first. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 503 | How the full run before a push is enforced (M1-129) | **A pre-push hook that refuses the push unless a full, unskipped `check` passed in both trees at exactly the commit being pushed, from a clean working tree**, plus a `check-full` target that makes that run. Considered: the hook running `check` itself, which ties up the push for the length of both runs; a written step only, which nothing enforces. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 504 | Reproducible builds in every toolchain (M1-129) | **Each toolchain's own setting for it**, each measured in that toolchain, and **the test that rebuilds a program and requires identical bytes run in both `check` trees**. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 505 | How far decision 489's safety rule reaches (M1-129) | **Every test that does not yet record what it reads, and every test whose record names a file under `data/`, `tests/golden/` or the build files.** Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 506 | How `mutate.py` restores the tree (M1-129, item 6) | **From a snapshot**: at the start of a pass it copies the tree's build outputs and Ninja's two record files, puts back whatever a mutant changed **in that order -- the outputs, then the file's text, then its time** -- so that a pass stopped at any point leaves a tree the next build repairs, and deletes the copy at the end. **Everything the pass's tests write is restored too**: the probes' images and their record files (decision 394 relies on the application being rebuilt, which no longer happens), the read records of decision 498, and CTest's own files. A self-test and a mutant that restores the text and the time but not the outputs. Measured 2026-10-12 on three of M1-118's mutants: item 6 as first written left 13 outputs built from mutated code, even after `check`'s build, and judged each later mutant on top of the one before; the snapshot left every output identical to a clean build, cost nothing in either tree's next `check` against 231 s and 413 s after today's `git checkout`, and made the pass 77 s instead of 199 s. The copy is at most 261 MiB (Debug) and 144 MiB (RelWithDebInfo), one at a time. Considered: a separate tree for passes, as safe but about 1.4 GB kept per configuration and no faster; deleting what the pass wrote, which leaves about 77 s of re-linting per pass. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 507 | `doc-links` failing in a new clone | **Handed to M1-121, beside finding 4.7**, which changes the same script. Measured 2026-10-12: in a fresh clone with a `build/` folder, six backticked paths into build trees -- in `HISTORY.md`, `STATUS.md` and this register -- are reported as missing, so the first `check` on a new machine fails; the main tree passes only because those files exist there. Considered: fixing it in M1-129, which does not otherwise touch the script. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 508 | Tests that read what another test wrote in the same run (M1-129) | **A test runs whenever a test it depends on ran**, by CTest's `DEPENDS` and fixtures. Measured 2026-10-12 with Process Monitor over a full Debug run: five programs -- `test_tonemap_port` (33 files), `test_probe_grid` (11), `test_radiometry` (8), `test_probe_clear` and `test_probe_lines` -- read images the probes write earlier in the same run, which no fingerprint can hash before the probe has run. Considered: running in two stages, the probes first and then the images hashed, which saves a few runs at the cost of a more complicated driver. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 509 | Files outside the repository that decide a result (M1-129) | **Named in the fingerprints, from the list the audit of 2026-10-12 produced**: the Vulkan SDK's version, whose validation layer judges the GPU tests; Windows' time-zone database (`C:\Windows\Globalization\ICU\`), which `test_time_leap` compares the leap-second table against; and the versions of Python, CMake and clang the script tests run. **Anything not on the list is caught by the full run before a push** (decision 503), **and the audit is repeated with Process Monitor at each phase gate** to keep the list current. The validation layer's and the graphics driver's shader caches are caches, not inputs, and stay out. Considered: no repeated audit; leaving them out. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 510 | More tests that read a whole folder (M1-129) | **Decision 500 extended to `check-lint-deps`, which reads all of `src/`, and `check-accept-golden`, which reads all of `scripts/` and `cmake/`**, as the audit of 2026-10-12 measured; the other script tests read fixed files. Considered: listing their files one by one, which goes out of date with the next file added. Ruled 2026-10-12 |
| 511 | The MSVC tree and reproducible builds (M1-129, decision 504) | **Built without debug information**: no `-Zi`, CMake's `/debug /INCREMENTAL` taken out of the link flags, and `/Brepro /DEBUG:NONE /INCREMENTAL:NO` in their place, with `/Brepro` on compiles and libraries. Measured 2026-10-12/13: with `/Brepro` alone 947 of 965 files differed between two builds from clean; with debug information kept inside each object (`-Z7`) every object and library was identical but all 51 programs and DLLs differed, because link.exe gives a PDB it relinks over a new identifier and writes it into the program -- 65 bytes of `test_half.exe`, unchanged by `/experimental:deterministic` or `/INCREMENTAL:NO`. With the ruling: 0 of 967 differ, `reproducible_build` passes, and 556 of 556 tests pass (4 skipped, as in the other trees). Considered: accepting that MSVC programs always look changed, which costs only reruns in a tree run before a milestone; deleting each PDB before its link, reproducible with debug information but extra build machinery. The cost: an MSVC build cannot be stepped through in a debugger. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 512 | Which of M1-129's items comes after step 1 | **Item 6, the snapshot restore**: one script, needed by none of the others, and it removes the rebuild every later step's mutation pass would leave -- 18 minutes for both trees. Considered: item 2, the fingerprints, next as the task document lists them. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 513 | What the snapshot copies (M1-129, decision 506) | **The whole build tree but the dependencies' downloaded sources, `_deps/<name>-src/`**, which are listed and not copied, so a change there stops the pass. First ruled as everything outside `_deps/`; amended the same day when a real pass, m1-95.json's, showed that a CMake mutant's configure rewrites 236 files under `_deps/` -- every dependency's download build and generated scripts -- and none under the sources. Measured 2026-10-13, copy and delete: Debug 2,297 files, 1.38 GiB, 11.1 s and 4.1 s; RelWithDebInfo 5,150 files, 1.16 GiB, 22.8 s and 9.0 s -- about as long as everything outside `_deps/`, 8.4 s and 23.0 s. Considered: the whole tree, 36-39 s to copy, with 36 read-only git files among the sources; keeping the first ruling, which stops every pass with a CMake mutant -- 13 mutants. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 514 | A snapshot a stopped pass leaves behind (M1-129) | **The next pass refuses to start, and `mutate.py --restore --tree <tree>` finishes the put-back** and deletes the snapshot; one never finished, taken before any mutant ran, is only deleted. Considered: refusing and saying what to do by hand. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 515 | Where the snapshot is kept (M1-129) | **`build/mutate-snapshot-<tree>/`**: beside the tree and not inside it, where neither Ninja nor CTest looks, and on the same disk, which is what makes the copy fast. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 516 | The rebuild after each mutant (M1-129) | **Replaced by a check**: after each put-back the tree is built and Ninja must report "no work to do", a real build since its dry run always counts the glob check's re-run of CMake as work. `rebuild_plan`, `rebuild_targets` and their self-test cases are deleted (decisions 221 and 224 superseded), with m1-97.json, whose six mutants were all of that code, and its pass record; `mutants_due_probe_shaders` uses m1-104.json in its place, the same script judged by the same test. Considered: keeping both as a second safeguard; rewriting m1-97.json's mutants against the new code. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 517 | A pass starting on a tree that is not up to date (M1-129) | **The pass builds the whole tree first, then takes the snapshot** -- the build it used to run at the end, moved to the start -- 0.3-0.5 s where the tree is current. Considered: refusing to start. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 518 | How a mutated source is put back (M1-129) | **Through `git checkout`, as before, then its time** -- but only where git gave back the very bytes the pass started from; otherwise the time is left new for the build to see, and the pass stops. Keeps the clean-tree check and m1-104.json's three mutants. Considered: writing the saved bytes back directly. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 519 | How far the snapshot's self-test reaches (M1-129) | **A whole two-mutant pass on a scratch repository, shaped as m1-95.json is**, so that a loop not putting the tree back after each mutant is caught; about 10 s. **Two mutants survive, declared**: the order of the put-back's steps, which matters only to a pass stopped between two of them and could be tested only through a hook in the harness, and the check that the snapshot's copies kept their times, which cannot fail without faking the copy. Considered: leaving the two out of the mutant file; the hook. Ruled 2026-10-13 |
| 520 | How the `mutants_due` tests read the build graph once (M1-129, item 7) | **Stored answers in the build tree, `mutants-due-graph.json`**: Ninja's header record and each walked program's inputs and build commands, reused only while the size and time of `build.ninja`, `CMakeFiles/rules.ninja`, `.ninja_deps` and the `ninja` program, the tree's path and a hash of `mutants-due.py` itself are unchanged -- the last so that a mutant of the script is never judged by answers its unmutated self wrote. Measured 2026-10-14 with a throwaway wrapper: 25.5 s for the first reading, 1.1-1.2 s for the next, the same 14 of 40 files due; the answers 19.8 MiB, loaded in 0.11 s. Considered: one test in place of the seven, which renames the judges of 15 mutants; a setup test writing results the others read, which a run without it could read stale. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 521 | `mutants_due_fingerprints` under the stored answers (M1-129) | **One reading from scratch and one from the stored answers, required to agree**: still two independent computations, and a check that the stored answers are what a fresh reading gives. Considered: both from scratch, as before, 28-43 s. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 522 | A judge that does not exist, during a pass (M1-129, M1-121 finding 4.5) | **Refused before the first mutant**, as an unknown graphics card is: every CTest judge must be in the tree's test list. And every `ctest -R` a pass runs takes `--no-tests=error`, with "no tests" reported as INVALID, never as caught. Considered: `--no-tests=error` alone, which would count a missing judge as a kill. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 523 | Judge names in `--verify` (M1-129, finding 4.5) | **Checked against the tree `check` runs in, where that tree builds the application**; elsewhere `--verify` says the names were not checked. The core-only Linux trees have neither the GPU tests nor the `mutants_due` tests. `--verify` also refuses a mutant file with no mutants. Considered: checking everywhere, failing in the core-only trees. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 524 | What makes a mutant file due after an edit of its own (M1-129, finding 4.8) | **Only its mutants**: the `mutants` list, each mutant's `why` included, compared with the list at the commit its pass recorded; the `note`, `task` and `tree` fields do not count. A file changed in git but with the same mutants stays current. Considered: the whole file, a note's edit included. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 525 | How a program is fingerprinted (M1-129) | **By the bytes of the program file and of every library it loads**, the libraries found from the program's own import table with `llvm-readobj`, which ships with clang, followed through each library in turn, Windows' own included -- so a Windows update reruns everything. Possible because step 1 made the builds reproducible (decision 488). Measured 2026-10-14: every program and library in the tree hashed in 0.13 s (Debug, 53 files, 124 MiB) and 0.07 s (RelWithDebInfo, 52 files, 61 MiB). Considered: what the program was built from, through Ninja's graph as `mutants-due.py` does, which reruns a test after any edit to those files and needs more code. Ruled 2026-10-14. **Extended by decision 706** (2026-10-20): for a program that carries the coverage hook, the bytes stay in the fingerprint with the function part, and the driver skips the test when one of the two is unchanged |
| 526 | Hashing a file for a fingerprint (M1-129) | **In full, on every run**: no file is taken as unchanged because its size and date are. Measured 2026-10-14: all 741 tracked files, 9 MiB, in 0.22 s. Considered: the size-and-date shortcut `mutants-due.py` uses (decision 250). Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 527 | What a read record holds (M1-129, amends decision 498) | **One line per file: its SHA-256 and its path, the hash taken as the test case ends**, so that a later test in the same run rewriting the file cannot hide the version this one read. Considered: the path alone, as decision 498 first had it. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 528 | How a read record is named (M1-129, decision 498) | **The test's name, with each character Windows forbids in a file name -- and `%` itself -- written as `%` and two hexadecimal digits**: readable and reversible. Measured 2026-10-14: 24 of 560 test names hold such a character (`f/16 at 1/125 s ...`), and no two differ only in case. Considered: a hash of the name. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 529 | How a test names a file it reads (M1-129) | **Through a helper that names one file and records it** -- `dataFile("skyfield/tdb-minus-tt.txt")` in place of `dataDirectory()`, and the same for the folder the probes write their images to. The tests change at their call sites only; no assertion changes. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 530 | Knowing every read is recorded (M1-129, decision 499) | **By structure, checked in `check`**: the folder locations are compiled into the recording helper alone, and no other file under `tests/` names them or opens a file itself. `test_file_replace` reads back what it wrote through a helper that accepts only its scratch folder. The stand-alone programs under `tests/` do not record, and stay under the cautious rule (decision 505). Considered: a named exception in the check script for `test_file_replace`. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 531 | Tests run through `cmake` or `python` (M1-129) | **Fingerprinted on every file under `scripts/` and `cmake/`, `CMakeLists.txt` and `CMakePresets.json`**, besides the files on their command lines: scripts load other scripts (`mutate.py` loads `mutants-due.py`, `downsample-reference.py` loads `srgb-reference.py`), so the command line alone is not all they read. Editing any script reruns all 86 of them. **The "build files" of decisions 489 and 505 are exactly `CMakeLists.txt`, `CMakePresets.json` and `cmake/`.** Considered: a list of files for each script, which goes out of date silently. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 532 | Knowing the graphics card before the run (M1-129, decision 502) | **The driver runs the application's clear probe once before CTest, into a scratch folder in the tree**, and reads the card, the driver and the Vulkan versions from its record. It triggers no other test. Considered: the CTest probe test running every time, with decision 508 changed so that its dependents do not run every time with it. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 533 | The files outside the repository in a fingerprint (M1-129, decision 509) | **One "machine" part in every test's fingerprint**: the five files of `C:\Windows\Globalization\ICU\`, the Python, CMake and clang programs, and the validation layer's version from decision 532's record. A change reruns everything. Considered: each attached only to the tests the audit found. Ruled 2026-10-14. **Amended by decision 615** (2026-10-16): the registry value Windows' leap seconds are read from joins the part |
| 534 | Which trees skip tests (M1-129) | **`check` skips in every tree**; the instructions for the gate trees -- the sanitizers, MSVC, Linux -- name `check-full`. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 535 | The full run before a push, in detail (M1-129, decision 503) | **A clean working tree has no untracked file git does not ignore**; **"full" means every test**, while the linter keeps re-checking only files whose text changed (ADR 0024); and **the order of work is: commit, the mutation pass, commit its record and `STATUS.md`, `check-full` in both trees, push.** Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 536 | Which runs are remembered (M1-129) | **Only a test CTest reports as passed**: one that failed, did not run, or skipped itself is never remembered, so it runs the next time. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 537 | The skipping's self-tests (M1-129, item 5) | **A fast self-test of the decision rules on made-up inputs; one end-to-end test on a small scratch project, built as item 6's self-test is**, that changes one file and requires exactly the expected tests to run; and **`check-full` checking that it ran as many tests as CTest lists**. The scratch project's cost is measured before it is kept in `check`. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 538 | Hashing the read records in an unoptimised build (M1-129) | **`tests/TestReads.cpp` compiled with optimisation, `-O2`, in every clang and gcc tree**; the standard library's Debug checks stay on. Measured 2026-10-14 in the Debug tree, the same programs with only the hashing changed: a port check 2.4 s without hashing, 7.9 s hashing unoptimised, 2.8-2.9 s optimised; the clear probe's frame check 23.5 s, 31-34 s, about 26 s -- and those suites run one at a time under the GPU lock. **The MSVC tree keeps the file unoptimised**: `cl` refuses `/O2` beside the Debug run-time checks, `/RTC1` ("D8016: '/RTC1' and '/O2' command-line options are incompatible", measured the same day), and that tree runs only before a milestone -- an exception the measurement forced after the ruling, put to the owner for confirmation the same day. Considered: accepting the cost; Windows' own SHA-256, a second implementation and Windows-only code in the tests. Ruled 2026-10-14 |
| 539 | Libraries Windows loads only on first use (M1-129, decision 525) | **Not followed**: a program's libraries are followed through the libraries each loads at start, not through those it loads when a function in them is first called. Measured 2026-10-14: none of the Debug tree's 53 programs and libraries loads one that way, only Windows' own do; following them took the application from 26 libraries to 249, all programs from 41 libraries (56 MiB) to 253 (199 MiB), and the fingerprints from about 5.7 s to 8.7 s. Ruled 2026-10-16 |
| 583 | What a program carrying the hook is fingerprinted on (M1-129, item 9; extends decision 569) | **Our own code by the hook, everyone else's code by its binary.** Function by function: the modules compiled with the hook from `src/` and `tests/`, linked directly or from a library built from them -- that library's file is never hashed whole, which would rerun every test of the 44 programs that take code from `orbsim_core` after any change to it. By binary, per program: the code compiled in the tree without the hook -- the recorder, and the targets of decision 588; the libraries fetched and built in their own folders -- Catch2, OpenEXR, vk-bootstrap, SDL3's import library; and the libraries from outside the tree -- Visual Studio's msvcrtd.lib, vcruntimed.lib and msvcprtd.lib, the Windows SDK's ucrtd.lib and kernel32.lib, the Vulkan SDK's vulkan-1.lib -- so that an update of any of them reruns the tests of every program it reaches; decision 585 says what is hashed. The linker's own pieces -- the import tables and the manifest -- by the program's link command, from Ninja, with lld-link.exe joining the machine part (decision 533). The libraries a program loads at start (decisions 525 and 539) and the card (decision 532) as before. Measured 2026-10-16 from the debug information of the Debug tree's 54 programs: msvcrtd.lib, vcruntimed.lib, ucrtd.lib, kernel32.lib and the linker's own pieces in all 54, msvcprtd.lib in 51, `orbsim_core` in 44, Catch2 in 43 (3,796 modules), ERFA in 7, OpenEXR in 5 (497 modules); neither Ninja's graph nor any digest saw the libraries from outside the tree. Code without the hook counts per program, since nothing records whether a test entered it. Considered: the hook on everything compiled here, the owner's first framing, which reverses decisions 569 and 588, needs the hook in every dependency's own build and the recorder inside SDL3's DLL, and gains only at a version pin, since every test enters Catch2's runner; only what decision 569 names, leaving the libraries from outside the tree to the full run before a push; the toolchain's library folders in the machine part, which reruns everything at any Visual Studio update. Ruled 2026-10-16. **Extended by decisions 705 and 706** (2026-10-20): the paths of the libraries from the .pdb file of the program; the bytes of the program kept with the function part |
| 584 | How a module is known to carry the hook (M1-129, item 9; decision 583) | **By its content**: an object file carries the hook when it holds the hook's address table, so the classing cannot drift from the build, and a mistake either way stays safe -- a file of ours compiled without the hook is hashed whole, someone else's compiled with it is followed function by function. A module holding data and no function counts as without the hook. Considered: a list the configure step writes, a second record of what the build does. Ruled 2026-10-16 |
| 585 | What a binary is, for code without the hook (M1-129, item 9; decision 583) | **The bytes of the library or object file, and the list of the modules the program took from it**, read from the linker's map (decision 582): a program that starts to pull in another module -- a standard-library feature used for the first time, which can bring start-up code of its own -- reruns its tests. Considered: each module's own bytes, finer but not always possible, since every module of an import library such as kernel32.lib carries the same name, and these libraries change only with a version pin or a toolchain update. Ruled 2026-10-16. **Extended by decision 705** (2026-10-20): the path of each library from the .pdb file of the program. **Extended by decision 713** (2026-10-20): a member without the hook in a library with the hook goes in with its own bytes and its name |

Measured 2026-10-16 in the hooked Debug spike tree, over the project's 85 objects: 1,189 of 54,939 functions (2.2%) carry no flag -- all of them standard-library code generated for this project's types, mostly helpers that only throw, and a few `std::visit` dispatchers; 63 name the project's types, and none is written in the project directly. They change with the toolchain, when everything reruns anyway. The optimised tree's figure is measured in step C: the spike's optimised tree used the other hook, which has no address table. Considered: leaving them out, which is unsafe. Ruled 2026-10-16. **Extended by decision 711** (2026-10-20): the rule examines code sections, not symbols, so that a funclet in a section with an entry in the table of the hook does not count alone |

| 626 | When the coverage recorder writes (M1-131, item 2; decision 559) | **At the hook's first start-up call the recorder registers its writer with `atexit`, so that the writer runs after every static destructor.** Measured 2026-10-17 on a scratch program built with the Debug tree's flags: clang places the hook's start-up call in `.CRT$XCA00002`, which the linker sorts before the standard library's initialisers (`.CRT$XCL`) and every ordinary C++ initialiser (`.CRT$XCU`), and the C library calls the functions `atexit` was given newest first -- so a writer registered there runs after the destructors of every global object constructed later. The program's record named the functions two files' static destructors entered and not the one never entered: 12 of its 13 flags, as its map predicted. Without `ORBSIM_COVERAGE_RECORDS`, no file. A program carrying the hook and not the recorder fails to link, so the recorder cannot be left out unnoticed. Considered: a global object of the recorder's own whose destructor writes, as the prototype did, whose place among other files' destructors is not guaranteed and which needs `-Wglobal-constructors` and `-Wexit-time-destructors` off at its site. Ruled 2026-10-17. **Extended by decision 634** (2026-10-18): the same first call registers the handlers that write the record on an abort, a crash and a bad argument to the C runtime |
| 627 | The coverage recorder's warnings and lint findings (M1-131, item 2; amends decision 565) | **What remains once the recorder is written to avoid the rest**, measured 2026-10-17 on scratch copies of it compiled with the Debug tree's flags and checked with the project's `.clang-tidy`, the copy built as ruled here ending with no warning and no finding and recording correctly. (a) **`-Wunsafe-buffer-usage-in-container` off at the two lines where the hook's begin and end pointers become `std::span`** -- no length exists anywhere, the linker decides it -- as `src/app/main.cpp` and `src/render/VulkanContext.cpp` do for theirs; and the recorder **checks both pointers against its own section markers**, the start marker plus its eight bytes and the stop marker, measured to match exactly, writing "out of line" and no functions where they do not. (b) **A NOLINT for `readability-identifier-naming` on each of the six declarations the hook dictates**, in the same comment as decision 565's `bugprone-reserved-identifier` with its aliases `cert-dcl37-c` and `cert-dcl51-cpp`; and the program's base address from `GetModuleHandleW(nullptr)` rather than the linker's `__ImageBase`, so that no seventh reserved name enters. (c) **The callbacks' parameters `const bool* begin, const bool* end`**, and `const std::uintptr_t*` for the table, which clears `bugprone-easily-swappable-parameters`, `readability-non-const-parameter` and `misc-const-correctness` with no suppression: the recorder only reads through them; a function of C linkage is found by its name, and `const` does not change how a pointer is passed; the compiler is the only caller; and a declaration elsewhere that disagreed would be a compile error, not a silent one. (d) **`Windows.h` marked `// IWYU pragma: keep`, with the reason, and the three headers declaring what is used beside it**, `processenv.h`, `libloaderapi.h` and `minwindef.h`, which clears every `misc-include-cleaner` finding -- those headers do not compile without `Windows.h` first ("No Target Architecture"). The process number from the C runtime, `_getpid`, and the start time in a record's name from the C++ clock, read at the hook's first start-up call, rather than two more Windows calls; the command line and the program's path stay Windows calls, since for a program with `main` the C runtime holds no wide arguments (`__wargv` measured empty) and `_get_wpgmptr` stops a Debug program. **Decision 565 narrowed**: `-Wlanguage-extension-token` never fired, and `cppcoreguidelines-avoid-non-const-global-variables` did not either -- the markers have C linkage, and the recorder's state is held inside a function -- so neither is used; `-Wreserved-identifier` and the `bugprone-reserved-identifier` NOLINT stay. Any other finding the real file raises comes back to the owner. Considered: for (a), `std::ranges::subrange`, measured silent because the warning does not recognise it, which is the same unchecked pair hidden from the instrument (VERIFICATION.md rule 23), and bare pointers, which draw the wider `-Wunsafe-buffer-usage`; for (b), an exception for the names in `.clang-tidy`, and asm labels -- our own C++ names, with the dictated ones only as the names the linker sees -- measured to clear both naming findings and to bring `portability-no-assembler` on all 11 labels and `misc-use-internal-linkage` on both callbacks; for (c), NOLINTs keeping the documented `bool* start, bool* end`; for (d), a NOLINT on each line using a Windows name, which would hide a missing include there too, and an `IgnoreHeaders` entry in `.clang-tidy` for the Windows SDK's headers. Ruled 2026-10-17. **Extended by decision 634** (2026-10-18): one NOLINT more, `modernize-deprecated-headers` on `<stdlib.h>`, and `excpt.h` and `minwinbase.h` among the declaring headers. **Extended by decision 659** (2026-10-18): the record written with Windows' own file calls rather than a C++ stream, which a program's own exit-time cleanup had already torn down |
