| 634 | The coverage record written however a program ends (M1-131; amends decisions 559, 590 and 633, extends 626 and 627) | **The recorder writes its record when a program stops abnormally too**, from three handlers registered at the hook's first start-up call beside `atexit`'s writer (decision 626): on `abort` -- which a failed `ORBSIM_EXPECTS` or `ORBSIM_ENSURES` calls, and `std::terminate` -- a handler for the signal `abort` raises, `SIGABRT`, after which `abort` ends the program as it would have; on a crash, the debug standard library's own checks among them, a handler Windows calls for every exception before the program's own (`AddVectoredExceptionHandler`), acting only on the kinds that end a program and handing each on; and on a bad argument to the C runtime, its invalid-parameter handler, which then ends the program with the same `__fastfail` the runtime's own handler uses. A record is never being written twice at once. **Measured 2026-10-18** in a scratch Catch2 suite carrying the hook, built with each `check` tree's flags and linked with the project's `src/core/Contract.cpp` and `tests/AbortBehaviour.cpp`, one case per way of stopping, against the same suite with the recorder as ruled before: in Debug, a failed precondition, the library's check, a crash, an exception escaping a `noexcept` function, a plain `abort` and a bad argument each left a record naming the functions entered up to the stop -- `contractViolated`, the failing function and its test case among them -- where before none was left; in RelWithDebInfo the same for the four that exist there, assertions being compiled out; and in all 14 cases, the two builds run alike, the exit code -- 3, 0xC000001D, 0xC0000005 or 0xC0000409 -- and the output were the same as before, word for word once the random seed and the stack trace's addresses are set aside, with a passing and a failing case unchanged too. Found on the way: Catch2 replaces Windows' last-chance crash filter during each test case and does not hand a crash on to the filter it replaced, so a filter of that kind saw nothing inside a suite, where the vectored handler runs first; and the library's check ends as an illegal instruction, not through the invalid-parameter handler, which a bad argument to `_itoa_s` reached. **The cost**: no compiler warning; one clang-tidy finding, `modernize-deprecated-headers` on `<stdlib.h>`, which declares `_set_invalid_parameter_handler` -- a NOLINT at that line, the owner's go-ahead given 2026-10-18 (working agreement 7), as decision 155 has for `_set_abort_behavior` in `src/app/main.cpp` and `tests/AbortBehaviour.cpp` carries; and `excpt.h` and `minwinbase.h` included for the constants the crash handler names. **What no tool checks**: `bugprone-signal-handler`, which is switched on, reports nothing in C++17 and later -- measured on a planted call, reported in C and in C++14, not in C++17 or C++23 -- so the handlers rest on this measurement and on the self-test. C++ allows a signal handler very little, and writing a file is not among it; on Windows the handler runs in the thread that stopped, and it worked in every case measured. A crash caused by a broken heap may stop the write itself, and the record is then missing, which is the safe side. How often the crash handler acts in a program that goes on to end normally -- an exception some code catches -- is measured in M1-131's item 4. **What follows**: the four tests that pass by an assertion firing leave a record and are skipped like any other (decisions 559 and 590 amended); the mutants caught only by a program stopping -- 8 of the 418 caught by a test in the final logs of 2026-10-17 -- leave one, so M1-134 treats them as the rest (633 amended). Considered: the record at a normal end only, as decision 559 had it; Windows' last-chance filter, measured blind inside Catch2; the bad-argument handler left out, which needs no NOLINT and leaves those endings to run again. Ruled 2026-10-18, the owner asking for the plan to change if the spike showed it working. **Amended by decisions 637 and 638** (2026-10-18, before the work began): a write stopped part-way leaves a record without its `end` line, which counts as none, rather than no record; and the writers take turns |
| 635 | Shaders judged alike by mutation passes and by tests (M1-131, item 5; amends decision 394, extends 629) | **`scripts/mutants-due.py` judges the shaders a test loads as `scripts/run-tests.py` does: by the compiled files' bytes, each read in full, for exactly the shaders the test's last run loaded** (decision 629) -- not by the shader source files' text through `git diff`, as it did. A pass records each such file's hash among its judge's fingerprint parts, and a mutant file is due when one differs. The owner's ruling: the two should not differ. The part of a judge's fingerprint holding the shader compile commands (decision 394's "shader builds") is dropped, the compiled bytes recording whatever a compiler or a flag changes. **The shader a file's own mutants change is judged by its compiled bytes too**, the owner ruled the same day, against the proposal to keep it on its source text as every other mutated file is. What that accepts: an edit that compiles to the same bytes unmutated -- code the compiler drops until a mutation reaches it -- can still change a mutated shader, and now leaves that file's mutants unrun; an edit to a mutant's own text still brings its file back, since `check`'s `mutant-anchors` fails until the mutant is rewritten, and a file whose mutants changed is due by itself (`own_mutants_reason` in `scripts/mutants-due.py`). It concerns 18 mutants in 5 files; C++ mutated files stay on their source text until M1-134. Measured 2026-10-18: a comment added to `shaders/line.frag` compiles to the same bytes with the project's `glslc --target-env=vulkan1.3 -O` (Vulkan SDK 1.4.357.0), so such an edit now makes no file due where it made 14 of 41 due; the ten compiled shaders hold 12,452 bytes, so hashing them in full costs nothing measurable; and `line.frag.spv` is the same in both trees. Built with decision 629 in M1-131's item 5, estimated at two to four hours more. Considered: the source text, as before; the source text for the shader a file's own mutants change, the compiled bytes for the rest. Ruled 2026-10-18. **Amended by decision 643** (2026-10-18): `--assume-changed` given a shader source treats its compiled file as changed |

Ruled 2026-10-18 |
| 637 | A record cut off part-way (M1-131, items 2 and 5; amends decision 634) | **Each record ends with a line `end`, the recorder's carrying the number of functions it lists; a record without it, or with another number, counts as no record** -- for the shaders, decision 629's rule that a test leaving no record keeps every shader. The application writes its `end` line after `run()` returns, so that every return, the error exits among them, completes its record, and an abort or a crash does not. Found 2026-10-18, before the work began: decision 634 has a crash that stops the write itself leave the record missing, "which is the safe side"; but the spike's recorder writes line by line into the record's own file, so a write stopped part-way -- or one failing on a full disk, which a file stream does without a word -- leaves a shorter file that reads as a record of fewer functions, which is the unsafe side for M1-132: a function missing from a record is one whose change would rerun no test. Considered: writing under a temporary name and renaming at the end, as safe, with one more step inside a crash handler that can fail; decision 634 as ruled. Ruled 2026-10-18 |
| 638 | When two writes of a record meet (M1-131, item 2; extends decision 634) | **The writers take turns**: a writer on another thread waits until the first has finished, then reads the flags and writes, so that the last record written is the fullest; a writer on the same thread -- a crash inside the write itself -- gives up at once, and decision 637's `end` line marks the record that write left as cut off. The wait is bounded (non-negotiable 4), well above the longest write item 4 measures; where the bound runs out, something is already stuck, and the program ends as failed, so that its test fails and nothing is stored. Found 2026-10-18, before the work began: decision 634 says a record is never written twice at once, and not what the second writer does; the crash handler acts on an exception before any code that may catch it, so it can write in a program that then goes on, and an end-of-program writer that gave up while such a write ran on another thread would leave the earlier record -- complete in form, and missing every function entered since. Considered: the second writer giving up. Ruled 2026-10-18 |

| 711 | Eight points of step 1b, the digests (M1-132; decisions 545, 586, 624, 709 and 710) | **(1)** Four rules on the safe side stay: the name of a section is part of its digest; the sections that belong to a piece of data are part of its digest -- 484 `.CRT$XCU` sections with `.bss` in each tree, the test registrations; a relocation into a code section that holds a function and its funclets names the keys of all of them, sorted -- a funclet is the code of a `catch` block or of a clean-up, which clang puts into the section of its function; and a code section that holds no function is digested as data, which does not occur. **(2)** The reader also gives the default symbol of a weak external, and a digest names the function of that default when no given object defines the weak name. Measured: each weak external is an alias -- `??_E...` to `??_G...`, 688 uses in Debug and 639 in RelWithDebInfo, and `?__global_delete@@YAXPEAX_K@Z` to `?__empty_global_delete@@YAXPEAX_K@Z`, 30 uses in Debug. **(3)** The digests also get the keys of the objects without the hook. A name that the map gives to an object in none of the two sets is a problem, so that `check` fails. Measured: the map gives 1,800 names in Debug and 755 in RelWithDebInfo to objects without the hook. The self-test case that required no problem for such a name changes with this ruling. **(4)** The key of an object in the build tree is its path relative to the tree, with `/`: `orbsim_core_d.lib(Orbit.cpp.obj)` for a member of a library, and `CMakeFiles/test_orbit.dir/tests/test_orbit.cpp.obj` for an object linked directly. A funclet in a section with an entry is part of the digest of its function, and it does not count alone. Its code comes from the compiler, and the compiler is in the machine part. **(7)** The fingerprint of each program that carries the hook holds the linker directives (`.drectve`) of its objects with the hook, all of them, `/INCLUDE` included. Measured in the objects with the hook in Debug: `/FAILIFMISMATCH` 644 times, `/INCLUDE` 606 -- nearly all of them one for each test registration -- `/DEFAULTLIB` 368 and `/alternatename` 92. A changed `/alternatename` changes the symbol that a name resolves to, and no digest sees that. What this costs: when a test is added to a program, each test of that program runs again, because the directives of the program change. **(8)** The driver computes only the digests that the stored records use -- of the functions that they name, and of the functions without a flag -- when it uses them, not the digest of each function. The cost in use is measured in step 4 and goes to the owner. Considered: for (2), the weak name only; for (3), the name only, with no problem; for (4), full paths; for (5), the symbols, so that each funclet counts as entered by each test; for (6), decision 586; for (7), the directives without `/INCLUDE`, which the assistant recommended, and no directives; for (8), each digest computed, and decision 709 (2) as it was. Ruled 2026-10-20 |
| 712 | The hook's table counted from two sources, and the debug information's procedures (M1-132 step 2; decisions 560, 704 and 709 (8)) | **(1)** The number of entries in a program's hook table comes from two sources, and they must agree, or `check` fails and names the program: from the linker's map -- from the end of the 8-byte start marker `__start___sancov_pcs` to the stop marker `__stop___sancov_pcs`, divided by 16 bytes for one entry, as LLVM's SanitizerCoverage pass gives the hook the start and the end of its table on Windows (`CreateSecStartEnd` adds 8 bytes to the start symbol) -- and from the length of the program's section `.SCOVP`, less the 16 bytes of the recorder's two markers. Thus a change of layout, such as padding or a different marker, makes `check` fail, and does not give an incorrect count. Found 2026-10-20 in step 2: the coordinator's brief gave the length of `.SCOVP` divided by 16, but the section also holds the recorder's two markers (tests/CoverageRecorder.cpp), so that formula gave one entry too many; it agreed with no record of the 586 in each tree, and it accepted a planted record with one flag too many. Measured: the two counts are equal in each of the 54 programs with the hook in each tree, and equal to the count of flags in each of the 586 records of a full run in each tree. Sources read on 2026-10-20: clang's SanitizerCoverage documentation (pairs of an address and a flag, each the length of a pointer), `CreateSecStartEnd` in LLVM's SanitizerCoverage.cpp, and compiler-rt's sanitizer_coverage_win_sections.cpp, with a 1-byte stop marker where the recorder has an 8-byte one. **(2)** In the one-time measurement of decision 560, a recorded address agrees with the debug information when one procedure record starts there, and only one; an `S_THUNK32` record counts as a procedure record, and a funclet and a label `$ehgcr_` in a procedure's range count as parts of that procedure. The measurement script counts `S_THUNK32` records too. Found 2026-10-20: the debug information of `test_orbit` and `test_earth_orientation` gives one function, a thunk of the vector deleting destructor of `std::basic_ostringstream<char>`, only as `S_THUNK32`. Microsoft's cvinfo.h gives `S_THUNK32` an offset, a segment and a length, as a procedure has, and LLVM's CodeViewDebug.cpp writes `S_THUNK32`, not a procedure record, for a thunk. A label `$ehgcr_` is the symbol that LLVM's MachineBasicBlock.cpp gives a block where the unwinder of Windows can continue after an exception; it is a label in a function, not a function. A funclet is part of the digest of its function (decision 711 (1)). Considered: for (1), the length of the section alone, and the map's markers alone; for (2), the same reading with the script as it was. Ruled 2026-10-20 |

The M1-03 rulings of 2026-09-10 — the storage of an instant, the day boundary,
the arithmetic contract, one error per calendar field, years 1–9999, the
Julian-date interface, the epoch constants' scales, no default constructor —
are recorded in [ADR 0009](../adr/0009-time-is-a-type-with-a-scale.md)'s
update and in [M1-03](tasks/m1-03-timepoint.md)'s amended text, which is where
the code that depends on them is.
